## 1. Purpose & scope

The flagship: semantic admission (FR-20). A new Agent/Model revision earns traffic by passing its EvalSuite against the candidate *through the production path*. In scope: the EvalSuite CRD, the **EvalRunner slot** (`evalrunner/v1`), the gate controller's interaction with design 02's rollout machine, the dataset builder, metric semantics, verdict rules, nightly trend runs. Out of scope: session substrate (17), drift consumption of trends (20), runner pack packaging (18).

### 1.1 The first slice — blue/green, one suite (approved by the human on 2026-09-14)

**The human decided Q1 to Q13 on 2026-09-14, each as recommended ("Decisions of the human", at the end of this section).** The slice below is drawn on those answers. Wherever another answer would have changed a rule, the rule names its question, and each question keeps its alternatives as the record.

**What the slice is.** An Agent names one EvalSuite in `spec.gates`. A revision minted by the Agent's current spec is evaluated before it takes any traffic. An eval controller in the operator's manager sends each of the suite's cases to the candidate revision's own Service as one A2A `SendMessage` task, and compares the answer with the case's expectation (Q1). If enough cases pass, the revision is promoted wholesale: `status.activeRevision` flips, and the serving route's one `backendRef` moves to it, as for any promotion today (design 07 A6.10). If not, the candidate is held, named by no route, while the active revision keeps serving. There are no canaries and no traffic split, so the slice never sets phase `Canary`.

**Why this is the smallest gate that is still real.** It keeps three properties:

- The candidate is the real revision workload: the digest-pinned image it would serve with, its immutable material, and its injected environment (design 02 §3.2).
- Each case is a real A2A 1.0 task, on the binding the candidate's own card declares, not a bespoke echo path.
- The verdict alone decides promotion, and it is bound to the full revision digest it was earned on, not the forty-bit name (A2).

What it gives up is in the "Out of the slice" table: no runner image, no Job, no judge, no dataset artifact, and no path through the gateway for the eval's own requests.

#### The EvalSuite, in the slice

The slice's schema is a strict subset of §3's, so the full schema can add fields later without migrating a stored suite (Q8). Minimal:

```yaml
apiVersion: assayd.dev/v1alpha1
kind: EvalSuite
metadata: {name: smoke, namespace: team-a}
spec:
  dataset:
    cases:
      - {id: arithmetic, message: "What is 2 + 2?", expect: {contains: "4"}}
```

Realistic, with the one Agent field the slice reads:

```yaml
apiVersion: assayd.dev/v1alpha1
kind: EvalSuite
metadata: {name: claims-regression, namespace: claims}
spec:
  dataset:
    cases:
      - id: deductible-lookup
        message: "What is the deductible on policy P-1042?"
        expect: {contains: "500"}
      - id: refuses-travel
        message: "Book me a flight to Lisbon."
        expect: {contains: "cannot help with travel"}
      - id: health
        message: "status"
        expect: {equals: "ok"}
  metrics:
    - {name: task_completion, threshold: "0.66"}
  caseTimeoutSeconds: 10
```

```yaml
# on the Agent claims/pa-reviewer, beside the rest of its spec
gates:
  - evalSuiteRef: claims-regression
```

The rules, each on the CRD. Every count and length bound but `dataset.cases`' lower one is the schema's own `maxItems`, `minLength` or `maxLength`, and `caseTimeoutSeconds`' range its `minimum` and `maximum`, whose refusals are the API server's generic messages, because the API server estimates CEL cost from those bounds: on envtest's API server 1.36.2, with `metrics` unbounded and the threshold at `maxLength: 16`, the threshold's rules exceeded the cost budget by factors of 6.7 and 3.1 and the CRD was refused. Every other rule, and `spec.gates`' cap, is CEL with a refusal that names the fix (A20.21):

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| `dataset.cases` | 1 to 20 items. An empty list is refused at admission, because an eval with no cases would pass every revision. This moves §7's "a suite with zero cases fails closed" from a condition to admission |
| `cases[].id` | a DNS label, unique within the suite |
| `cases[].message` | 1 to 4096 characters, sent as the task's one text part |
| `cases[].expect` | exactly one of `contains` or `equals`, each 1 to **512** characters. Compared with the answer text, case-sensitive, with no normalization. **A case whose message already satisfies its own expectation is refused**, by `(!has(self.expect.contains) || !self.message.contains(self.expect.contains)) && (!has(self.expect.equals) || self.message != self.expect.equals)`: an agent that echoes its input would pass it, and `test/responder` is such an agent. A4, from the critique's m9. The rule catches only the literal echo. An agent that paraphrases its input can still pass a weak expectation. **The bounds are the ones measured to install** (A5, from the re-critique's N6, on envtest v1.36.2). cel-go estimates `contains` as the product of the two strings' sizes. At 4096 × 4096 over 20 cases, the API server refused the CRD itself: "estimated rule cost exceeds budget by factor of 5.4x". A message of 4096 with expectations of 512, 256 or 128 installed, and so did 1024 with 1024. The slice keeps the message at 4096 and caps each expectation at 512. The `has()` guards are required: without them, every well-formed case is refused with `no such key` |
| `metrics` | optional, at most one entry, and its `name` must be `task_completion`. Absent means `threshold: "1"`: every case must pass; such a suite digests differently from one that writes `threshold: "1"`, as the suite digest below states |
| `metrics[].threshold` | a decimal string greater than 0 and at most 1, with at most three decimal places. It is a string because controller-gen refuses float fields without `allowDangerousTypes`, and §3's `threshold: 0.85` is a float (below, "What design 16 says that the slice cannot keep"). A threshold of 0 would pass every revision, so it is refused |
| `caseTimeoutSeconds` | 1 to 10, default 10 (Q1) |
| every other §3 field: `runner`, `target`, `dataset.goldenSetRef`, `dataset.fromSessions`, `gate`, `budget`, and any other metric | absent from the slice's schema. kubectl's default server-side field validation refuses an unknown field. A client that does not ask for that validation has the field pruned in silence. That is the API server's behaviour for every CRD, not a rule this slice adds |

On the Agent, `spec.gates` holds at most one `GateRef` (Q4). `evalSuiteRef` resolves in the Agent's own namespace. The suite carries no `target` in the slice, so the Agent's reference is the only binding between the two (Q8).

The **suite digest** is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the suite's `spec`, with the CRD's own defaults applied, today `caseTimeoutSeconds: 10` alone, object keys sorted, and cases sorted by `id`. A suite with no `metrics` therefore digests differently from one that writes `threshold: "1"`, and `"1"`, `"1.0"` and `"1.000"` digest differently from each other. The canonical form is a compatibility contract: `failed[]` is keyed on the digest, so a change to the form re-opens every recorded failed pair (A20.17). A change to any case's message or expectation, to the threshold, or to the timeout changes it. Reordering cases does not. Because a reorder leaves the pair unchanged, a run's record is kept by `id`, never by position (step 10, A12).

#### Who runs the eval (Q1)

On Q1's recommended answer, a second controller in the agent-operator's manager runs the eval. It has its own work queue, and `MaxConcurrentReconciles: 2`, so at most two cases are in flight across the cluster. It is not a new pod, image or ServiceAccount. It exists because the Agent reconciler has one worker: the operator sets no `MaxConcurrentReconciles` on it, so controller-runtime's default of one applies (the eval controller, built in build step 6, sets 2), and a case run there would make every other Agent wait. That includes another tenant's design 03 `Lock`, whose passes must all fit inside `AuthTransactionDeadline`, 4 minutes (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`). A tenant whose image sleeps on `POST /message:send`, or who re-digests a suite against a held candidate, would then push other tenants' `Lock`s past that deadline, into `PolicyApplyIncomplete/AuthLockUnverified`, which pages (`reviews/16-a3-critique.md` M2). With a separate eval controller, a slow tenant delays only other tenants' evals. It never delays another Agent's reconcile, `Create` or `Lock`. The delay grows with the number k of tenants whose candidates are slow: two workers give a benign case a wait of about k/2 × `caseTimeoutSeconds`, and a run has no deadline, so a 20-case run can take about 20 × k/2 × 10 s. *(Corrected by A5, from the re-critique's n3. A4 stated the bound for one slow tenant only.)*

**On the Agent being evaluated, the eval and the Agent's own `-auth` transaction are serialised.** The eval controller claims no case while a `Create` or a `Lock` is recorded in `status.auth.transaction` on that Agent, at any of its stages, and while one is being abandoned. The exceptions are in the table below. Meanwhile the Agent reads `GatesPassed=False/EvalWaitingForAuth`. *(A5 to A11.)*

- **The stages.** For a `Create`: `PreparingRoute`, `ApplyingPolicies`, `Converging`, `ProbingAfter` and `Publishing`. For a `Lock`: `ProbingBefore`, `ApplyingPolicies`, `Converging` and `ProbingAfter`.
- **Why.** Every eval patch bumps the Agent's resourceVersion. The Agent reconciler writes status with a full `Update` at the resourceVersion it read: at the end of each pass (`writeStatus`), and ahead of each gateway write (`persistStatus`, `internal/controller/authtxn.go`). So a transaction's pass that overlapped an eval patch would fail with `Conflict`, lose the pass, and sometimes lose a probe's observation.
- **The rule is for recorded transactions, not for incidents.** It reads `status.auth.transaction`, and, for the one exempt `Lock` below, the hold on it. A served Agent with no transaction recorded is evaluated and promoted whatever incident stands beside it, as an ungated Agent's revisions are. Two such incidents are:
  - W1 (design 03 A75): a served API-key Agent beside a Gateway-level policy that could widen its route. `reportAboveServed` raises `PolicyApplyIncomplete=True/GatewayAuthPolicy`, and the Agent pages and is `Degraded`.
  - A served Agent beside a foreign traffic policy on its route, reported as `ForeignTrafficPolicy`.

  The rule exists to keep eval patches off a transaction's status writes. It does not freeze promotion during an incident. Q12 counts this among its costs. *(A11, from the eighth critique's m4.)*

What the rule does with each record in `status.auth.transaction`:

| Recorded | The eval | Why |
|---|---|---|
| a `Create`, at any stage | waits | its status writes must not conflict with eval patches |
| a missing-policy `Lock`: a re-creation, whose `targetMode` equals the recorded `mode` | waits, and can wedge ("The missing-policy wedge", below) | the same |
| a J2 or K2 `Lock` before `ProbingAfter` | waits | the policy that closes the route is not yet written, converged and probed: at `ProbingBefore` the `Lock` probes the route and writes no policy |
| a J2 or K2 `Lock` at `ProbingAfter` that meets the exemption's five clauses (below) | claims | it can finish only through a promotion |
| a J2 or K2 `Lock` at `ProbingAfter` that does not meet them | waits | it can finish on its own passes, or a hold stands on it. A held `Lock` stays held after a promotion, so exempting it would earn nothing ("What it buys", below) |
| a J2 or K2 `Lock` being abandoned while it waits on a finalizer (`abandonWaiting`), whose record meets the five clauses | claims | abandonment has no deadline, completes once the finalizer lets go, and each of its passes is safe to re-run. A pass lost to an eval patch delays it by at most one 30 s re-check |
| a refused `Adopt`, `{kind: Adopt, stage: Refused}` (`refuseAdopt`) | claims | a standing record, not a transaction in flight. It stays until the Agent is recreated, or until its owner edits it to `apikey` (K2, design 03 §1.1). After its first pass it writes nothing, because `writeStatus` returns early on an equal status. The route stays as `Adopt` leaves it, unauthenticated, and each promoted revision serves on it, which is design 03's `Adopt` behaviour and unchanged here |

- **A transaction held indefinitely holds the eval indefinitely, for new claims.** There are two cases. A Gateway-level hold keeps a transaction at `ProbingAfter` (design 03 A75). A `Create` or `Lock` past its deadline "stays in its stage and keeps working" (design 03 §3.3.3). Both are already paged, through `PolicyApplyIncomplete`. On Q12 (a), the eval keeps waiting after the deadline. `EvalWaitingForAuth`'s message then names the transaction, its stage, and when the deadline passed. On Q12 (b), a transaction past its deadline releases the eval, whether or not a hold stands. *(Scoped to new claims and to Q12 (a) by A11, from the eighth critique's m1.)*
- **What wakes the eval controller while it waits.** Its `For(&Agent{})` watch does. The transaction ending, or a hold clearing, changes the Agent's status: the transaction record goes, a condition is withdrawn, or `AuthLockUnverified`'s message stops naming the hold. That write enqueues the Agent. A pass that waits returns a requeue: `AuthProbeInterval` on a recorded `Create` or `Lock` that is not exempt, and on clause 5's hold while the pair has no final verdict and is not in `failed[]`; `CardRetryInterval` after a failed run-start fetch; and `CardDriftInterval` on a paused candidate, the I1 hold, no recorded card, a suite that does not exist, a final verdict, a failed pair — beside an exempt `Lock` too — a run-start refusal that read a card, a failed read-back, or, since build step 8, a pin to the recorded candidate that is not the spec's revision — one Q5 (a) admits, or one it refuses on `failed[]` — or one whose spec digest cannot be computed (`pinnedRollbackCandidate`, below). A pass that records `Interrupted` for an orphaned claim returns the 5 s backstop. A pass whose next step is driven by a write returns none: one that finds the Agent gone or deleting, no gate, the EvalSuite CRD not installed, no `spec.runtime`, generation lag, no candidate pending, a superseded record it clears (step 7), or every case recorded; one that loses a write to `Conflict`; and one that sees shutdown begun before a claim. A pass that fails returns the error, under controller-runtime's per-item backoff: a live Agent read, workload read or suite read that fails other than with NotFound; a read of an env source of the spec, made to decide a pin, that fails other than with NotFound or Forbidden; a suite that cannot be digested or whose threshold cannot be read; or a write — the run start, `blocked`, the clear, a claim or a record — that fails other than with `Conflict`. A pass whose candidate's Service cannot be read, or whose address `evalclient.Check` refuses, returns the error, so controller-runtime's per-item backoff governs it and no requeue interval applies; step 3 says what it records (A20.28, A21.11). The pass first writes the fourth reason, `EvalCandidateUnaddressable`, to `blocked`, for every Service read failure and every `Check` refusal, with no separate guard against a transient read failure, except on a run-start pass that started the run, whose one write was the run-start patch and which writes nothing more (A24.4, A24.5, A24.19 (a), built by PR #142). It still returns the error, because the fault is the operator's to repair, the error reaches the log, and the repair's own Agent status write wakes the next pass (A24.20). So the fourth reason can outlive its cause: the pass after the write fails the same way and writes nothing, and from then on each pass waits on the per-item backoff, which controller-runtime v0.24.1's default limiter grows from 5 ms to a cap of 1000 s, and neither controller watches Services. The operator's own re-creation of a Service writes Agent status (`persistServiceRecord`, `internal/controller/service.go`), which brings the next pass at once; so the reason stands for up to about 1000 s, about 17 minutes, only where a pass reads the cache before the re-created Service reaches it or a repair writes no Agent status (A24.4, read from the code, not measured). Sends stay fail-closed meanwhile, because the address is derived before every claim. The reasons for those intervals: `AuthProbeInterval`, 5 s (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`), is the rate design 03 probes at. Beside an exempt `Lock`, a pass whose pair has a final verdict, or is in `failed[]`, makes no clause-5 read: it writes `blocked` empty, with `blockedFor`, only when that differs from what is stored, and returns `CardDriftInterval`, as outside the exempt state (A24.2, built by PR #141). For a paused candidate, or a run-start refusal that read a card, `CardDriftInterval` is 5 minutes, because their inputs are the candidate's card and its availability, and each of those reaches the eval controller as an Agent event anyway, so 5 s would only re-fetch the card sixty times as often. That is the mistake `internal/controller/card.go` records as already made here: "a card is not a per-reconcile input". *(A13, from the tenth critique's m4. A12 gave every waiting pass 5 s, and costed only clause 5's reads.)* **A run-start fetch that failed is the exception: that pass returns `RequeueAfter: CardRetryInterval`, 15 s.** Nothing in Agent status changes when the eval controller's own fetch is refused or times out. The recorded digest is still valid, and the shared predicates already required it, so this branch has no Agent event to wake it and five minutes would be its whole retry. 15 s is design 02's own interval for a failed card fetch, chosen for this case (`cardFetchDue`, `internal/controller/card.go`). *(A15, from the eleventh critique's m4.)* **Every requeue the eval controller returns goes through one field**, whose zero value is the constant each branch names ("What the slice depends on"), so a test can shorten any of them without touching a constant. That is every waiting pass listed above, among them two stated where they arise: a candidate held with a final verdict, which requeues at `CardDriftInterval` to notice a suite edit ("EvalSuites are read uncached"), and a pass whose read-back failed, which returns `CardDriftInterval` and sends nothing (step 11). The two owed rows that shorten the field sit on those last two. *(Scope stated by A17, from the twelfth critique's m2. A15 introduced the field under an enumeration of three branches, and both rows that shorten it are on the other two.)* A pass whose clause-5 read fails records the hold (below) and returns the error instead, so controller-runtime's per-item error backoff governs its retry. A pass whose pair has a final verdict, or is in `failed[]`, makes no clause-5 read, so it returns no such error (A24.2). `gatewayAuthPolicies` never fails, so the error returned for an unreadable Gateway or a failed Gateway-namespace list is built from `gatewayAuth`'s `unreadable` text, which carries the API error's own text except for a Gateway the API server does not serve or that does not exist; when the run-namespace foreign list fails on the same pass, that list's error is returned instead. That text reaches the log and controller-runtime's backoff, never `blocked`, whose message names only the error's class ("What clause 5 costs"). *(A11, from the eighth critique's noted items. Corrected by A12, from the ninth critique's m1: A11 cited a backstop requeue that no waiting pass returned.)*

#### The exemption: a J2 or K2 `Lock` that can finish only through a promotion

A `Lock` credits its `401` in only two cases: `beforeObserved` is set, or `status.cards[]` records a digest for **the revision the route's single `backendRef` names** (`cardAttributes`, `internal/controller/authtxn.go`). A route that does not carry exactly one `backendRef` names no revision and is unattributable, whatever `status.cards[]` records (design 03 A77, §3.3.3). Neither slice's emitter writes a second one. *(Corrected by A19. A7 wrote "a revision that one of the route's `backendRefs` names", which was `cardAttributes`' own reading until design 03 A77 narrowed it to the single ref.)* A J2 or K2 `Lock` re-asserts the route on `status.activeRevision` on every pass (`runLock`), so for these two kinds the route follows promotion. Suppose the serving revision has no recorded card, and nothing was observed before the `Lock`. While a candidate exists, the serving revision is not the desired one, and the Agent reconciler re-reads only the desired revision's card. So no card re-read can end this. The `Lock` reaches `Served` only after a promotion moves the route to a revision whose card is recorded, and on a gated Agent that promotion needs a verdict. So the eval must be allowed to earn it. The rule exempts the state in which all five of these hold. *(A7, narrowed by A8, A9 and A10.)*

| # | Clause | Read from | Fails closed on | Pinned by the row |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `transaction.kind` is `Lock`, and it is a J2 or K2 entry, not a re-creation: `status.auth.mode` is empty (K2), or differs from `transaction.targetMode` (J2). That is `isReCreation`'s test, negated | Agent status | — | the missing-policy wedge |
| 2 | `stage` is `ProbingAfter`, so the policy is written and converged and only attribution is left | Agent status | — | the exemption's edges |
| 3 | `beforeObserved` is false | Agent status | — | the exemption's edges |
| 4 | `status.cards[]` records no non-empty digest for `status.activeRevision` — no entry whose `revision` is that **name** carries a non-empty `digest`. It is read by name (`cardedRevision`), **not** by `status.activeRevisionDigest` (`hasCardFor`). For a J2 or K2 `Lock` that is the revision `cardAttributes` reads, because the `Lock` re-asserts the route on it every pass and `cardAttributes` matches a card entry by name too. Design 03 A77 requires that reading and the re-point's never to be able to disagree about one revision; keyed on the digest they can, because a revision name is 40 bits and two entries can carry one (A2). **Reading a revision by name alone is a departure from A2**, which keeps every revision reference in this design a `{name, digest}` pair, and it is forced rather than chosen: this clause has to negate `cardAttributes` exactly, and `cardAttributes` is name-keyed — a design 03 residue that `internal/revision/collision_test.go` and `upsertCard`'s comment both call the weaker identity. If design 03 ever tightens `cardAttributes` to `{name, digest}`, this clause follows it back, because a name-keyed clause against a digest-keyed attribution is over-inclusive in the unsafe direction. *(Corrected by A19. A10 keyed this clause on `status.activeRevisionDigest`, which is not the key the function it cites uses.)* | Agent status | — | the J2 `Lock` that can finish only through a promotion |
| 5 | no Gateway-level or foreign-traffic hold stands, as design 03's own reads find it: `gatewayAuthPolicies(r.Gateway.Hostname(agent.Name, agent.Namespace)).stands()` (`internal/controller/authabove.go`), and `foreignTrafficPolicies` for `<agent>-serving` (`internal/controller/authforeign.go`) | the Gateway and two policy lists, read by the eval controller only | an unreadable Gateway; a Gateway-namespace list that fails; a foreign list that fails with any error but NotFound | the Gateway-level hold, and the foreign traffic policy |

While all five hold, cases are claimed as if no transaction were recorded, whether or not the `Lock` is past its deadline, so Q12 does not govern this state. That covers claims and the `Interrupted` record, and not the clear of a superseded `Failed` record (step 7): the eval controller makes no such clear while an exempt `Lock` is recorded, because the clear is an eval write no claim needs and the `Lock`'s status writes are spared it, so a superseded record's verdict, `score` and `at` stay until the `Lock` ends or the next run start replaces them. What the Agent then reads comes from `status.eval`, as "`blocked`: one slot, one computation" below says, not from the exemption alone.

- **Clause 1's boundary has gone under-inclusive, and the human decided on 2026-09-17 to leave it: option (a).** The decision and what it does not carry are at the end of this bullet; the three answers below stay as the record of what was weighed. *(Decided 2026-09-17, carried by A19. Until then this bullet read "A19 does not move it, and the question goes to the human".)* A8 kept the missing-policy `Lock` out of the exemption because that `Lock` "never moves its route", so the promotion the exemption earned would leave the route where it was and the exemption would switch itself off (§14, S1). Design 03 A77 removed that reason. **Under-inclusive how**: a missing-policy `Lock` in the residue below — neither the revision the route's single `backendRef` names nor `status.activeRevision` carded — now fits the exemption's own premise exactly, because it cannot finish on its own passes and a promotion to a carded revision is what finishes it. Outside the residue it does not — but **not for the reason an earlier draft gave**. A `Lock` whose `status.activeRevision` is carded cannot be exempted by any option, because **clause 4 already fails on it**, and clause 4 is one of a conjunction of five that widening clause 1 does not suspend. The state that a widened clause 1 could exempt and that still finishes on its own is the other one: the **route's** revision carded while `status.activeRevision` is not. There the re-point is skipped, so the route does not move, so the gate passes, and `cardAttributes` attributes on the route's carded revision — the `Lock` credits and reaches `Served` within a pass or two. That is the whole difference between the two widenings below. *(Corrected by A19's fourth draft, from `reviews/16-a19-critique3.md` MAJOR 1: the third draft argued this boundary against a `Lock` clause 4 already forbids any option from exempting.)* *(A19.)*
  - **The second direction, and why it argues the other way.** A77's inventory says the boundary is wrong "in both directions": a missing-policy `Lock` can now finish through a promotion, **and can be held back by one**. The second is real, and it is an argument against widening rather than for it. The re-point's own write bumps the route's generation, so the route gate ends that pass short of the request, and design 03 §3.3.3 states the unbounded form: an Agent promoting faster than the Gateway reports never probes. A `Lock` held back that way is the last one the eval should be let past, since earning it more promotions is what holds it. Neither option below is argued from it. *(A19's second draft, from `reviews/16-a19-critique.md` MINOR 7, which found the direction unanswered.)*
  - **Three answers, and what each costs. The human took (a) on 2026-09-17.** All three stay here with their costs, because a later amendment that wants the liveness should find what was weighed and not have to re-derive it. They differ by less than they look: the exemption is a conjunction of **five** clauses, and widening clause 1 suspends none of the other four, so clause 4 binds every option below. **What a widening switches on is clause 5**, and clause 5 is not free: it runs only on a pass that has met clauses 1 to 4, and a missing-policy `Lock` fails clause 1 today, so for that Agent the eval controller makes **none** of clause 5's **four** reads. Both widenings turn all four on, and "What clause 5 costs" below itemises them. Three are uncached and one is not; the cached one is clause 5's too, not (c)'s, because `foreignTrafficPolicies` returns early only on an empty run-namespace list and at `ProbingAfter` that list holds `<agent>-auth`. Five rounds of critique each found a defect in this block; it is a table first so that an omission reads as an empty cell. *(A fourth option, "widen on clause 4 alone", stood here in the third draft; `reviews/16-a19-critique3.md` MAJOR 1 shows it is (b) restated, since clause 4 already binds (b), and it is withdrawn.)*

    | Option | What it exempts | Marginal reads over the row above (cumulative) | Contention | What it ends |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | **(a)** leave clause 1 | nothing new | none | none: no eval patch ever lands beside a **re-creation** `Lock`'s writes; the J2 and K2 exemption stands and is budgeted at "It does not bring back N4's contention" | nothing; (a)'s four endings below are the state's own |
    | **(b)** clause 1 becomes "kind is `Lock`" | every missing-policy `Lock` that also meets clauses 2 to 5 | clause 5's **four** reads per clause-5 pass — one Gateway `get`, one paged policy list in the Gateway's namespace and one in the run namespace, all through the uncached `r.reader()`; and, since that list then holds `<agent>-auth`, one **cached** `Get` of `<agent>-serving` (`servingRouteLabels`) | (a)'s none, plus one bounded window: the state where the route's revision is carded and `status.activeRevision` is not, which the `Lock` credits itself out of within a pass or two | in the residue, the candidate's promotion, which cards the promoted revision (Q10) and so fires the re-point |
    | **(c)** (b) restricted to the residue | (b)'s set, minus that one state | **no new read**: (b)'s fourth, the cached `Get`, moved to clause 1, so made on every pass that finds such a `Lock` recorded — including those that fail clauses 2 to 4 and never reach clause 5 | (a)'s none | (b)'s |

    The rate is clause 5's own: about once per `AuthProbeInterval`, 5 s, while a run is live, and once per `CardDriftInterval`, 5 minutes, while a candidate is held on a final verdict ("What wakes the eval controller while it waits"). **They run for as long as a candidate is pending, and that is not bounded by the run.** A `Passed` verdict ends the wedge and the reads with it: the promotion cards the promoted revision and the re-point fires. A **`Failed`** verdict does not — Q2 (a) holds that candidate at zero traffic until its owner edits the spec or the suite, the pass requeues at `CardDriftInterval` to notice a suite edit, and clauses 1 to 4 still hold on it, so clause 5 goes on reading every 5 minutes with no bound. Under A24.2, built by PR #141, a pass whose pair has a final verdict makes no clause-5 read, so this costing of an option not taken no longer holds of a candidate held on a `Failed` verdict; it is kept as the record of the question it answered. Refusing a candidate is what the gate is for, so that is the ordinary path and not a corner. The **rate** is the same order as the cost §1.1 already accepts for an exempt J2 or K2 `Lock`; the **duration** is the axis on which this class differs, because a J2 or K2 `Lock` reaches `Served` and stops, and a missing-policy `Lock` in the residue is defined by not finishing. Under (a) that Agent makes none of these reads, ever, because clause 1 fails. *(The read accounting was wrong in four successive drafts, each time in whichever option the previous correction had not touched: "adds no read" of (c) (`reviews/16-a19-recritique.md` MAJOR 2), "a new object kind on the eval path" of (c) (`reviews/16-a19-critique3.md` MAJOR 2), "No new read" of (b) (`reviews/16-a19-critique4.md` MAJOR 1), and the fourth read billed to (c) alone (`reviews/16-a19-critique5.md` MINOR 2). The table exists because prose kept losing one of the four reads; the test for any later statement of cost here is whether it lists all four and names the passes each is made on.)*
    - **(a) Leave clause 1 as it is.** The exemption is J2's and K2's, and no re-creation is ever exempt — the rule twelve critiques have read, and fail-closed: the eval claims nothing beside a **re-creation** `Lock`'s status writes. It still claims beside an exempt J2 or K2 `Lock`'s, which is the rule itself and is budgeted below. **Cost**: on a gated Agent in the residue no candidate is evaluated until one of four things happens — an administrator deletes `<agent>-serving`; the spec is reverted to the revision the route names; `status.activeRevision`'s own card records and a pass reaches the re-point — which needs that revision to be desired again, since a pending candidate is the desired revision instead and nothing is fetching the active revision's card meanwhile ("The ending that needs nobody"); or a candidate promotes on a verdict that was already `Passed` before the `Lock` began, or that came from the case in flight when it began, where that case is the run's last (step 7). An ungated Agent has every other promotion besides.
    - **(b) Widen clause 1 to every missing-policy `Lock`** — clause 1 becomes "`transaction.kind` is `Lock`", and clauses 2 to 5 bind as they do today. Clause 4 keeps it off any `Lock` whose `status.activeRevision` is carded, so its whole excess over (c) is **one state**: the route's revision carded while `status.activeRevision` is not, which is a `Lock` that `cardAttributes` credits out of within a pass or two. **Cost**: one bounded window of the contention N4 named and A12 bounded — at most a couple of passes of eval patches beside a transaction that is about to write — **and clause 5's four reads**, three uncached and one cached, which nothing makes for this class today, at the rate and for the duration above. No new shape beyond clause 1's own text. *(Corrected by A19's fifth draft, from `reviews/16-a19-critique4.md` MAJOR 1: the fourth draft said "No new read", which is the correction of the two rounds before it, applied to (c) and not carried to (b).)*
    - **(c) Widen clause 1 only to a missing-policy `Lock` in the residue**: the route does not carry exactly one `backendRef`, or the revision its single `backendRef` names is uncarded; **and** `status.cards[]` records none for `status.activeRevision`. That is "When it names the exits"' own disjunction — which words it "has no recorded card digest" — because a route not carrying exactly one `backendRef` names no revision at all and the shorter form has no truth value there. What it buys over (b) is closing (b)'s one window. Why the second half is needed at all: clause 4 is a **proxy** for `cardAttributes` that holds for J2 and K2 because their route always names `status.activeRevision` (clause 4 says so), and widening clause 1 to re-creations is exactly where that equivalence breaks, because A77's re-point is conditional. **Cost**: (b)'s four reads, and **no fifth**: clause 1 needs the route, and clause 5's fourth read is already that route. The residue's second half is in `status`; its first half is the route's single `backendRef`, which `status.auth` does not carry — `beforeRevision` is `ProbingBefore`'s, and a missing-policy `Lock` never runs that stage. **It is not a new object kind on the eval path**: clause 5 already `Get`s `<agent>-serving` for its labels through the manager's cached client whenever the run namespace holds any policy (`servingRouteLabels`, "What clause 5 costs"), which it does once the `Lock` has written `<agent>-auth`. What (c) changes is **when**: (c) moves the same cached `Get` to clause 1, so it is made on every pass that finds such a `Lock` recorded, including the passes that fail clauses 2 to 4 and so never reach clause 5, where (b) reads nothing. The two can share it. **And one more shape** for every reader of the exemption to carry. Two things a reader taking (c) must also settle, stated because they are not free either: "What clause 5 costs" commits to moving `foreignTrafficPolicies` and `gatewayAuthPolicies` "with their bodies unchanged", and "What the slice depends on" calls the same move "a move, not a change". Sharing the `Get` needs `servingRouteLabels` to hand back the route rather than its labels, and so changes **`foreignTrafficPolicies`**, which consumes those labels and is one of the two functions the commitment binds — so that commitment needs a carve-out; and clause 1 would decide from the manager's **cache**, which is where `runLock` reads the same object too — `currentServingRoute` is `r.Get`; `liveServingRoute` is the uncached read, and the `Lock` does not use it — so the two cannot disagree with each other, and (c) closes (b)'s window to the freshness of that cache, which is the staleness design 03 A77's own route-gate comment already states. **An implementer must not give clause 1 an uncached read to "match" `runLock`**: it would not match, and it would add a fifth read no cell bills. *(A19's fifth draft wrote the opposite, that `runLock` reads live; `reviews/16-a19-critique5.md` MINOR 3 refutes it against `httproute.go`, and disagrees with `reviews/16-a19-critique4.md`'s noted item, which is where the claim came from.)* *(Corrected twice. `reviews/16-a19-recritique.md` MAJOR 2 found the second draft's "adds no read" false; the third draft's replacement — "a new object kind on the eval path", "one `HTTPRoute` read per eval pass" — overshot in the other direction and is corrected here from `reviews/16-a19-critique3.md` MAJOR 2, against §1.1's own costing of clause 5. Those two settlements, and the disjunction above, are `reviews/16-a19-critique4.md`'s.)*
  - **Decided 2026-09-17: (a), as recommended. Clause 1 is unchanged, and the exemption stays J2's and K2's.** The reasoning the decision was taken on is below, unedited. **What the decision does not do**, stated because a later reader will ask: it takes no answer among Q1 to Q13 with it — Q13 (a) still accepts the wedge with its exits, and Q12 (a) still holds the eval for any recorded `Create` or `Lock`; it changes no rule of §1.1, so ADR-0024 Amendment 1's record of what was approved on 2026-09-14 stays exactly true; and it leaves the residue as A19 describes it, with the four endings in (a)'s cost line. **If a later amendment wants the liveness**, (b) and (c) are the recorded alternatives, each with its costs above, and the fifth critique's preference between them — (c), because its excess over (b) is a cached read the manager already holds and what it buys is that the exemption never covers a `Lock` `cardAttributes` would in fact have credited — stays on the record (`reviews/16-a19-critique5.md`). Either remains a rule change to an approved section, so either goes by the route Q13 (a) named for design 03's own change. **No ADR amendment is added for this decision, and that is a choice**: ADR-0024 Amendment 1 records what the human approved in §1.1, clause 1 is part of that approved text and is unchanged, so no statement of that ADR becomes false and there is nothing in it to amend. ADR-0034 Amendment 6 is the shape for the other outcome — it exists because design 03's approved rule *changed* — and taking (b) or (c) later would need an ADR amendment of its own for the same reason. A decision to leave approved text alone is recorded by that text standing, plus this log. *(A19.)*
  - **The recommendation the decision was taken on, kept as written.** What a widening buys is the right to evaluate a candidate on an Agent that is already paged and `Degraded`, whose route is enforcing but unproven — liveness for a promotion nobody should be making until the page is cleared — against (a)'s four endings, which all exist and are documented. **And in the residue what a widening enables is not only liveness**: the promotion it lets the eval earn is the exit that ends the wedge **with nobody acting** — Q10's fourth condition cards the promoted revision, `cardedRevision` is then true, and the first pass that reaches the re-point moves the route. Against that: on a gated Agent in the residue the other three endings need an administrator, an owner's revert, or a card fetch that a pending candidate has stopped. **If the human wants that, (b) and (c) are both defensible**: both pay clause 5's four reads for as long as a candidate is pending — with no bound on that where the verdict is `Failed` — and (c) adds no read at all, moving the fourth earlier and closing (b)'s one contention window. *(A19, as put to the human, and answered (a) on 2026-09-17. (c) is `reviews/16-a19-critique.md`'s; all five critics reach (a) independently, the fifth taking (c) between the widenings. An earlier draft said "(c) … and not (b)", which `reviews/16-a19-critique3.md` MAJOR 1 and MAJOR 2 show the corrected costs do not support.)*
- **Clause 5's reads.** `stands()` is true in three cases: a counting policy on a listener that can take the Agent's traffic; a Gateway that admits ListenerSets; and a Gateway, or a Gateway-namespace policy list, that cannot be read. `foreignTrafficPolicies` returns an error for a failed list, and the predicate reads that error as a hold. A NotFound is different: design 03 lists the run namespace with `notServedIsEmpty`, so a NotFound there reads as no foreign policies, not as an error. The Gateway half lists without it, so the same NotFound makes the Gateway unreadable, and it holds. *(Corrected by A11, from the eighth critique's m3. A10 said every failed foreign list held.)*
- **Clause 5 is the eval controller's, and the Agent reconciler reads its result from status.** The eval controller evaluates it last, only on a pass that has met clauses 1 to 4. It is last after every other send predicate too — the generation, a candidate pending, its availability, no I1 hold, a recorded card — and after the suite is read and digested. A pass that clause 5 holds fetches no card and starts no run. Its result goes into `status.eval.blocked`, which the eval controller computes once per pass from every reason (next bullet). The Agent reconciler's gate branch makes no Gateway or policy reads of its own. A gate branch that checked clauses 1 to 4 and showed `EvalRunning` while nothing was claimed would be loud and wrong (rule 8). *(A11, the orchestrator's decision on the eighth critique's m2.)*
- **`blocked`: one slot, one computation.** `status.eval.blocked` holds one `{reason, message}`, and four reasons share it. The eval controller writes it, with `blockedFor`, at most once per pass, and only on a pass on which every shared send predicate holds. When a run starts, step 1's run-start patch is that write; a pass whose run start stops at an unusable candidate address (step 3) writes the fourth reason when the address cannot be derived, which overwrites a stored `EvalWaitingForAuth` as the clear it replaced did; so does a pass of a run in progress that would claim a case and cannot derive the address, except on a run-start pass that started the run, whose one write was the run-start patch and which writes nothing more. Every address error writes it (A24.4, A24.5, A24.19 (a), built by PR #142). A mid-run pass that records `Interrupted` for an orphaned claim, or finds every case recorded, writes `blocked` empty and derives no address; one with a case to claim writes it after finding that case and deriving the address, the fourth reason or empty (A24.4). On a pass on which every shared send predicate holds it computes `blocked` from every reason, in this precedence, and writes it only when the computed value, or the pair or `Lock` it is computed for, differs from the stored one: *(A12, the orchestrator's decision on the ninth critique's MAJOR 1, and its m2.)*
  1. the fourth reason, `EvalCandidateUnaddressable`, when this pass would start a run or claim a case and cannot derive the candidate's address (A24.5), except on a run-start pass that started the run, whose one write was the run-start patch and which writes nothing more (A24.4). A pass that clause 5 holds would do neither, so it reads no Service, and the fourth reason is computed only once the hold has cleared. It outranks the run-start refusal because the run-start fetch goes to that address (A24.17, built by PR #142);
  2. a run-start refusal, `EvalBindingUnsupported` or `EvalAwaitingCard` (step 2), when this pass would start a run and the card refuses it. A pass that clause 5 holds is not one that would start a run, so it fetches no card, and a refusal is computed only once the hold has cleared;
  3. otherwise `EvalWaitingForAuth`, when clauses 1 to 4 hold and, on a pass whose pair has no final verdict and is not in `failed[]`, clause 5 finds a hold (A24.2);
  4. otherwise empty.

  So a pass that finds the exempt state gone computes no `EvalWaitingForAuth`, and a standing one is rewritten by the first pass after it that reaches the computation, not by the pass that finds the exempt state gone: cleared if that pass finds no hold, and written again if the exempt state has come back and clause 5 still holds; a final-verdict or failed-pair pass beside an exempt `Lock` writes it empty without reading clause 5 (A24.2). Only a pass that reaches the computation clears the hold: one on which every shared send predicate holds and the suite is read, so one with no `Create` or `Lock` recorded, or with an exempt one. A pass that waits on a recorded `Create` or `Lock` that is not exempt reads and writes nothing, `blocked` included, so it clears nothing, because an eval write beside such a transaction is the contention the transaction predicate exists to prevent ("Why", above). A standing hold is masked meanwhile rather than cleared: the gate branch answers that transaction first ("The gate branch's order"), and once the `Lock` ends `blockedFor`'s deadline no longer matches, so the hold is not applied (A21.7). The exempt state goes, among other ways, when the `Lock` advances past `ProbingAfter`, when a fresh NACK or a policy found no longer intact returns it to `Converging` or `ApplyingPolicies` (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`), when a card is recorded for `status.activeRevisionDigest`, when a spec edit abandons the `Lock`, or when the gate is removed. The `Lock` advancing to `Served`, and a spec edit whose abandonment completes, remove the record in the Agent pass that does it — a J2 `Lock` gives way to the recorded mode, and a K2 `Lock` to a refused `Adopt`, which does not count — and the next eval pass that reaches the computation rewrites the hold. A stage regression, and a card recorded for the active revision, leave the `Lock` recorded and not exempt, and the eval controller writes nothing beside it while the hold is masked; once the gate is removed it returns before any write. An abandonment that waits on a finalizer ends nothing: the `Lock` stays recorded at `ProbingAfter`, still meets clauses 1 to 4 on its record, and is claimed beside, as the `abandonWaiting` row above says, so "when a spec edit abandons the `Lock`" holds only of an abandonment that completes. *(The two stage regressions added by A15, from the eleventh critique's m1: neither is "advancing past `ProbingAfter`", and A13 presented its four as the exits.)* `recreateRoute` is not among them: its two `runLock` call sites are both inside the re-creation branch that clause 1 negates — the route found gone or unpublished at the top of that branch, and, since design 03 A77, a route found gone under the re-point's own write — and its others are in `reconcileServed`, which is not reached while a `Lock` is recorded (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`). *(Count corrected by A19; A13's rule is unchanged, because both sites are still behind clause 1.)* *(Corrected by A13, from the tenth critique's m1. A12 listed `recreateRoute`, which cannot displace an exempt `Lock`.)* A refusal that stands computes the same value on every pass, so nothing is written and nothing loops. The message is built from the hold's stable causes, the policies' names and the class of a read error, and not from an API error's raw text, so a varying error string does not change the value.

  A `Lock` that leaves `ProbingAfter` on a fresh NACK and comes back keeps its deadline, so a `blocked` and `blockedFor` written before the excursion match again, and the gate branch shows them until the next eval pass: a hold whose cause may have gone, or an empty slot that reads `EvalRunning` while clause 5 now holds, or a fourth `blocked` reason (A24.4) whose address can now be derived, which the next eval pass derives again. That window is one eval pass, because the status write that brings the `Lock` back to `ProbingAfter` enqueues the Agent on the eval controller's `For(&Agent{})` watch, and that pass re-reads clause 5. Sends stay fail-closed throughout, because clause 5 is re-read before every claim. It is "Outside the exempt state"'s one-pass default, met on the way back in, and closing it would need the waiting pass to write, which the rule above does not do (A21.8, read from the code and not measured).
  - **`blockedFor` says what was evaluated.** Beside it, `status.eval.blockedFor` records `{revisionDigest, suiteDigest, lockDeadline}`: the pair the pass computed `blocked` for, and the `deadline` of the `Lock` recorded then. Design 03 sets that deadline once, on entering the transaction's first stage (`status.auth.transaction.deadline`); it is empty when no `Lock` was recorded. `blockedFor` is written in the same patch as `blocked`, on the passes that write `blocked` (above): a pass on which every shared send predicate holds, when the pair or `Lock` differs from the stored one even if `blocked` does not. Beside an exempt `Lock`, a pass whose pair has a final verdict, or is in `failed[]`, writes it too, without reading clause 5, so there a matching `blockedFor` does not prove that clause 5 was evaluated; that is harmless, because rows (3) and (4) of the gate branch's order answer every such pair first (A24.2). A pass whose run start stops at an unusable candidate address writes the fourth reason, with `blockedFor` for the pair and the `Lock` (A24.4, built by PR #142), so A21.6's two residues — a `blockedFor` naming another pair or `Lock`, and a stored run-start refusal still reported — are gone. **Its fail-closed reading:** `blocked` applies only while `blockedFor` matches the current candidate's digest, the current suite's digest and the current `Lock`'s deadline. A `blockedFor` that is absent, or does not match, means that nothing has been evaluated for this state. The deadline is truncated to the second (`enterLock`, `lockMissingPolicy`, and `recordedLockDeadline`, with which the eval controller writes `blockedFor` and the gate branch matches it), so two transactions entered inside one second share it. They would also need the same candidate and suite digests, and the spec edit that re-enters a `Lock` changes the candidate, so the residue is at most one pass of a wrong condition, with sends still fail-closed (A13).
  - **What the gate branch derives in the exempt state.** After the head of the order both states share, the gate branch answers a pair that has failed, `EvalFailed`, then `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`; then no card recorded for the candidate, `EvalAwaitingCard`; and then a candidate that is not available, `EvalPaused` (A24.1, built by PR #141). The eval controller evaluates clause 5 only on a pass on which the candidate has a card and is available, so on no other pass was `blockedFor` computed for the current state. "The gate branch's order", below, states the whole order. Then, if `blockedFor` does not match, it sets `GatesPassed=False/EvalWaitingForAuth`, whose message says the eval controller has not yet evaluated clause 5 for this `Lock`. If `blocked` is set, it sets `blocked`'s reason, with `blocked.message`. Otherwise it takes the table's rows, from the run: `EvalRunning` while a suite `id` has no entry in `cases[]` **with a recorded outcome**, which is step 7's verdict predicate read the other way round, and the promotion branch once a `Passed` verdict is written; a `Failed` verdict, and the pair in `failed[]`, are answered at (4) (A24.1). *(A15, from the eleventh critique's m3. A13 tightened step 7 and left this one counting a `Sending` entry, so between the last case's claim and its record the chain offered no row at all, and the Agent read the right condition only because `GatesPassed` is sticky. Step 10's pick of the next case is left as it is, where a `Sending` entry must count.)* It never shows `EvalRunning` on a `blocked` that was not evaluated for the current state. Before any run has started, the gate branch computes the pair itself, from the candidate's digest and the suite it read, because `status.eval` records none yet (A13).
  - **Outside the exempt state**, the gate branch answers a pair that has failed, no card recorded, a recorded `Create` or `Lock` that is not exempt (`EvalWaitingForAuth`), and a candidate that is not available (`EvalPaused`) before `blocked` — the last as step 8 orders the not-ready branches before the gate — then applies `blocked` only while `blockedFor` matches on all three parts, as above, and otherwise takes the table's rows. "The gate branch's order", below, states the whole order. That default is open for one eval pass: a refusal written while an exempt `Lock` stood stops applying the moment that `Lock` ends, so until the next eval pass rewrites `blockedFor`, the gate branch can read `EvalRunning` while the eval controller is still refusing the card. The `Lock` ending is itself an Agent status write, and that write enqueues the Agent on the eval controller's own `For(&Agent{})` watch, so the window is one eval pass. The requeue backstop on this branch is `CardDriftInterval`, not `AuthProbeInterval`, which the split above moved it off. Sends stay fail-closed throughout either way, because clause 5 and the run-start fetch are re-read before every claim. *(A13, from the tenth critique's m3. A12 matched on the pair alone here, and stated no bound. Bound corrected by A15, from the eleventh critique's m2: A13 named a constant this branch no longer uses.)*
  - *(A11 said the gate branch showed `EvalRunning` whenever `blocked` was not the hold, which contradicted the `EvalBindingUnsupported` row. It also said clause 5 cleared `blocked` when no hold stood, so a card refusal and clause 5 could rewrite the slot in turn on every pass.)*
- **What clause 5 costs.** Only a pass of the eval controller that has met clauses 1 to 4 pays. A waiting pass comes round at least once per `AuthProbeInterval`, 5 s, and more often on an Agent event, so these reads run at about that rate for each exempt `Lock`, and a failing read at controller-runtime's backoff rate (A12, worded by A13). A pass whose pair has a final verdict, or is in `failed[]`, makes none of them (A24.2). Every waiting pass but a paused, I1-held or uncarded one also reads the suite uncached, at the same rate. A pass held by a run-start refusal comes round once per `CardDriftInterval` instead, or once per `CardRetryInterval` after a failed fetch, and re-fetches the card and reads the suite at that rate rather than once per 5 s (A13, from the tenth critique's m4; the retry rate by A15). A paused-candidate pass fetches no card at all: the path is chosen at run start, and before a run starts the availability predicate has already failed. It reads no suite, and neither does a pass held by the I1 hold or by no recorded card (A20.27). **The Agent reconciler reads the suite too**, on every pass of a gated Agent whose desired revision is not the active one and whose Agent CRD schema check (step 11) passes, so beside a `Lock` probing at 5 s that is a second uncached `get` per Agent per 5 s. *(A15, from the eleventh critique's m7, and its noted item on the paused branch: A13 costed the eval controller's reads alone and attributed a card fetch to a branch that makes none.)* Each clause-5 pass makes:
  - one `get` of the Gateway, and a paged `list` of `AgentgatewayPolicy` objects in the Gateway's namespace, 250 per page;
  - a paged `list` of `AgentgatewayPolicy` objects in the run namespace, 250 per page;
  - only when that list is not empty, one read of `<agent>-serving`, for its labels, through the manager's cached client (`servingRouteLabels`).

  The Gateway `get` and both lists go through `r.reader()`, the uncached reader, so each reaches the API server. The operator already holds the rights: `get` on the Gateway through the chart's gateway-labels Role, and `list` on `agentgatewaypolicies`. The two functions were `AgentReconciler` methods when the slice was approved, and moving them, with their bodies unchanged, onto a receiver both controllers can use was owed with it. Build step 4 (PR #106) moved them, and the `listPolicies` and `servingRouteLabels` they call, onto `gatewayPolicyReads`, a type declared in `internal/controller/policyreads.go` that carries the client, the uncached reader and the `GatewayConfig`. Build step 7 (PR #118) then added two fields to `gatewayAuth`, `gatewayErr` and `listErr`, and two assignments to `gatewayAuthPolicies`' body that fill them with the errors behind `unreadable`, so that clause 5's hold can name their class; design 03's readings of `gatewayAuth` read neither, and its `describe` is unchanged. So the four bodies are as they were but for those two assignments, and `gatewayAuthPolicies` now begins at `authabove.go:204`, not at `:199`, where design 03 cites it; `foreignTrafficPolicies`, `listPolicies` and `servingRouteLabels` stay at `authforeign.go:37`, `:82` and `:107`. That citation is design 03's text, and goes through design 03's process (A21.14).
- **Why clause 5 does not read a condition reason.** At the transaction's deadline, `runLock`'s J2 and K2 tail runs its deadline branch before its hold branch, so `PolicyApplyIncomplete`'s reason becomes `AuthLockUnverified`, and the hold survives only in the message, as design 03 A75 specifies. `raiseIncomplete` likewise keeps `AuthLockUnverified` ahead of `ForeignTrafficPolicy`. No field of `status.auth.transaction` records the hold.
- **Clause 5 stops new claims only.** Clauses 1 to 4 are covered by the claim's optimistic lock (step 10). Clause 5's reads are outside it, but they are made afresh before every claim. So a hold that appears while a case is in flight does not stop that case, and if it is the last case, its record carries the verdict. Nor does a hold stop a promotion whose `Passed` verdict is already written, because the promoting pass checks only the four promotion conditions. *(A11, from the eighth critique's m1.)*
- **The hold delays the eval and never wedges it.** Once the hold clears, a `Lock` that still cannot attribute its `401` meets all five clauses.
- **It does not bring back N4's contention.** Before `ProbingAfter` the policy that closes the route is not yet written, converged and probed, and clause 2 keeps the eval off it. At `ProbingAfter` in the exempt state, no pass can complete the `Lock` except through a promotion. So an eval costs the `Lock` at most two probe passes per case, one per run start, and one per change of `blocked` or `blockedFor`. A case is two patches, its claim and its record. A run adds one for its start. The verdict adds none, because it rides in the last case's record (step 7). `blocked` and `blockedFor` change only when the computed value does, once per change of the state they describe, never once per pass. Each pass lost to these loses nothing that could have finished the `Lock`. *(Bound restored by A12, from the ninth critique's MAJOR 1: A11's consolidation dropped A10's "at most two probe passes per case, and one per run start".)*
- **What it buys.** A passed candidate is promoted only under the fourth promotion condition, which requires its card to be recorded. The J2 or K2 `Lock` then re-asserts the route on it, clears `beforeObserved` and `beforeRevision`, and credits a `401` on that card, as design 03 §3.3.3's rule for a promotion during a `Lock` specifies. **Not in the promoting pass**: since design 03 A77, no `Lock` takes a `ProbingAfter` answer until the assayd Gateway reports `Accepted` and `ResolvedRefs` on the route at its current generation, and the write that moves the route bumps that generation. So the promoting pass ends short of the request, and the credit comes on a later pass. That costs the `Lock` passes, never the verdict, and design 03 takes it as the price of not crediting a `401` the previous revision's backend produced (design 03 A77, `research/auth-lock-false-credit-2026-09.md`). *(Added by A19. A7 wrote "its next `401`", which before A77 was the promoting pass's own.)* A Gateway-level hold still holds that `Lock` afterwards, as it would any `Lock`. A pin, or removing the gate, also moves the route for these two kinds, and so also ends the wait.

#### The missing-policy wedge

The `Lock` of a missing policy is a re-creation: its target mode equals the recorded mode. **Since design 03 A77 it writes the route, conditionally.** At `ProbingAfter`, after the policy is read intact and after A75's Gateway read, it re-asserts `<agent>-serving` published on `status.activeRevision` — but **only while `status.cards[]` records a digest for that revision** (`cardedRevision`, `runLock`, `internal/controller/authtxn.go`). When that condition fails the route is taken as found, as it always was, and `reconcileServed`, which re-asserts a served Agent's route on the active revision, is still not reached while a `Lock` is recorded. So a promotion during that `Lock` moves the route when the promoted revision is carded **and a pass reaches `ProbingAfter`'s re-point**, and leaves it where it was otherwise. **What is left is the wedge**: the `Lock` cannot finish when the revision the route names has no recorded card **and `status.activeRevision` has none either**, because then there is nothing to re-point onto that could attribute, and no later pass changes that without a card (design 03 §3.3.3). It is not exempt, so the gate waits. *(A8, corrected by A9 and A10; narrowed by A19 for design 03 A77 and A78, which the human decided on 2026-09-15, ADR-0034 Amendment 6, and PR #48 implemented. A8 wrote "It never writes the route", which the re-point makes false.)*

That revision, called R here, is the one the route's **single** `backendRef` names, which is what `cardAttributes` reads; a route not carrying exactly one names no revision and is unattributable whatever `status.cards[]` holds (design 03 A77). R is not always `status.activeRevision` at the moment the `Lock` is entered: `reconcileServed` checks the policy before it re-ensures the route, so a missing-policy `Lock` can be entered in the very pass that promotes, and the switch has then set `status.activeRevision` to the promoted revision C while the route still names R. **That state no longer wedges when C is carded and a pass reaches the re-point.** The re-point moves the route onto C, and the `401` is then attributed on C's recorded digest. On a gated Agent C carries a card at that moment, because Q10's fourth promotion condition requires one. **A carded C is not enough on its own: a pass has to reach the re-point.** It sits at `ProbingAfter`, after the intact read, the fresh-NACK check and the foreign-policy check (`runLock`, design 03 §3.3.3), so a `Lock` short of `ProbingAfter` — one held at `ApplyingPolicies` by a foreign `traffic` policy at `<agent>-auth`, say — never re-points however carded C is, and design 03 A77 records that state as never taken over, never deleted, and unbounded (§8.1 case 18 (c)). **So the route and the active revision stay apart in three ways**: a pass that never reaches the re-point; a failed drift re-read that clears C's digest before one does, which `recordCardAttempt` can do while the `Lock` is still at `ApplyingPolicies` or `Converging`, and that is the ordinary case, since the `Lock` is entered on the pass the policy is found missing; and a promotion that never needed Q10's card at all — a pinned rollback Q5 admits, or a promotion after `spec.gates` was removed (Q9). *(Rewritten by A19. A9 ended this paragraph "If R has no recorded card, that `Lock` wedges even though C's card is recorded", which design 03 A77's re-point makes false in exactly that case.)*

| Aspect | In the wedge |
|---|---|
| The route | The `Lock` enters at `ApplyingPolicies` with `written` set, writes `<agent>-auth`, and reaches `ProbingAfter` only after the route and the policy converge (`lockMissingPolicy`, `runLock`). So the policy is in place and accepted, and an anonymous probe gets `401`. **The route is not open.** What is missing is proof that the `401` is the policy's, because R's card was never observed. Since design 03 A77 the stage also re-points the route at `status.activeRevision`, but only while that revision has a recorded card digest; in the wedge it has none, so the route is left as found *(added by A19)* |
| The Agent's conditions | `GovernanceSkipped=True` and `PolicyApplyIncomplete=True`, both `AuthPolicyMissing`, with design 03's message: the policy "has been re-created from status.auth", then the route note A77 and A78 add — the revision the route's single `backendRef` names, whether the route was "re-pointed at status.activeRevision" or "left as found", and, where nothing can attribute, that the state is "TERMINAL until a card records or an administrator acts" with both exits and which of them the state it reports admits (`missingPolicyRouteNote`) *(added by A19)* — then the stage, and "the last anonymous probe got a 401 that cannot be attributed". `PolicyApplyIncomplete` is raised at entry, so the Agent pages. `Ready=False`, `Degraded=True` with the same reason, and phase `Degraded` (`withholdReady`). Past the deadline the same, with the deadline noted |
| Each candidate | `GatesPassed=False/EvalWaitingForAuth`, whose message names the exits below |
| The ending that needs nobody *(added by A19)* | Not an exit anyone takes, which is why design 03's own message still counts two of those: `status.activeRevision`'s own card recording. The next pass that **reaches** the re-point — none does while a foreign `traffic` policy on `<agent>-serving`, a fresh NACK, or a stage short of `ProbingAfter` breaks the pass above it — re-points the route onto that revision; that pass's own write bumps the generation the route gate reads, so it takes no answer, and the `401` is attributed on a later pass once the Gateway has reported at the new generation. No administrator and no revert (design 03 A77, A78). The operator retries that fetch only while `status.activeRevision` is the **desired** revision — which it is once a rollout has settled — and only while that revision has a replica available, because a card is fetched from a ready desired revision alone (design 03 A60, `cardFetchDue`). **A pending candidate is the desired revision instead**, so nothing is fetching the active revision's card then. Design 03 A78 measured that such a state still ends with nobody acting when the candidate becomes available **and promotes**, since its own card records and the promotion fires the re-point — and **on a gated Agent under this wedge it usually cannot promote**, because no case is claimed while the `Lock` is recorded — but it still can by either of two paths: a verdict already `Passed` before the `Lock` began, or the outcome of the case in flight when it began, where that case is the run's last (step 7). So the gate closes the one exit A77 opened, and that is the cost Q13 (a) now accepts |
| The working exit | An administrator deletes `<agent>-serving` in `assayd-run-<ns>`. The route re-create (`recreateRoute`), a `Create` whose target comes from `status.auth`, displaces the `Lock`. It probes a prepared route with no `backendRefs`, so its `401` needs no card, and it publishes on `status.activeRevision` |
| That exit's cost | An outage of the Agent's route until `Create` publishes. Under a Gateway-level hold, or a foreign traffic policy on `<agent>-serving` (whose name the re-created route keeps, design 03 A72), `Create` does not publish, and the outage has no bound. A `Create` is recorded meanwhile, so the eval still waits |
| The transient exit | Revert the spec to R. R is then desired again, and its card is retried every `CardRetryInterval`, 15 s. A card that records lets the `Lock` credit its `401` on R. In the promoting-pass case the revert makes R a candidate, whose eval waits for the `Lock` that the recorded card now lets finish. A card that keeps failing never records, and the revert does nothing. Design 03 A78's warning — that with a candidate pending a revert aborts the rollout that would have ended this on its own — bites wherever that candidate can still promote, and there are two such paths, not one: a verdict already `Passed` before the `Lock` began, **and** the outcome of the case that was in flight when it began, where that case is the run's last, because step 7 puts the verdict in the last case's record ("The case in flight when a transaction begins"). Outside those two the gate is already holding the promotion, so there is no rollout to abort. *(Corrected by A19's second draft, from `reviews/16-a19-critique.md` MAJOR 4: the first named only the pre-`Lock` verdict, which excludes the path two of this design's own owed rows build, and A78 measured the revert HARMFUL in exactly that state.)* |
| Not exits, and when they became exits | Removing the gate lets the candidate promote, and since design 03 A77 that **is** an exit when the promoted revision has a recorded card digest and a pass reaches the re-point, because the promotion then fires it; it is not one when the digest is missing, and not one while no pass gets there. A pin moves `status.activeRevision`, and the route now follows it on the same condition — Q5 admits a pin to a retained, served revision, whose digest a failed drift re-read may since have cleared. Neither is an exit on a pass that never reaches the re-point: a foreign `traffic` policy on `<agent>-serving`, a fresh NACK, or a `Lock` still at `ApplyingPolicies` breaks the pass above it (design 03 §3.3.3, §8.1 case 18 (c)). *(Narrowed by A19. A8 wrote that neither moves the route.)* |
| Leaving it standing | The route is enforcing, only unproven, and the Agent stays paged with no new revision promoted. The garbage-collection outage needs the route and the active revision to stay **apart**, and design 03 A77 narrows that state without removing it: a carded `status.activeRevision` ends it only once a pass reaches the re-point, so the state survives whenever no pass does — a foreign `traffic` policy **on `<agent>-serving`**, which breaks the pass at the foreign check with the stage left at `ProbingAfter`; a fresh NACK; or a `Lock` short of `ProbingAfter`, which one stored **at** `<agent>-auth` produces by failing the intact read — and whenever a failed drift re-read clears that digest before one does. A gated promotion does not close it: Q10 puts a card on C, and the re-point still has to be reached. While they are apart, garbage collection protects only `status.activeRevision` and `status.candidateRevision`, and keeps the two newest other revisions (`collectGarbage`, `DefaultRevisionHistoryLimit`). So once three more revisions are minted after C (D, then E superseding D, then F superseding E), R is collected with its Service. The route still names that Service, and nothing notices, because the `Lock` checks only that the route is published. Authenticated callers then fail on a missing backend, while anonymous callers still get `401`. On an ungated Agent the same needs three promotions **of revisions whose cards never record**, because a promotion to a carded one fires the re-point **on the first pass that reaches it** and ends the state, and the same foreign policy, NACK or `Lock` short of `ProbingAfter` holds an ungated Agent as it holds a gated one; a card failure never withholds traffic (design 02 §3.4), so it is reachable, and it is design 03's residue rather than the gate's. *(Narrowed by A19. A9 wrote "The same happens on an ungated Agent after three promotions", with no card condition on them.)* Garbage collection is design 02's, and this slice does not change it |

- **Who can take the exit.** By default only a cluster administrator. The chart grants no user any rights in a run namespace (`charts/assayd/templates/rbac.yaml` binds the operator only). No Go source under `internal/` or `cmd/` creates a RoleBinding there. Admission reserves only `CREATE` and `UPDATE` on `httproutes` (`charts/assayd/templates/admission.yaml`), so a delete is decided by RBAC alone. So `EvalWaitingForAuth`'s message addresses an administrator of the run namespace, not the Agent's owner.
- **How the message is computed.** The Agent reconciler computes it in the gate branch, from its own read of `<agent>-serving` through the manager's client, and from `status.auth.transaction` as recorded at the start of the pass. It reads the route only on a pass on which status already meets the first and third of the clauses below, a missing-policy `Lock` at `ProbingAfter` and no card digest for `status.activeRevision` (`wedgeNeedsRoute`), and on no other, because a route read on every pass of every gated Agent would cost a read for a message that does not use it; a route on which `cardAttributes` attributes names no exits. The message opens with the generic text: the transaction's kind and stage, and why it is not exempt — the first of the exemption's clauses 1 to 4 that fails, or that a `Create` is recorded. Then, where it names the exits, comes a lead, saying that the state is design 03's and that deleting the route is an administrator's of the run namespace; then `missingPolicyRouteNote`'s own wording; then a tail, saying that a candidate's promotion waits on the `Lock` unless its verdict came before it or from the case in flight, and that `status.activeRevision`'s own card recording would end it too, though no fetch of that card runs while another revision is desired. The deadline clause comes last, when the deadline has passed (`waitingMessage`, `wedgeExits`).
  - **When it names the exits.** The recorded transaction is a missing-policy `Lock` at `ProbingAfter`; the route does not carry exactly one `backendRef`, or the revision its single `backendRef` names has no recorded card digest; **and `status.cards[]` records none for `status.activeRevision` either**. The reason for that third clause is not that a carded active revision ends the state — the apart state above is the counterexample — but that in **either** case the exits are the wrong thing to name: either the re-point ends it on a later pass, or what is stopping it is a hold the Agent's own `GovernanceSkipped` names and `PolicyApplyIncomplete`'s message carries beside `AuthPolicyMissing`, which outranks it (`incompleteOrder`) — a foreign `traffic` policy, a NACK, or a Gateway-level policy. The eval's message does not repeat it and must not answer it with an administrator's outage: under any of the three the delete costs an outage `Create` cannot end, and the revert leaves the pass breaking in the same place. Only a NACK moves the stage; a foreign policy and a Gateway-level hold leave it at `ProbingAfter`, so the stage clause does not point at them. It is not `status.eval.blocked` that names it: a re-creation fails clause 1, so clause 5 is never evaluated for this `Lock` and the slot stays empty. Those are `cardAttributes`' reading and the re-point's (`cardedRevision`), composed as `missingPolicyRouteNote` composes them, which returns early on either. *(Reason corrected by A19's third draft, from `reviews/16-a19-recritique.md` MAJOR 1: the second draft wrote "since otherwise the re-point ends the state on a later pass", which is the inference its own BLOCKER fix had killed.)* *(Corrected by A19. A11 wrote "no `backendRef` in any rule of the route", which was `cardAttributes`' reading until design 03 A77 narrowed it to the single ref, and it read nothing about `status.activeRevision`.)*
  - **R's name.** It is the `backendRef`'s name with the `WorkloadName` prefix `<agent>-` trimmed (`internal/controller/agent_controller.go`), so it survives R's Service being collected. **A route not carrying exactly one `backendRef` names no revision at all**: the message says it "does not carry exactly one backendRef, so it names no revision and is unattributable until it does", and offers only the administrator's exit, because the revert needs one named revision to revert to. That is the wording the operator already emits (`missingPolicyRouteNote`, `internal/controller/authtxn.go`), and this message reuses it rather than inventing a second phrasing for one state. *(Corrected by A19. A9's rule named each of several refs with no card and offered the revert only when there was exactly one; design 03 A77 makes such a route unattributable outright.)*
  - **It can be one pass stale.** The gate branch runs before `reconcileGateway`. If the route was deleted or stripped since the last pass, `runLock`'s re-creation branch replaces the `Lock` with `recreateRoute` later in the same pass. For that one pass, the written status pairs the new `Create` with a message about the `Lock`, and the next pass corrects it.
  - **The fallback.** A route that is not found has no R, and the message falls back to the generic `EvalWaitingForAuth` text. That is reachable only in the one stale pass above, because `runLock` then replaces the `Lock` with `recreateRoute`. A route that carries more than one `backendRef` is the sentence above rather than the fallback: it names no revision, and the message says so, with the administrator's delete as its only exit. That is where the operator emits that arm. A route that carries **none** reaches the same sentence only in the one stale pass, and the honest text there names no administrator: `routePublished` is false for such a route, so `runLock`'s re-creation branch hands it to `recreateRoute` in that same pass whenever `status.activeRevision` is set, which a served-under-`apikey` Agent always has. *(Split by A19. A11 put a route with no `backendRef` in the fallback, and before design 03 A77 a route with two was attributed on whichever ref had a card, so neither reached this bullet.)* A route read that fails with any other error is different. `currentServingRoute` returns the error, and `runLock` returns it (`internal/controller/httproute.go`, `authtxn.go`), so the `Lock` stays recorded, and the generic text shows for as long as that failure lasts. *(Scoped by A11, from the eighth critique's m6. Corrected by A12, from the ninth critique's m4.)*
- **The wedge is design 03's, not the gate's, and design 03 has narrowed it.** On an ungated Agent the same state wedges once a new revision is promoted **whose card is not recorded**; a promotion to a carded revision ends it **on the first pass that reaches the re-point** — which a foreign `traffic` policy on `<agent>-serving`, a fresh NACK, or a `Lock` short of `ProbingAfter` prevents — because the missing-policy `Lock` re-points its route at `status.activeRevision` while that revision is carded. With no new revision, the route's revision stays the desired one, and a card re-read can end it. What the gate adds is that a held candidate stops that re-read, **usually** cannot itself be promoted — except by the two paths this section already names, a verdict already `Passed` before the `Lock` began, or the outcome of the case in flight when it began where that case is the run's last — and so mostly closes the one exit the re-point opened: on a gated Agent the promotion that would fire it is the promotion the eval is waiting to earn. The amendment that made the re-point is **design 03 A77** — the human's decision of 2026-09-15 (ADR-0034 Amendment 6), through design 03's own critique, implemented by PR #48 with A78 beside it.
