It is safe for the same reason it is safe for J2: the policy targets the route, so moving the `backendRef` changes which revision answers, not what the route admits. **It is conditional twice over, so it narrows the wedge rather than removing it**: where neither the revision the route names nor `status.activeRevision` has a recorded card digest there is nothing to re-point onto that could attribute, and no later pass changes that without a card; and where no pass reaches the re-point, a carded `status.activeRevision` changes nothing either (design 03 §3.3.3). Q13 is the human accepting that state on 2026-09-14, against the wider wedge as it then stood; A19 records that it is narrower and reopens nothing. *(Rewritten by A19. A9 wrote this bullet while the amendment was hypothetical — "A design 03 amendment would remove the wedge … That amendment is outside this slice … Q13 asks the human whether to accept the wedge in this slice meanwhile" — and it has since landed, narrowing rather than removing, by the route Q13 (a) directed.)*

#### The case in flight when a transaction begins

The rule is checked at the live read before each claim, so it stops new claims, not a case already in flight. *(A6, A9 and A10.)*

- **Its record.** A case in flight when a transaction is entered records its outcome after the transaction began. That record bumps the resourceVersion, and the transaction's next pass loses its `Update` to `Conflict`, or stops at `requireFreshAgent` with `errStaleAgent` (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`). The cost is one pass, once, from at most one outcome.
- **The verdict.** That outcome may be the last case's, and then it carries the verdict, because the verdict is written in the same patch as the last case's record (step 7). No other verdict is written while a `Create` or `Lock` is recorded, because no case is claimed.
- **An `Interrupted` record.** Recording `Interrupted` for a claim found with no outcome (step 10) is an eval write, not a claim, and it also waits on the no-transaction predicate. So an orphaned last case cannot bring a verdict in while a transaction is recorded.
- **A promotion.** A promotion whose `Passed` verdict is already written is not stopped by a transaction or by a hold, because the promoting pass checks only the four promotion conditions.

#### The two controllers

The split between the two controllers:

- **The eval controller writes `status.eval` and nothing else.** It writes with a JSON merge patch under an optimistic lock (`client.MergeFromWithOptimisticLock`). Before every write it reads the Agent with the manager's uncached API reader, because the manager's cache can lag its own last patch. The lock guards a write, not the request the write is about, so a case is claimed before it is sent ("How a run works", step 10). *(Corrected by A5, from the re-critique's N1. A4 said the lock meant the controller never acted on a status it had not read. The act is the send, and the send came first.)*
- **The Agent reconciler never writes `status.eval`.** It reads the verdict, and it sets every `GatesPassed` reason. Its status write is a full `Status().Update` that carries the resourceVersion it read (`writeStatus`). So an Update racing an eval patch fails with `Conflict`, and the pass re-runs from a fresh read. It does not write back a stale `status.eval`. **Its own status does not change with eval progress.** `GatesPassed=False/EvalRunning` names the run's pair and its N cases, not how many are done; `status.eval.cases[]` shows that. So an eval patch makes the Agent reconciler run a pass whose status write is a no-op, because `writeStatus` returns early on an equal status. The two writers contend only when a run starts, is blocked, or ends. *(A5, from the re-critique's N1.)*
- **Both decide whether a case may be sent with the same predicates**, implemented once, in `internal/controller/sendpredicates.go`, whose comment tables which controller evaluates which, and called by both: the gate is declared; the EvalSuite CRD is installed; `status.candidateRevisionDigest` is set and differs from `status.activeRevisionDigest`; the candidate is available; no I1 hold stands (`authHoldsPromotion`); a card digest is recorded for the candidate's digest; **no `Create` or `Lock` is recorded in `status.auth.transaction`**, where neither a refused `Adopt` nor a J2 or K2 `Lock` that can finish only through a promotion counts (above); the candidate is not a pinned rollback — one Q5 (a) admits, or one it refuses on `failed[]` (below); and **`status.observedGeneration` equals `metadata.generation`**. The last one stops the sends to a candidate that a spec edit is about to supersede, before the Agent reconciler has recorded the new one. An edit to a referenced env source's content mints a revision without changing the generation. Until the Agent reconciler's next pass records the new candidate, cases keep going to the old one. Each is a real request, and none can promote anything, because the verdict is bound to the old digest. *(A5, from the re-critique's N4 and n7.)* The generation clause belongs to the eval controller alone. The Agent reconciler is the observer that sets `observedGeneration` in the same pass, so it does not evaluate the clause, and no `GatesPassed` row depends on it. *(A6, from the third critique's m6.)* The exemption's clause 5 is the eval controller's alone in the same way. The Agent reconciler reads its result from `status.eval.blocked`, and makes no Gateway or policy reads of its own. *(A11, from the eighth critique's m2.)* Build step 6 (PR #112) built the predicates, and build step 7 (PR #118) the exemption: clauses 1 to 4 in `exemptTransaction`, beside them, and clause 5 in `clause5`, `internal/controller/evalclause5.go`. Build step 8 (PR #119) added the pinned-rollback predicate. The Agent reconciler evaluates it in its pin step, by promoting an admitted pin past the gate. The eval controller derives it itself, from the spec, status and the content of the spec's env sources, and reads no condition, so a forged `GatesBypassed` pauses nothing: it holds the recorded candidate as a pinned rollback — one Q5 (a) admits, or one it refuses on `failed[]` — when the pin names `status.candidateRevisionDigest` and that is not the revision the current spec mints (`pinnedRollbackCandidate`, `specDigestOf`, over the `resolveEnvSourcesFrom` that the Agent reconciler's `resolveEnvSources` wraps). So a pin the Agent reconciler refuses as the current candidate leaves the recorded candidate's run going. Where the spec's digest cannot be computed the eval controller holds, since the Agent reconciler judges no pin on such a pass: a source NotFound or Forbidden, or a digest error, holds the pass at `CardDriftInterval`, and any other read error of a source is returned and retried under controller-runtime's backoff, sending nothing either way. The predicate is read after the suite is read and digested, and before the threshold and clause 5; the Agent reconciler's status write when it promotes the pin, or when the pin goes, brings the next pass. On a pass whose pin names the recorded candidate it reads every env source of the spec, ConfigMaps and Secrets, which the manager does not cache (`cmd/operator/main.go`), so each is an API-server `get`, which "What clause 5 costs" does not bill. The I1 predicate is read one way wherever both controllers judge it, read from the code and not measured (A20.26). On every pass that reaches `authHoldsPromotion`, the pin is resolved exactly when `spec.release.targetRevisionDigest` is set, because `resolveReleasePin` returns `unresolvablePinError` for a set pin that does not resolve, and the Agent reconciler then reports `ReleasePinUnresolvable` and returns before the I1 hold. The readings differ in that state, which the Agent reconciler never judges, and, since build step 8, while a pin is refused under Q5 (a), because that refusal too returns before the I1 hold. In both the eval controller takes the pin as set from `targetRevisionDigest` alone, so its I1 predicate turns the hold off (`i1Hold`): a run can start on the recorded candidate, its verdict outlives the state, and a `Failed` verdict puts the pair in `failed[]` for good. That is the cost; having the eval controller hold while a set pin does not resolve would be a later amendment. **While a refusal stands, the recorded candidate can be run to a verdict.** One write that reverts the spec to a retained revision B and pins it, while a later candidate C is recorded, refuses B as the current candidate; the refusal returns before the supersession and stamps `observedGeneration`, so C stays recorded and every send predicate holds for it. Review 5 of #119 measured it by a probe: C was sent its cases and recorded `Passed`. Nothing promotes while the refusal stands, and clearing the pin supersedes C; the release is untouched, and `status.eval` is not. `TestARefusedPinLeavesTheCandidatesRunGoing` pins a neighbouring shape, cases sent to a recorded candidate that is the spec's revision while a pin to it is refused; the revert-and-pin shape and its verdict are unpinned, owed on #115. *(A22.33 decided (b): the eval controller also holds when the pin equals the revision the current spec mints and does not name the recorded candidate, derived from the env-source reads it already makes, which (b) moves from passes whose pin names the recorded candidate to every pass with a pin set, so it reads the spec's env sources, uncached, on those passes too; until a follow-up amendment and code PR build it under #135, this stands)*
- **EvalSuites are read uncached, by both controllers**, with the manager's API reader: the eval controller on every pass on which the shared send predicates hold, and the Agent reconciler on every pass of a gated Agent whose desired revision is not the active one and whose Agent CRD schema check passes (step 11), where the third promotion condition needs the suite's digest as read in that pass, `EvalSuiteNotFound` and `EvalSuiteUnreadable` come from the same read, and the gate branch computes the pair from it before a run exists. No EvalSuite informer is started, so an absent CRD cannot fail manager start, and the owed RBAC stays `get` alone. A cached read in either controller would start a cluster-wide EvalSuite informer and would need `list` and `watch`, which the owed role does not grant, so it would be refused on every gated Agent. *(A15, from the eleventh critique's m7. A13 pinned the eval controller's reader and left the Agent reconciler's unstated.)* Design 02 §3.3 requires a CRD installed after start to take effect without a restart, and this keeps that true. A suite edit is noticed on the eval controller's next pass. While a candidate is held with a final verdict, the controller requeues every `CardDriftInterval`, 5 minutes. That interval is the bound on noticing a suite edit that re-opens a held candidate. An EvalSuite watch, started when discovery first sees the CRD, is out of the slice. *(A4, from the critique's m4.)*

On Q1's option (a), the same run happens inside the Agent reconciler instead. The split above then collapses into one writer, and Q1 lists that option's costs.

#### How a run works

1. **When a run starts.** The eval controller starts a run when the shared predicates above hold, when no run and no final verdict exists for the pair of revision digest and suite digest, and when that pair is not in `status.eval.failed[]` (step 7, Q3).

    **A run start replaces the whole record of the previous pair.** Everything in `status.eval` that a run produces belongs to the pair `status.eval` records. When the pair a run starts for differs from the recorded one, one patch — the run-start write — accounts for every field of the table below:

    - it records `suite`, `revision`, `revisionDigest`, `suiteDigest`, `endpointPath` and `cardDigest` for the new pair;
    - it sets `verdict` to `Running`;
    - it clears `score`, `at` and `cases[]`, which the previous pair's run produced;
    - it leaves `failed[]`, which is keyed by pair and carries finality (step 7); *(A25.4, proposed; under (b2), adds: it keeps `passed[]`, and appends to it the replaced record's pair when that record holds a `Passed` verdict, as `passed[]`'s row says; under (b1), it keeps `passed[]` unchanged, and the verdict patch of step 7 appends a pair it passes instead)*
    - it writes `blocked` empty and `blockedFor` naming the new pair, because a pass that starts a run has computed that nothing blocks it, and a stale refusal would otherwise stand beside a started run. No row pins the empty `blocked` yet; one is owed, #115.

    **`score` is why the clear cannot stop at the outcomes.** `api/v1alpha1/agent_types.go` renders it verbatim in the `Eval` printer column, so it is the default `kubectl get agents` output, and the safeguard the CRD names for a stale one — "`Revision` names what was gated, so a stale score is recognisable as stale" — is exactly what this patch would remove, because it moves `revision` and `revisionDigest` to the new pair in the same write. Left standing, the previous suite's score would show beside the new candidate for the whole re-run, with `at` carrying the previous verdict's timestamp and nothing marking either stale; and it would stand indefinitely if the candidate then became unavailable, because a paused run has no deadline (step 8). *(A15, from the eleventh critique's MAJOR 1. A13 cleared `cases[]` and `verdict` only, under a heading that claimed the record.)* Without the clear of `cases[]`, a suite edit — which Q3 makes the only re-run path — would find an entry for every suite `id`, left by the previous suite. No case would be sent, step 7's predicate would already be satisfied, and the run would either recompute a verdict from the old outcomes or never write one. *(A13, from the tenth critique's MAJOR 2.)*

    **The clears are not read back on their own**, and the read-back list (step 11) does not carry them. A merge patch expresses a clear as a null, and a stale CRD that prunes the field returns the same absence as a clear that applied, so no installed-CRD state exists that a read-back of a cleared field could tell apart. They ride in the patch whose set fields *are* read back, and are covered by it: on a stale CRD `suiteDigest`, `endpointPath` and `cardDigest` do not come back, and the pass stops before any case is sent. *(A15, from the eleventh critique's m5. A13 claimed a guard that cannot fire.)*
2. **The endpoint path, chosen once per run.** At run start, the eval controller fetches the candidate's card from its Service. It uses the eval client (step 4: no redirect followed, and `Proxy: nil`, so no environment proxy is used), not the card fetch's `fetchOnce`. It accepts the card only if its digest equals the digest `status.cards[]` records for the candidate's full digest, and it records that digest as `status.eval.cardDigest`, beside the path it takes from the card. Otherwise no run starts on this pass: the eval controller writes `status.eval.blocked`, reason `EvalAwaitingCard`, and the next pass tries again. *(A5, from the re-critique's n4 and N3.)* **Two causes share that reason, and the message distinguishes them**, because they are not the same finding: a card that was fetched and whose digest does not match the recorded one, and a fetch that did not succeed at all — a refused connection, a timeout, a body that does not parse — where no digest was compared. Reporting a mismatch for a refused connection names a check that was never made, the distinction step 6 already draws between `Unreachable` and `Interrupted`. The failed-fetch message names the error class, and that pass retries at `CardRetryInterval` rather than `CardDriftInterval` ("What wakes the eval controller while it waits"). *(A15, from the eleventh critique's m4.)* `cardDigest` is a record of where the path came from. The fourth promotion condition still accepts any recorded card digest: a card that drifts after the run changes nothing the verdict covers, and requiring the same digest would hold a passed revision because its card was redescribed. From that card it takes the first `supportedInterfaces[]` entry whose `protocolBinding` is `HTTP+JSON` and whose `protocolVersion` is `1.0`. The join: take only `url.Parse(entry.url).Path`, admitted only as "Which paths are admitted" below says, trim one trailing `/`, and append `/message:send`. A path of `""` or `/` gives `/message:send`, and `/a2a/` gives `/a2a/message:send`. An entry whose URL carries a query or a fragment is refused, as is a URL that does not parse. So is a card with no such entry at all. Each of these refusals is written to `status.eval.blocked`, reason `EvalBindingUnsupported`. The chosen path is recorded in `status.eval.endpointPath` and used for every case of that run, so a restart resumes against the same path. `status.cards[]` records no interfaces, so without this record the path would have to be re-derived from a card that may have changed. *(A4, from the critique's M3.)*

   The run-start fetch is bounded by `DefaultCardFetchTimeout`, 5 s. A body over 1 MiB is a fetch that did not succeed, and so is one that is not JSON: `json.Valid` is checked before the digest is compared, so such a body never reads as a mismatch. No row pins the 5 s bound or the 1 MiB bound yet; both are owed, #115.

   A body whose digest matches the recorded one but whose `supportedInterfaces[]` do not decode — an entry with a field of the wrong JSON type, a numeric `tenant` say — is `EvalBindingUnsupported`, retried at `CardDriftInterval`, because it is a property of the card the operator recorded. No row pins that interval yet; one is owed, #115.

   A run-start refusal's message names a class of failure, never an error's raw text, and never quotes the card's URL, an address the agent chose. A failed fetch's message is the same while the failure is the same; a digest mismatch's message names the digest the candidate served.

   The first `HTTP+JSON` 1.0 entry is the one judged, and a refused entry is not passed over for a later one. It is refused, under `EvalBindingUnsupported`, for a URL that does not parse; then for a query or a fragment, a bare `?` or `#` included; then for a path the eval does not admit; then for a `tenant`; and the endpoint is then bounded, as below.

   **An interface that names a tenant is refused**, under `EvalBindingUnsupported` (the human's decision of 2026-10-18, #108, option (a), to be revisited when a multi-tenant agent needs a gate). `AgentInterface.tenant`, when set, must be sent with every request to that interface, and the eval's `SendMessageRequest` carries none: sending anyway would either be refused by an honest agent, putting the pair in `failed[]` for the eval's own omission, or be routed to another tenant, whose answers the verdict would then certify. The refusal holds the candidate and records nothing final. The card is decoded into `evalclient.Interface`, which carries `tenant`.

   **Which paths are admitted.** A path is admitted only if the card wrote no percent-escape in it (`RawPath` is empty) and every byte is an ASCII letter, a digit, or one of `-` `.` `_` `~` `/` `:` `@` `$` `&` `+` `,` `;` `=`. Those are the bytes `url.URL` puts on the request line unescaped, so an admitted path is recorded and sent exactly as the card wrote it. Any other path is refused, under `EvalBindingUnsupported`, with a message that lists the allowed set and names the fix. A URL with no path component, an opaque one such as `urn:x` or `http:a%2Fb`, has an empty `Path` and is admitted as `/message:send`.

   `status.eval.endpointPath` holds at most 1024 characters. The eval controller counts runes to match the CRD's `maxLength` and refuses a longer endpoint — the trimmed path with `/message:send` appended — before anything is written. It also refuses an endpoint that does not begin with `/`, which a relative card URL such as `a2a` gives. Both refusals are `EvalBindingUnsupported`.
3. **Where the request goes.** To the candidate revision's Service, by its ClusterIP and `spec.runtime.port`, as the card fetch does (`internal/controller/card.go`), at `endpointPath`. The host and port in the card's URL are ignored: a card is written by the agent, and the operator sends nothing to an address the agent chose. `evalclient.Check` is the one validator of that address, for the client and the controller alike: `net.SplitHostPort` succeeds, the host is an IP address, the port is from 1 to 65535, and a request can be built. `Send` refuses exactly what it refuses. The eval controller calls it before the run-start fetch and before every claim, and when it refuses, or the candidate's Service cannot be read, the pass sends nothing, and claims and records no case, because an address fault is not the candidate's, and recording it would fail the candidate for good through `failed[]`; the pass returns the error ("What wakes the eval controller while it waits"). Before it returns, the pass writes the fourth reason, `EvalCandidateUnaddressable`, to `blocked` with `blockedFor`, for every Service read failure and every `Check` refusal, except on a run-start pass that started the run, whose one write was the run-start patch and which writes nothing more; the message carries the failure's class or the part refused, not the error's text, so a failure that persists writes once (A24.4, A24.5, A24.19 (a), built by PR #142). `Check` returns a typed refusal that names the part refused — the host, the port, or the request — and maps `net.SplitHostPort`'s refusal, which has no part of its own, to the host. The message is "the eval controller cannot derive an address to send a case to: <fault>. Nothing is fetched, claimed or sent, and no outcome is recorded, because the fault is not the candidate's. The Agent reconciler re-creates a missing Service, and replaces a headless one it recorded, on a pass that reaches its Service step; its own conditions say when it does not", where <fault> is "the candidate's Service <namespace>/<name> was not found", "… could not be read (<class>)", with the class from `readErrorClass`, or "the candidate's Service <namespace>/<name>: " followed by the part refused: "its ClusterIP <ip> is not an IP address", "spec.runtime.port <n> is not from 1 to 65535", or "no request can be built for the path <path>" (A24.7, answered (a)). The repair sentence is hedged because the Agent reconciler does not reach its Service step while a pin refusal stands or a source is unresolved. One residue (#143): a request refusal is reached through a zoned IPv6 host whose escaped zone `http.NewRequest` cannot parse, and there the message names the path although the host is the cause, as `TestCheckNamesThePartItRefuses` and `TestAnUnaddressableCandidateIsNamed` measure; that no path alone reaches it, because `url.URL` escapes the path, and that a Service's ClusterIP carries no zone are read from the code, not measured. The controller's branch for an error from `Send` therefore cannot fire, and no test reaches it, which is rule 5's tension, stated.
4. **No redirect is followed.** The eval client's `CheckRedirect` returns `http.ErrUseLastResponse`, so any `3xx` is `ProtocolError`. Go's default client follows up to 10 redirects, and on `307` and `308` it re-sends a `POST` body. So a candidate answering `307 Location: <anywhere>` would make the operator re-post the suite's message from the operator's own network position, and read one bit back. `internal/controller/authprobe.go` already refuses redirects this way. **The card fetch had the same hole, for `GET`, when the slice was approved:** `fetchOnce` used `&http.Client{}` with no `CheckRedirect`. Its fix was owed to design 02 §3.4, in a separate change outside this slice, and PR #38 made it: `fetchOnce` now uses `cardHTTPClient`, with `CheckRedirect: refuseRedirects` and no environment proxy (`internal/controller/card.go`) (#101). *(A4, from the critique's M4.)*
5. **What is sent.** One `SendMessageRequest` per case, as `docs/research/a2a-v1.0-card-and-transport-2026-09.md` §2 pins it: the header `A2A-Version: 1.0`; a `message` with a fresh `messageId`, `role: ROLE_USER`, and one text part carrying `cases[].message`; no `contextId`, so every case opens a fresh context; and no `configuration`, so the call blocks until the task is terminal or interrupted. **That last one is the protocol's default, not this design's choice**, and it is load-bearing: were it the other way, every case would come back `TASK_STATE_SUBMITTED` and step 6 would score it `fail`/`TaskNotCompleted`, failing every honest revision. The default is `SendMessageConfiguration.return_immediately`, a `bool` whose proto3 default is false, whose normative comment in `specification/a2a.proto` at `v1.0.1` reads "If `false` (default), the operation MUST wait until the task reaches a terminal (`COMPLETED`, `FAILED`, `CANCELED`, `REJECTED`) or interrupted (`INPUT_REQUIRED`, `AUTH_REQUIRED`) state before returning". §2 of the research document records it. `test/responder` is not cited for it: it is this repository's own fixture, and a fixture cannot corroborate a protocol default — that is the circularity `internal/controller/card.go` records as how a v0.x card shape survived here undetected. *(A15, from the eleventh critique's m8. A13 attributed the default to a §2 that listed `configuration` as optional and said nothing about omitting it.)* No credential is sent. The candidate is on no route, and design 03 puts `-auth` on the route, so nothing between the operator and the candidate asks for one. The request also carries `Content-Type: application/a2a+json`, the type the A2A specification at `v1.0.1`, §11.1, says SHOULD be used for requests and responses on the HTTP+JSON binding (`docs/specification.md`).
6. **What a case's outcome is.** The answer text is every text part of `task.artifacts[].parts[]`, or of `message.parts[]` when the response is a message, joined by one space, in order. The body is read up to 1 MiB. Only `{id, outcome, reason}` is recorded, never the answer text.

   | Answer | Outcome | Reason |
   |---|---|---|
   | `200`; `task.status.state` is `TASK_STATE_COMPLETED`, or the response is a message; the answer text satisfies `expect` | pass | — |
   | the same, and the answer text does not satisfy `expect` | fail | `Mismatch` |
   | `200`, and the task is in any other state | fail | `TaskNotCompleted` |
   | any `3xx` (step 4), any other status, a body over 1 MiB, or a body that is not a `SendMessageResponse` | fail | `ProtocolError` |
   | a refused connection, or no answer within `caseTimeoutSeconds` | fail | `Unreachable` |
   | a claim found with no outcome (step 10), or a send cut short by cancellation | fail | `Interrupted` (Q11) |

   A case the candidate answered badly, or not at all, fails. The cost is that one network blip fails a good revision (Q3).

   `status.eval.cases[].outcome` is `Sending`, `Passed` or `Failed`, and the Agent CRD's enum admits those three alone. The table's pass and fail are `Passed` and `Failed`; `reason` carries the table's words.

   `Unreachable` is every failure before a status line is read — a refused or reset connection, an answer that is not HTTP — and the timeout, whether it passes before the status line or while a `200`'s body is still arriving. A body that breaks off for any other reason is `ProtocolError`.

   In this table, "a body that is not a `SendMessageResponse`" means one whose answer path does not read as one. The members the answer is taken from — `task` or `message`, `task.status.state`, and the parts — are read strictly: keys match exactly, a duplicate key anywhere in the body is `ProtocolError`, and so is a member on that path of the wrong JSON type, or a text part beside another member of its oneof. The body must be valid UTF-8. The rest of the body is not validated, so a body protojson would refuse can be scored: a task whose `status.timestamp` is not a timestamp, a string holding an escaped lone surrogate, which is read as U+FFFD, or a part with no text, which is checked only for being an object.
7. **The verdict, and the record of failed pairs.** Once every suite `id` has exactly one entry in `cases[]` **with a recorded outcome**, `score` is passed ÷ total. *(A12; tightened by A13, from the tenth critique's m5: a `Sending` entry is a claim in flight, not an outcome.)* The verdict is written in the same patch as that case's record, so it adds no patch of its own (A9, from the sixth critique's m3). The run passes when `passed × 1000 ≥ t × total`, where `t` is the threshold in thousandths. That is integer arithmetic, so no rounding decides a boundary. On Q3's recommended answer, a `Failed` verdict also appends `{revisionDigest, suiteDigest}` to `status.eval.failed[]`. *(A25.4, proposed; under (b1), adds: a `Passed` verdict appends its pair to `status.eval.passed[]` in the same patch, with no read-back)* *(A25.11, proposed; under (b), adds: and removes the revision's entry from `status.eval.passed[]`, in the same patch)* That list holds at most 10 entries, and the oldest is evicted, the same cap as `status.supersededCandidates`. A pair in `failed[]` never starts a run again, and reads `EvalPairAlreadyFailed` once `status.eval` no longer holds its `Failed` record (below). So reverting a failed revision B to A and re-applying B does not re-run B. The record is bounded, and a re-run is still possible in two ways: after ten other failed pairs have evicted this one, or after any visible edit of the suite, which gives a new pair. Q3 states this. *(A4, from the critique's M1.)*

   From the patch that writes a `Failed` verdict, the pair is both the record's and `failed[]`'s. The gate branch gives `EvalFailed` while `status.eval` holds the failed run's record for the pair, since that names the failed cases and the score, and `EvalPairAlreadyFailed` once it does not (the human's decision of 2026-10-18, #111).

   When the spec moves away from a failed revision, the eval controller clears that run's record, so that re-applying it reads `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`. It does so when `status.eval.verdict` is `Failed` and `status.eval.revisionDigest` differs from `status.candidateRevisionDigest` while a candidate is pending, otherwise from `status.activeRevisionDigest`, on a pass of a gated Agent with the EvalSuite CRD installed, `observedGeneration` current and no `Create` or `Lock` recorded, an exempt `Lock` counting as recorded ("The exemption"). It clears `verdict`, `cases[]`, `score` and `at`, and keeps `failed[]`, `suite`, `revision`, `revisionDigest`, `suiteDigest`, `endpointPath`, `cardDigest`, `blocked` and `blockedFor`. A `Passed` record is never cleared, nor a `Running` one.
8. **An unavailable candidate pauses the run.** The rollout step's not-ready branches come before the gate. So a candidate that stops being available mid-run is sent no further case. Its recorded outcomes stay, and the run resumes at the next case by `id` once it is available again. In the gate branch too, a candidate that is not available is answered before `blocked` and `blockedFor`, in both states ("The gate branch's order"), because nothing computes `blocked` on a pass whose candidate is not available, so a `blocked` that matches was computed before the candidate went away. The first clause-5 pass after the candidate comes back re-reads the hold, and clears it if it has gone. The candidate reads `GatesPassed=False/EvalPaused`, naming k of N. It also reads `EvalPaused` before any run has started, saying none has, and k counts only the current pair's record. The not-ready branches report whatever the gate branch answers for the candidate, `EvalPaused` only when no reason ranked above it holds, so a new gated Agent carries `GatesPassed` before its revision is available. When a `blocked` applies, the message says so: beside a clause-5 hold recorded for this `Lock`, that the run resumes once the candidate is available again and that hold, re-read then, has cleared; beside a run-start refusal, naming it, that a run starts only once the candidate is available again and the refusal is answered. A `blocked` whose `blockedFor` names another pair or `Lock` is not named. Nothing times a paused run out: a crashlooping candidate stays held for good, and that is fail-closed. This departs from §9, which fails "the crash cases": the slice fails only cases that were sent. A run-level deadline is out of the slice. *(A4, from the critique's m1.)*
9. **Pacing.** The eval controller sends one case per pass. After each case it patches the outcome into `status.eval.cases[]`, and returns `RequeueAfter: 5s` as a backstop. The next pass is driven by that patch: the controller's `For(&Agent{})` has no generation predicate, so its own status write enqueues the Agent again. It does not return `Requeue: true`, because that goes through the rate limiter, whose per-item backoff grows exponentially and is not reset while the run continues. *(A4, from the critique's m2.)* How each case is sent at most once is step 10.
10. **The send protocol: read, claim, send, record.** *(A5, from the re-critique's N1. A4 said a recorded case was never sent again, but the send came before the locked write. So a routine race with the Agent reconciler dropped an outcome and re-sent its case, and cache lag re-sent a case already recorded.)*
    - **Read live.** Before claiming, the eval controller reads the Agent with the uncached API reader. A pass that trusted the cache could see a recorded case as unrecorded.
    - **Keyed by `id`, not position.** The next case is the first suite `id`, in `id` order, that has no entry in `cases[]`. Claims and records find their entry by `id`, and the verdict requires exactly one entry per suite `id`. A reordered suite keeps its digest and its pair, so a pick by position could re-send a case whose outcome is recorded and never send another. Entries belong to the pair `status.eval` records, and a run start for a different pair clears them (step 1). *(A12, from the ninth critique's MAJOR 2; the pair scoping by A13.)*
    - **Claim.** It writes the next case's entry, `{id, outcome: Sending, claim: <nonce>}`, by a merge patch under the optimistic lock, with a nonce that is fresh for this send, and reads the claim back (step 11). A request follows only a claim that succeeded and came back. A claim that fails with `Conflict` sends nothing, and the next pass starts again at the live read. So a lost race costs a pass, never a second request.
    - **Send, on a context that shutdown does not cancel.** One request, as steps 3 to 6 say. It is sent synchronously inside the pass, never in a goroutine, because controller-runtime's `Start` waits only for its workers and would not wait for a goroutine. The claim before the send, the send, and the record after it all run on `context.WithoutCancel` of the reconcile context. The claim is bounded by 5 s, the send by `caseTimeoutSeconds`, and the record by 5 s including its retries. The client reads a deadline on its caller's context that passes first as `Unreachable`, and only a cancellation as `Interrupted`, so a caller must not bound the send tighter than `caseTimeoutSeconds`. Nothing enforces this. Immediately before the claim, the pass checks the reconcile context: once `ctx.Err()` is set, it claims nothing and returns. So no case is claimed after shutdown begins, and a claim that starts always completes. Between the cancel and the controller's call to `Queue.ShutDown()`, a worker that returns from a pass can still dequeue a queued Agent and run its reconcile on the cancelled context. After `ShutDown`, the default priority queue hands out nothing. So the check guards two kinds of pass: one already running between its live read and its claim when the cancel lands, and one that starts in the window before `ShutDown` (A8, from the fifth critique's s3; widened by A9, from the sixth critique's m6). *(A7, from the fourth critique's r1. A6 left the claim on the reconcile context, where a cancellation during its patch could commit the claim and lose the response, and gave no mechanism for "no new case".)* Controller-runtime cancels the reconcile context when the manager shuts down, then waits up to `GracefulShutdownTimeout` for reconciles in flight. `managerOptions` in `cmd/operator/main.go` sets it explicitly, to 30 s, and `TestManagerWaitsForReconcilesInFlightOnShutdown` holds it at 25 s or more (build step 2, PR #104), because a bound that rests on an unset default is rule 7's case (A7, from the fourth critique's r2). The chart's `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` is also 30 (`charts/assayd/templates/operator.yaml`). A claim, a case and its record take at most 5 s + 10 s + 5 s = 20 s, inside both. So a graceful stop lets the case in flight finish and record what the candidate answered. That covers every `helm upgrade`, drain and rolling update. A chart test, `TestOperatorGracePeriodLetsACaseInFlightFinish` (`test/chart/shutdown_test.go`, build step 2), keeps `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` at 25 s or more. *(A6, from the third critique's P2. A5 named no context. The obvious one, the reconcile context, would have failed the case in flight on every rolling update of the operator.)*
    - **Record.** It writes the outcome in place of `Sending`. On `Conflict`, it re-reads live and re-applies the outcome, but only while the claim still stands: the same `revisionDigest`, `suiteDigest` and `claim` at that case's entry, found by `id`, **and `outcome` still `Sending`**. The last condition stops an old leader, still finishing its send during a handover, from overwriting an `Interrupted` that the new leader wrote after it may already have computed the verdict. *(A6, from the third critique's m3.)* The eval controller is the only writer of `status.eval`, so a newer status written by the Agent reconciler never invalidates the outcome. A claim that no longer stands, because a new generation superseded the run, is dropped with the run.
    - **A claim found with no outcome** means the eval controller stopped between claiming and recording without a graceful stop. That is SIGKILL, an OOM kill, a lost node, a lease lost to a partition, or a record that exhausted its 5 s bound. Nobody knows whether the candidate received the request. Q11 decides what happens. On its recommended answer, the case is never sent again: it is recorded as failed, reason `Interrupted`, and counts against the threshold like any failed case. A send cut short by cancellation, which only a hard stop can now do, is also `Interrupted`, never `Unreachable`. `Unreachable` names a failure observed before a status line was read — a refused or reset connection, an answer that is not HTTP — or a timeout, before the status line or while a `200`'s body is still arriving (step 6). *(A6, from the third critique's P2 and m3.)*
    - **An ambiguous claim fails closed.** A claim patch that the API server commits, but whose response is lost (a timeout, a 5xx, a reset), is not a `Conflict`. That pass sends nothing. The next pass finds the claim with no outcome and records `Interrupted`, for a case the candidate never received. It costs what a hard stop costs, and it never sends twice. Keeping ambiguous nonces in memory, to send them later within the same process, is out of the slice. *(A6, from the third critique's m3.)*
    - **The eval controller is leader-gated.** It runs only in the manager that holds the lease. That is controller-runtime's default for a controller, and nothing in `cmd/` or `internal/` overrides it. At most once does not depend on it: the optimistic lock admits one claim per case, whoever makes it. *(A6, from the third critique's m3.)*
    - **A re-sent case is not assumed to be idempotent for the candidate.** A2A gives each message a client-chosen `messageId`, and this design does not rely on any agent de-duplicating by it. Every send carries a fresh `messageId`. So a case sent twice would be two tasks, with two sets of tool calls and spend. That is why the protocol claims first.
11. **The record must be kept.** *(A5, from the re-critique's N2.)* The new `status.eval` fields are fields of the **Agent** CRD, and `helm upgrade` never updates `crds/`. An Agent CRD that predates them prunes them without an error. Every claim would then vanish, and case 1 would be sent every 5 s for as long as the candidate exists, with no condition saying so. Two guards stop that:
    - **The eval controller reads back what it writes.** It reads back the fields its run-start write sets — `verdict: Running`, `suite`, `revision`, `revisionDigest`, `suiteDigest`, `endpointPath` and `cardDigest` — every `blocked` and `blockedFor` write, and every claim, from the object the patch returns, as design 03's `persistStatus` reads back `status.auth` (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`). If a field did not come back, it sends nothing, writes nothing more, and returns `RequeueAfter: CardDriftInterval`. The run-start write carries nothing that changes from pass to pass: `score` and `at` are cleared by it rather than written by it, and a clear carries the same value every time. So on a stale CRD, a repeated run-start write carries the same values, the API server stores no change, and no event re-enqueues the Agent. The cost is at most one card fetch and one no-op patch per gated Agent per `CardDriftInterval`, plus one more per other event on the Agent, such as the Agent reconciler's own status writes, until the CRD is applied. On a stable Agent those events are the card drift re-reads (A8, from the fifth critique's s4). *(A7, from the fourth critique's r3. A6 said only that the controller sent nothing, and the obvious reading re-fetched the card and rewrote status on every pass.)* This read-back is the eval controller's only guard against a stale CRD. It does not also check the schema, so each guard is pinned by a test of its own (A6, from the third critique's m1). A `Running` record with no `endpointPath` or no `cardDigest` starts the run again rather than being sent on, so the run start's read-back stops the pass each time; without it a later pass would send to the path `""`.
    - **The Agent reconciler names the cause.** A pruned write cannot carry the new fields, `blocked` included, so the eval controller cannot report the pruning through `blocked`. The older `status.eval` fields and the conditions do survive, and a run-start write that left `revisionDigest` naming the candidate with no `verdict` would betray a stale CRD. The slice checks the schema instead, because that names the cause before any write. On every pass of a gated Agent whose desired revision is not the active one, the Agent reconciler reads the `agents.assayd.dev` CustomResourceDefinition. It checks that version `v1alpha1`'s entry in `spec.versions[]` carries, under `schema.openAPIV3Schema`'s `status.eval`, each of `suiteDigest`, `endpointPath`, `cardDigest`, `blocked`, `blockedFor`, `verdict`, `cases` (with `claim` in its items) and `failed`. *(A25.6, proposed; adds: and `passed`, which `evalRecordFields` gains; `passed[]` is not read back, so on a CRD that does not keep it the pair a run moves is lost, and the hold above stops promotion, not evaluation)* `blockedFor` is in the set because the gate branch's fail-closed reading rests on it: without the field, every read of it is "absent", and an exempt `Lock` would report `EvalWaitingForAuth` for ever while clause 5 found nothing every 5 s. *(A13, from the tenth critique's MAJOR 1.)*
      - **The reader.** The read registers `apiextensions.k8s.io/v1` types in the operator's scheme (`cmd/operator/main.go`, build step 5, pinned by `TestTheSchemeKnowsTheCustomResourceDefinition`). `k8s.io/apiextensions-apiserver` is already a direct dependency. It uses the manager's uncached API reader behind a TTL cache like `EvalSuiteDetector`'s, whose TTL is 30 s, `DefaultEvalRecordCheckTTL`. So the Agent CRD, about 74 KB, is fetched once per TTL rather than once per pass, and no cluster-wide CRD informer is started. The operator already holds `get` on `customresourcedefinitions`.
      - **A missing field.** It sets `GatesPassed=False/EvalRecordNotKept`, with the remedy design 03's `AuthRecordNotKept` gives: apply this release's `charts/assayd/crds/` with `kubectl`, because `helm upgrade` never updates it.
      - **A failed read.** The last answer in the TTL cache stands, as the detector's does. With no answer cached, it sets `GatesPassed=False/EvalRecordUnverifiable`, naming the error.
      - Either way the candidate holds, which is fail-closed. *(A6, from the third critique's m2. A5 said a pruned write could carry no reason at all, and the older fields do survive.)*
    - Applying the Agent CRD's new status fields on upgrade is owed to design 07, beside Q7.

#### Promotion

The rollout step keeps its order (`internal/controller/agent_controller.go`): the not-ready branches, then the I1 hold, then the gate, then promotion. The slice changes only what the gate branch asks. Before the slice, with a gate declared and the EvalSuite CRD installed, that branch held with no exit, reason `GateControllerUnimplemented`; build step 6 retired that reason, and the branch answers from the four conditions below, in `decideGate` (`internal/controller/gate.go`). In the slice the gate is satisfied only when all four of these are true, read in the same pass:

- `status.eval.verdict` is `Passed`;
- `status.eval.revisionDigest` equals the desired revision's full digest (A2);
- `status.eval.suiteDigest` equals the digest of the EvalSuite as read in this pass;
- `status.cards[]` records a non-empty card digest for the desired revision's full digest. *(A4, from the critique's M6.)*

A satisfied gate is not a branch of its own. The gate branch's condition is "the gate is not satisfied", so a satisfied gate falls through to the promotion branch in the same pass, as a gate-free Agent does today. That pass is the Agent reconciler's first pass after the eval controller writes the `Passed` verdict, which its status patch triggers. *(A4, from the critique's m2.)*

**The fourth condition is new enforcement (Q10).** Today a card failure never withholds traffic (design 02 §3.4, `agent_controller.go`). Under the slice, a gated candidate with no recorded card cannot be promoted. That is because the card precondition at run start does not hold until promotion. The card is re-read every `CardDriftInterval`, and a failed re-read clears the recorded digest (`recordCardAttempt`, `internal/controller/card.go`). A run of 20 cases can span one such re-read. The fourth condition is what makes the guarantee in "How it composes with what exists" true at the moment of promotion.

If any condition is false, the candidate holds. The branch keeps its guard, `status.activeRevisionDigest != desiredDigest`. So adding a gate, or editing the suite, never evaluates the revision already serving. `gates` is on design 02's policy surface, so adding one mints nothing, and the serving revision is evaluated only if a later spec mints it again.

**A failed verdict holds the candidate. It does not delete it (Q2).** The phase is `Held`, and no route names the candidate, until a new generation supersedes it (design 02 §3.3) or the suite's digest changes. Design 02 §3.3's rollout step 5, and §4 step 5 here, say "candidate deleted". The slice cannot keep that: the desired spec still names the revision, so the next pass would re-create it and evaluate it again (below, "What design 16 says that the slice cannot keep").

**Two edits skip the gate, and the slice states both.**

- **Removing `spec.gates` promotes a held candidate on the next pass**, under `GatesSkipped=True/NoGatesDeclared`. The candidate may be one whose verdict was `Failed`. `gates` is on the policy surface, so removing it mints nothing, and anyone with `agents/update` can do it. Q9 decides whether that stays so.
- **A pin, `spec.release.targetRevisionDigest`, selects any retained workload that carries the pinned digest.** It does not check that the revision ever served (`resolveReleasePin`, `internal/controller/release.go`). A failed candidate is retained, because garbage collection protects `status.candidateRevision`. So without a rule, a pin to the failed candidate's digest would serve the revision that just failed, under a reason that calls it a rollback. Q5 gives the rule. *(A4, from the critique's M7.)* The rule applies only while a gate is declared. On an Agent with no gate, pins behave exactly as today (A5, from the re-critique's N5).

#### Status, conditions and phase

`status.eval` (design 02's `EvalStatus`) gains eight fields, owed to design 02: *(A25.4, proposed; under (b1) or (b2), replaces "eight": "nine", and adds a row after `failed[]`'s: "`passed[]` | new: at most 10 `{revisionDigest, suiteDigest}`, pairs whose `Passed` record a run start replaced (under (b2)) or that a verdict patch passed (under (b1)), at most one per revision digest: a new entry for a revision replaces its old one at the newest place, and the oldest whose revision digest is not `status.activeRevisionDigest` is evicted. Under A25.11 (b), a `Failed` verdict removes the revision's entry. The unpassed statement reads it by revision digest (A25.1), and under A25.5 (i) so do `GatesPassed` and `GatesBypassed`; the gate never does")*

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `suite`, `revision`, `revisionDigest` | as today; written at run start, for the pair being evaluated (step 1) |
| `score`, `at` | as today; `score` is rendered `passed/total` and is the `Eval` printer column (`api/v1alpha1/agent_types.go`). Both are written with the verdict (step 7), and a run start for a different pair clears them (step 1, A15), and so does the clear of a superseded `Failed` record (step 7) |
| `suiteDigest` | new: the suite digest the run used |
| `endpointPath` | new: the path chosen at run start (step 2); at most 1024 characters, the CRD's `maxLength`, and decoded, which for a path the eval admits is the path as the card wrote it |
| `cardDigest` | new: the digest of the card `endpointPath` was taken from (step 2) |
| `blocked` | new: `{reason, message}`: the one reason, if any, why no case can be sent, among four that only the eval controller observes. They are `EvalCandidateUnaddressable`, for a candidate whose address cannot be derived (step 3); `EvalBindingUnsupported` and `EvalAwaitingCard`, from the run-start fetch (step 2); and `EvalWaitingForAuth`, for clause 5's hold (A24.4, built by PR #142). It is computed once per pass in that precedence, and written only when the computed value, or the pair or `Lock` it is computed for, changes ("`blocked`: one slot, one computation"). The Agent reconciler turns it into `GatesPassed` (A5, A11, A12) |
| `blockedFor` | new: `{revisionDigest, suiteDigest, lockDeadline}`: the pair, and the `Lock`, that `blocked` was computed for. `blocked` applies only while it matches the current state; absent or unmatched reads as not evaluated (A12) |
| `verdict` | new: `Running`, `Passed` or `Failed`, or empty after the clear of a superseded `Failed` record (step 7), with the pair kept |
| `cases[]` | new: at most 20 `{id, outcome, reason, claim}`, at most one per suite `id`, kept sorted by `id`, the digest's canonical order, and never in the suite's written order (A12). The list belongs to the pair `status.eval` records, and a run start for a different pair clears it (step 1, A13). `outcome` is `Sending` between a claim and its record (step 10), and `Passed` or `Failed` after it, the only three values the CRD's enum admits. The clear of a superseded `Failed` record (step 7) empties the list too |
| `failed[]` | new: at most 10 `{revisionDigest, suiteDigest}`, oldest evicted (step 7, Q3) |

Only the operator writes it, through the status subresource, and only the eval controller within the operator (above). A principal who can write `agents/status` can forge a pass, exactly as it can forge `activeRevisionDigest`. Design 02 §3.3's argument that "`status` is the authority" covers both.

**Where each reason is set.** `assessGates` runs before the card fetch, the I1 hold and the verdict are known (`agent_controller.go`). So it keeps only the reasons it can decide there: `GatesSkipped` with `NoGatesDeclared` or `EvalSuiteCRDAbsent`; `GatesPassed=False/GateDetectionUnwired`; and `GatesPassed=False/NoGatesDeclared` on an Agent whose gate was removed (A5). Since build step 8 it also sets the serving form of `GatesBypassed=True/PinnedRollback`, one of three places that set `GatesBypassed`: `assessGates` sets its serving form from status as the pass found it, so every exit of the pass keeps it; the pin step sets its pending form as soon as it admits a pin, so an exit after the pin step, the collision guard, keeps it; and the block after the rollout switch derives either form again, or unsets it. `GatesBypassed` is owned and not sticky (`ownedTypes`, `internal/controller/conditions.go`), derived on every pass that enforces the gate from what that pass reads — the spec, status, the content of the spec's env sources and, for the pending form, the suite, read uncached by `pinRule` — never from a stored judgement or condition, and not asserted on a pass that does not enforce the gate. It is withdrawn on the first pass on which it no longer derives: the pin removed, or changed to a digest neither admitted nor serving without a `Passed` verdict; a `Passed` verdict recorded for the serving pinned revision; the pin refused; or a pending pin that stops resolving. The pending form is also withdrawn on an exit before the pin step — the run namespace unavailable, or a source of the current spec unresolved — and comes back on the next pass that reaches it. *(A22.16 decided (b): a serving form of `GatesBypassed` is kept while the gate is enforced and `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict for the serving revision, pinned or not, derived from status alone; until a follow-up amendment and code PR build it under #133, `GatesBypassed` is withdrawn on the first pass without the pin, and once the pin goes nothing names a serving revision that has no `Passed` verdict unless the spec mints it, when the promotion branch reads `GatesPassed=False/ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated`)* *(A25.1, A25.2, A25.7 and A25.9, proposed; adds: while the gate is enforced, `status.activeRevisionDigest` is set, and no `Passed` verdict is recorded for it — in `status.eval`, or under A25.4 (b1) or (b2) in `status.eval.passed[]` — a condition says so, pinned or not: under A25.2 (b), `ActiveRevisionPassUnrecorded=True`, under A25.7 (a) reason `NoPassedVerdictRecorded`; under A25.2 (a), a serving form of `GatesBypassed` that outlives the pin, the pending form carried instead beside an admitted pin not yet active. It is derived in `assessGates` and after the rollout switch, from the spec and status alone, owned and not sticky, and withdrawn only when it no longer derives: a pin removed, refused or changed does not withdraw it. It moves no `Ready`, `Degraded` or phase. Its message names only the active revision, "is the active revision", and no `status.eval` pair, so a run start or a case record leaves the Agent reconciler's status write a no-op. It also fires for a revision active before the gate was added, as decided (A25.8))* The Agent reconciler sets every other `GatesPassed` reason below inside the rollout switch, in the branch the pass takes. *(A4, from the critique's m3.)* Four reasons are observed only by the eval controller. Two come from its run-start fetch: `EvalBindingUnsupported`, and `EvalAwaitingCard` when the fetched card does not match the recorded digest. The third is `EvalWaitingForAuth` for clause 5's hold on an otherwise exempt `Lock` (A11). The fourth is `EvalCandidateUnaddressable`, when a pass that would start a run or claim a case cannot derive the candidate's address (step 3; A24.6, built by PR #142). It writes them to `status.eval.blocked`, and the Agent reconciler's gate branch sets `GatesPassed` from that field, only while `status.eval.blockedFor` matches the current state (A12). `EvalRecordNotKept` cannot travel that way, because a stale CRD prunes `blocked` too, so the Agent reconciler derives it from the CRD's schema (step 11). *(A5, from the re-critique's N3.)* **`EvalRecordNotKept` and `EvalRecordUnverifiable` come first** ("The gate branch's order", below). The gate branch derives them from the CRD's schema before it reads any of `status.eval`, `blocked` and `blockedFor` included. A stale CRD prunes both, so without that precedence the exempt state would report `EvalWaitingForAuth`, naming a clause-5 evaluation that is happening, for a cause that is really the pruned CRD. *(A13, from the tenth critique's m2.)*

For a revision the gate evaluates:

| State | `GatesPassed`, and the branch that sets it | Phase | `Progressing` | `Ready`: an earlier revision serving / none serving |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| no gate declared | `GatesSkipped=True/NoGatesDeclared`, in `assessGates`, as today. `GatesPassed` is not set on an Agent that never had it. On one that had it, because its gate was removed (Q9), it flips to `False/NoGatesDeclared`, naming the revision now serving and any verdict the removal overrode. Otherwise the sticky type would keep `EvalFailed` beside a revision it no longer describes (A5, from the re-critique's n2) | as today | as today | as today |
| EvalSuite CRD absent | not set; `GatesSkipped=True/EvalSuiteCRDAbsent`, in `assessGates`, as today | as today | as today | as today |
| the candidate is not available, whether or not a run exists, and no reason the gate branch ranks above `EvalPaused` holds ("The gate branch's order") | `False/EvalPaused`, naming k of N, or that no run has started, and, beside a matched fourth reason, that a run starts or resumes only once the candidate's address can be derived, as it names a matched hold or run-start refusal (A24.6), in the not-ready branches, which report whatever the gate branch answers for the candidate | as those branches set it today | as today | as today |
| an I1 hold stands | `False/EvalHeldForAuth`, in the I1 branch | `Held` | `True/AuthInputUncompilable`, as today | `True/Available` / cannot arise: I1 applies only to a served Agent |
| a `Create` or `Lock` is recorded on the Agent, where a refused `Adopt` does not count and neither does a J2 or K2 `Lock` that meets all five exemption clauses; or, for a `Lock` that meets the first four clauses, `status.eval.blocked` records clause 5's hold, or `blockedFor` does not match the current state | `False/EvalWaitingForAuth`, naming the transaction's kind and stage, and when its deadline passed if it has, in the gate branch. For a transaction that is not exempt, the message also says why: the first of the exemption's clauses 1 to 4 that fails, or that a `Create` is recorded. For clause 5's hold, the message also carries `blocked.message`, which names the policy or the failed read that holds. When `blockedFor` does not match, it says clause 5 has not yet been evaluated for this `Lock` (A12, from the ninth critique's m3). For a missing-policy `Lock` at `ProbingAfter` where the route does not carry exactly one `backendRef`, or the revision its single `backendRef` names has no recorded card digest, **and `status.cards[]` records none for `status.activeRevision` either** — the third clause because in either other case the exits are the wrong remedy to name, not because a carded active revision ends the state ("When it names the exits") — the message also names the exits. An administrator of the run namespace deletes `<agent>-serving`, with the outage that follows. Or, for a transient card failure, the spec is reverted to the revision the route names, which a route naming no single revision does not admit. Beside them it says the state also ends with nobody acting once a pass reaches the re-point with `status.activeRevision`'s card recorded, or the candidate promotes on a verdict written before the `Lock` began or in flight when it began (A8, corrected by A9, and rewritten by A19 for design 03 A77 and A78: this cell kept the plural-`backendRef` reading A77 abolished, read nothing about `status.activeRevision`, and so named an administrator's outage for a state the re-point was about to end) | as design 03's transaction sets it: a held, re-created or past-deadline transaction withholds `Ready` and sets `Degraded` (`withholdReady`) | `True/AwaitingGates` | as design 03's transaction sets it. No transaction runs before a revision serves (A6, from the third critique's m5) |
| the installed Agent CRD lacks the new `status.eval` fields | `False/EvalRecordNotKept`, naming the `kubectl apply` remedy, in the gate branch | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/EvalRecordNotKept` |
| the Agent CRD cannot be read, and no earlier answer is cached | `False/EvalRecordUnverifiable`, naming the error, in the gate branch | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/EvalRecordUnverifiable` |
| the suite does not exist | `False/EvalSuiteNotFound`, in the gate branch | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/AwaitingGates` |
| the suite cannot be read (an API error) | `False/EvalSuiteUnreadable`, in the gate branch | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/AwaitingGates` |
| no card recorded for the candidate's digest, or `status.eval.blocked` says the run-start card fetch did not match the recorded digest, or did not succeed, which its message distinguishes (step 2, A15) | `False/EvalAwaitingCard`, in the gate branch | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/AwaitingGates` |
| `status.eval.blocked` says the card has no usable `HTTP+JSON` 1.0 interface | `False/EvalBindingUnsupported`, in the gate branch, from `blocked` | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/AwaitingGates` |
| `status.eval.blocked` says the candidate's address cannot be derived (step 3) | `False/EvalCandidateUnaddressable`, in the gate branch, from `blocked` (A24.6, built by PR #142) | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/AwaitingGates` |
| the pair is in `failed[]` | `False/EvalPairAlreadyFailed`, naming the pair and saying that a suite edit re-opens it, in the gate branch, once `status.eval` no longer holds the pair's `Failed` record; while it does, the `failed` row's `EvalFailed` | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/EvalFailed` |
| running: some suite `id` has no entry in `cases[]` with a recorded outcome (A15) | `False/EvalRunning`, naming the pair and N, in the gate branch. Progress is in `status.eval.cases[]`, so the condition does not change as cases complete. Its message says that `status.eval.cases[]` records the outcomes so far, and asserts nothing about cases being sent, because the eval controller watches no Service, so this row can be shown between a candidate's Service going and the next eval pass, which writes `EvalCandidateUnaddressable` (A24.6) | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/AwaitingGates` |
| failed | `False/EvalFailed`, naming each failed case and its reason, the score against the threshold, and both digests, in the gate branch | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/EvalFailed` |
| passed, with the card still recorded | `True/EvalPassed`, naming both digests and the score, in the promotion branch. It is asserted only when `status.eval` records `Passed` for the revision being promoted, never merely because the promotion branch ran *(A25.5, proposed; under (i), adds: or when `status.eval.passed[]` records a `Passed` pair for it, and then the message names that pair's suite digest and says the score is not kept)* | `Ready` | `False/RolloutComplete` | `True/Available` |
| passed, and the card is no longer recorded | `False/EvalAwaitingCard`, in the gate branch | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/AwaitingGates` |
| a gate declared over a serving revision for which `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict, because the gate was added after it began serving, or because the spec was reverted to a revision that passed after a later candidate's run start replaced `status.eval` | `False/ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated`, naming the serving revision and saying that the next revision will be evaluated. Its message says what `status.eval` records rather than why. It is asserted in the promotion branch, where the `active != desired` guard sends this state (A5, from the re-critique's n2) *(A25.5, proposed; under (i), adds: the revert cause ends where `status.eval.passed[]` records the revision, which then reads `EvalPassed`; under (ii), the message says what `status.eval`'s pair records, not `status.eval`)* | `Ready` | as today | `True/Available` |
| a pinned rollback that Q5 admits | unchanged; `GatesBypassed=True/PinnedRollback`, in one of two forms. The pending form, for a pin admitted on this pass and not yet serving, says the revision will be promoted once available without its eval gate, that the eval controller sends it no case, that it is judged again on each pass, and why it was admitted. The serving form, for a pin naming the revision serving while `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict for it, says it serves by pin and then what the record holds, chosen from a fixed set by `servingRecord` and by nothing else — no run; a run of another revision, which says that whether this one passed its gate, or, when `failed[]` names it nowhere, passed or never ran, is not recorded; a `Failed` verdict for it, naming the suite digest; a run of it with no verdict yet; or its pair with the verdict cleared — with how many pairs `status.eval.failed[]` records for it and against which suite digests, and never that the gate was bypassed or why the revision was not passed. On the one pass that admits and promotes a pin it also carries the admission reason ahead of the record; every later pass, and `assessGates`, give one steady text. A serving pinned revision with a `Passed` verdict reads no `GatesBypassed`. *(A25.5, proposed; under (i), adds: the serving form reads `status.eval.passed[]` too, so a pin to a revision it records reads no serving form, and `GatesPassed` is asserted for it, reversing A22.9's record and hiding a later failure of it (A25.5); under (ii), this row's "while `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict for it" and `servingRecord`'s "is not recorded" both say `status.eval`'s pair. Under A25.2 (a), the serving form also derives with no pin (A25.1))* While the serving form holds, the promotion branch does not assert `GatesPassed`, which keeps describing the revision `status.eval` names (`servingPinBypass`, `servingRecord`, `pendingPinBypass`). A pin that promotes a revision whose run was in flight leaves its `Running` pair standing, because the clear of a superseded record clears only a `Failed` one, so the serving form says of it "a run of it ... with no verdict yet" while nothing will finish that run (measured by a reviewer's probe, unpinned, owed on #115); and on an Agent CRD that predates `verdict` and `suiteDigest` on the pair, the cleared-verdict sentence renders with an empty digest | as the pin resolves today | as today | as today |
| a pin that Q5 refuses | unchanged; the pin is refused through the existing unresolvable-pin path, reason `PinnedRevisionNotEligible`, and the current release is untouched | as today for an unresolvable pin | as today | as today |

`GatesPassed` stays owned and sticky (`internal/controller/conditions.go`): a pass that does not assert it keeps the previous condition. After a promotion it keeps describing the revision `status.eval` names, until the next candidate resets it. *(A25.5, proposed; under (i), adds: except where the promotion branch finds the revision it promotes in `status.eval.passed[]`, when it asserts `EvalPassed` from that record's newest pair for it)* The slice retires the reason `GateControllerUnimplemented`. On Q5's recommended answer, `GatesBypassed` gets its first reason. Every reason above belongs in the one named vocabulary list, with its test (write-spec). That list is `internal/refgen/reasons.yaml`, and `make reference`, and so `make verify`, fails on a reason the code sets with no entry or an entry no code sets. Every reason build steps 5 to 7 added has its entry, and `GateControllerUnimplemented`'s is gone; build step 8 added the pin rule's two, `PinnedRollback` on `GatesBypassed` and `PinnedRevisionNotEligible` on `Degraded`. A failed gate while an earlier revision serves leaves `Ready=True`. The slice adds no alert of its own.

**The gate branch's order.** First match wins. Both states begin with (1) `EvalRecordUnverifiable`, then `EvalRecordNotKept`; (2) `EvalSuiteNotFound`, then `EvalSuiteUnreadable`; and (3) a `Passed` verdict for the pair, which is satisfied with its card recorded and `EvalAwaitingCard` without it. **Outside the exempt state** — no `Create` or `Lock` recorded, a refused `Adopt`, or a recorded `Create` or `Lock` that is not exempt — the order goes on: (4) a `Failed` verdict for the pair, `EvalFailed`, then the pair in `failed[]`, `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`; (5) no card recorded for the candidate, `EvalAwaitingCard`; (6) a recorded `Create` or `Lock` that is not exempt, `EvalWaitingForAuth`; (7) the candidate not available, `EvalPaused`; (8) `blocked`, with its reason, while `blockedFor` matches; (9) `EvalRunning`. **In the exempt state** — a J2 or K2 `Lock` that meets clauses 1 to 4 — it goes on: (4) a `Failed` verdict for the pair, `EvalFailed`, then the pair in `failed[]`, `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`; (5) no card recorded for the candidate, `EvalAwaitingCard`; (6) the candidate not available, `EvalPaused`; (7) `blockedFor` does not match: `EvalWaitingForAuth`, saying that clause 5 has not yet been evaluated for this `Lock`; (8) `blocked`, with its reason; (9) `EvalRunning`. The eval controller computes `blocked` only on a pass on which the candidate has a card and is available, so neither state applies a `blocked` on any other pass. Build step 7 (PR #118) gave `decideGate` (`internal/controller/gate.go`) the order outside the exempt state, and the exempt order as A21.2 stated it; PR #141 gave it the exempt order above (A24.1). Why, outside the exempt state: (1) to (3) are the two orderings this section fixes elsewhere, the schema reasons first (A13) and a written `Passed` verdict above a recorded transaction ("Clause 5 stops new claims only"); (4) comes before (5) and (8) because a failed pair holds whatever card is recorded and whatever `blocked` says, and its two reasons are in the human's order of 2026-10-18 (#111); (5) comes before (8) because the eval controller computes no `blocked` on a pass whose candidate has no card recorded, and before (6) and (7) because such a candidate cannot be evaluated whatever the transaction or its availability does; (6) comes before (7) and (8) because the eval controller computes nothing while that transaction is recorded, so a `blocked` beside it was computed before it began; and (7) comes before (8) as step 8 orders the not-ready branches before the gate, because no pass whose candidate is unavailable computes `blocked`, so one that matches may name a cause that has gone, for as long as the candidate stays away. In the exempt state, (4) comes first for the reason it does outside it, and (5) and (6) come before the derivation because `blockedFor` cannot come to match while no card is recorded or the candidate is away (A21.1 to A21.3, A24.1).

#### How it composes with what exists

- **`candidateRevision` and `activeRevision`.** As above. The candidate is recorded in `status.candidateRevision` and `candidateRevisionDigest` while it is evaluated and while it is held. At most one is in flight, as today.
- **A new Agent, and design 03's `Create`.** `Create` is entered only once a revision serves: while `status.activeRevision` is empty, the operator enters no `-auth` transaction and collects the Agent's routes (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`). So a new gated Agent is evaluated first, promoted on a pass, and only then prepared, given its policy, probed and published. A new Agent whose first revision fails has no route at all. Its time to first answer is the run plus `Create`.
- **A gated promotion while a `Create` is in flight is harmless.** `Create`'s probe is anonymous, against a prepared route with no `backendRefs`, so its `401` never depends on which revision serves. `Publishing` names `status.activeRevision` as it stands at the moment of publishing. *(A4, from the critique's "Verified sound".)*
- **The I1 promotion hold.** It comes before the gate, and no case is sent while it stands. Such a candidate cannot be promoted while the hold stands. The edit that fixes its `-auth` mints a new revision anyway, because `expose` is on the behaviour surface. So a run would be spent on a revision that can never serve.
- **A promotion during a `Lock` (J2, K2, and a missing policy).** The gate adds no ordering. A `Lock` works on the serving route. A J2 or K2 `Lock` keeps that route on the active revision, and a missing-policy `Lock` re-points it at `status.activeRevision` at `ProbingAfter` while that revision has a recorded card digest, and leaves it where it found it while it has none (design 03 A77; below, A9 as narrowed by A19). A held candidate is named by no route, so neither `ProbingBefore` nor `ProbingAfter` can reach it. J2's edit mints a revision, because `expose.a2a.auth` is behaviour surface, and on a gated Agent that revision is evaluated before it can be promoted. The `Lock` does not wait for the verdict, with one exception. A J2 or K2 `Lock` whose serving revision has no recorded card, whose before-state was not observed, and on which no Gateway-level or foreign-traffic hold stands, can finish only through a promotion. So the rule exempts it and lets the eval earn that promotion ("Who runs the eval", A7). Otherwise the verdict waits for the `Lock`: no case is claimed while a `Create` or `Lock` is recorded on the Agent. So at most one outcome, of the case in flight when the `Lock` began, can make the `Lock`'s status writes conflict ("Who runs the eval", A5 and A6). A run already under way pauses at its next unsent case, and resumes once the transaction is gone. A promotion can still happen during a `Lock`, when a verdict was already `Passed` before the transaction began: for example, a missing-policy `Lock` that starts while a passed candidate awaits its promoting pass. What follows holds for a J2 or K2 `Lock`, which re-asserts the route on the promoted revision. A missing-policy `Lock` moves the route too, but only at `ProbingAfter` and only while `status.cards[]` records a digest for `status.activeRevision` (design 03 A77): it then re-points onto the promoted revision and attributes on that revision's card. While that digest is missing it keeps attributing on the revision the route still names, and the route follows the promotion only after the `Lock` reaches `Served` and `reconcileServed` re-asserts it (A8, from the fifth critique's S1, narrowed by A19). That `Lock` is often entered in the promoting pass itself, after the switch, because `reconcileServed` checks the policy before it would re-ensure the route. If **neither** the revision the route still names **nor** `status.activeRevision` has a recorded card, the `Lock` never reaches `Served`. It is then the wedge under "Who runs the eval", with the same exits (A9, from the sixth critique's M1, narrowed by A19). A pass that lands while a `Lock` is short of `Served` is a promotion during a `Lock`, which design 03 §3.3.3 already handles: the promotion clears `beforeObserved` and `beforeRevision`, and a later `401` is credited only on the card digest `status.cards[]` records for the new revision. The fourth promotion condition guarantees that digest is recorded in the pass that promotes. It can still be cleared by a failed drift re-read afterwards. The `Lock` then fails closed, into `AuthLockUnverified`, as design 03 §3.3.3 specifies for an unattributable `401`. *(Corrected by A4, from the critique's M6. A3 said the run-start precondition guaranteed it, and it does not.)*
- **A Gateway-level auth policy (design 03 A75), and the gateway itself.** Eval requests do not cross the gateway. The candidate's own egress does: its tool calls and LLM calls go to `ASSAYD_GATEWAY_URL`. So a Gateway-level policy, a policy on the MCP listener, or a gateway fault in flight can fail a case whose answer depends on a tool. Neither digest covers that state, and on Q3's recommended answer the resulting `Failed` verdict is final for the pair. That is defensible, because the candidate is evaluated under the egress policy it would serve under. But it means the verdict depends on cluster state outside its binding, and Q3 carries the cost. A new Agent that passes its gate and then meets a Gateway-level hold on `Create` is promoted and stays unpublished, reason `GatewayAuthPolicy`, as today. *(Corrected by A4, from the critique's M5. A3 said a Gateway-level policy changed no case's outcome.)*
- **`GatesSkipped` and `GatesPassed`.** As the table says. The discovery rule stays: a failed discovery reads as "installed", so a gated Agent holds rather than promoting ungated.
- **Supersession.** A new generation abandons the run. Outcomes recorded for the old digest are dropped with it, and the new candidate starts at its first case. The old candidate is appended to `status.supersededCandidates`, as today. A failed pair stays in `failed[]` (step 7).
- **A pinned rollback, and a removed gate.** Q5 and Q9, above under "Promotion". Build step 8 built Q5 (a) (`internal/controller/pinrule.go`): on a gated Agent with the EvalSuite CRD installed, a pinned rollback Q5 admits is promoted with no run, under `GatesBypassed=True/PinnedRollback`, and one it refuses is reported through the unresolvable-pin path, `Degraded=True/PinnedRevisionNotEligible`. Between build steps 6 and 8 a pinned rollback on a gated Agent was instead evaluated like a candidate, which the review mutations on PR #119 that drop the eval controller's pinned-rollback predicate (M10, N10, O9) reproduce on the step-8 code, standing in for the code before it: `TestAPinnedRollbackIsSentNoCase`, which asserts that no case is sent and no run is started, failed on each, and the records say only that. That the run then passed rests on #82's step-8 comment, which names no test. The sentence that follows describes the code when the slice was approved, before build step 6, and the one after it the critique's check of that reading. As this design reads today's code, a pinned rollback holds with no exit whenever a gate is declared and the EvalSuite CRD is installed: the gate branch has no pin input, and the I1 hold does (`authHoldsPromotion`). The critique confirmed that reading by reading, not by running.
- **Card drift on a held candidate.** No new run. A card change without a spec change mints no revision (design 02 §3.4), and the verdict covers the revision's behaviour. A drift re-read that fails clears the digest, and the fourth condition then holds promotion until a re-read succeeds.

#### What the verdict proves, and what it grants

- **It proves** that the candidate container, at the digest named, answered the suite's cases as recorded, from inside the cluster, at the time recorded, under the gateway state its own egress met at that time.
- **It does not prove the production path.** Eval requests do not cross the gateway, the route's `-auth`, or any policy on the route. §1's promise, that a revision passes its EvalSuite "through the production path", is not kept by the slice (Q1).
- **The candidate's own egress is real.** Every revision gets `ASSAYD_GATEWAY_URL` (design 02 §3.2).
