A tool call the candidate makes while answering a case goes through the gateway to a real tool, with real side effects, and any LLM spend is real. No receipt records it. A suite must be written as if every case runs in production, because its tool calls do.
- **Eval traffic is distinguishable, and the gate does not defend against a dishonest image author.** Nothing marks an eval request. It still differs from production traffic: it comes from the operator's Pod, with no credential, and not through the gateway. An image written to cheat can answer the eval one way and production another. The gate catches regressions by honest authors. *(A4, from the critique's m9.)*
- **EvalSuite write is unauthenticated invocation of the held candidate.** Anyone who can write an EvalSuite in the Agent's namespace can make the operator send the held candidate up to 20 messages of their choosing per suite edit. Those messages skip the API key and the group check that `<agent>-auth` requires of every caller through the route. They drive the candidate's real tools and LLM spend, with no budget, and each case reads one bit back. Under Q2's recommended answer a failed candidate is held indefinitely, so it is a standing target. The same writer can also pass a revision by weakening its suite. Q6 decides who may hold that grant. *(A4, from the critique's M8. A3 said the messages reach "that author's own agent", which nothing enforced.)*
- **The operator becomes a second way in, at the network layer.** Design 07 A5.4 admits the operator's Pods to agent Pods for the card fetch, and says the eval runner "arrives through the gateway and needs no rule of its own". The slice falsifies that premise. A NetworkPolicy works at L4, so the card-fetch allow rule now also carries arbitrary A2A tasks. Design 07 A5.4's amendment is owed (below).
- **The operator becomes an A2A client.** It sends only to a Service it owns, at an address it reads from the API server, never to an address from a card or a suite, and it follows no redirect (step 4). `test/responder` records that the A2A client is design 08's, as `assayd invoke`. The slice adds a second one, inside the operator, limited to one method (Q1).

#### In and out

| In the slice | Where |
|---|---|
| The `EvalSuite` kind, in the subset above: `dataset.cases`, `metrics` with `task_completion` only, `caseTimeoutSeconds`, and their admission rules, including the echo refusal | above |
| `spec.gates` with at most one entry, resolved in the Agent's namespace | above, Q4 |
| An eval controller in the operator's manager, with two workers, writing only `status.eval`, reading Agents live and suites uncached, claiming each case before it sends it, reading back what it writes, claiming nothing while a `Create` or `Lock` is recorded on the Agent, except a J2 or K2 `Lock` that can finish only through a promotion and is under no Gateway-level or foreign-traffic hold, read with design 03's own functions, and claiming, sending and recording synchronously on a context that shutdown does not cancel | above, Q1, Q11, Q12 |
| The Agent reconciler's check that the installed Agent CRD carries the new `status.eval` fields, with `EvalRecordNotKept` and `EvalRecordUnverifiable` | above, step 11 |
| One A2A 1.0 `SendMessage` per case, over `HTTP+JSON`, to the candidate's Service at a path chosen once per run, with no redirect followed, one case per pass, resumable | above |
| The mechanical case outcome, the suite digest, the integer threshold rule, and the bounded record of failed pairs | above, Q3 |
| Promotion only on four conditions, including a recorded card; wholesale, onto the route's one `backendRef` | above, Q10 |
| A failed candidate held, not deleted; an unavailable candidate paused | above, Q2 |
| A pin to an ineligible digest refused, on a gated Agent only | above, Q5 |
| `status.eval`'s eight new fields, `blockedFor` included, the `GatesPassed` reasons in the table, and `GatesBypassed=PinnedRollback` if Q5 says so | above |

| Out of the slice: specified, not approved | Why it is not in the slice |
|---|---|
| Canary steps, weighted shifts, SLO-judged progression (D1's canary half), phase `Canary` | ADR-0030 step 4 is blue/green before canaries. The serving route has one `backendRef` |
| The `evalrunner/v1` slot, DeepEval, Inspect, `byo`, runner Jobs and images, the runner trust bar, `evalreport/v1` and the contracts ledger (§8) | the slice's runner is in the operator (Q1) |
| The eval Job's own SVID, the candidate header route, its compiled temporary grant, and the eval principal's posture (§4 step 2, §6) | design 06's identity is not built, and design 03 §1.1 puts the candidate header route outside its approved slice |
| Evaluation through the gateway | the same; Q1 |
| The dataset builder (§5): `goldenSetRef`, ontology-derived seeds, session-derived cases, content-addressed dataset artifacts, `DatasetReady` | cases are inline. Design 12 is a hypothesis under ADR-0030, and design 17 is not built |
| `tool_correctness`, `faithfulness_to_kg`, `cost_regression`, judged metrics and `custom_geval`, `gate.minScore` and weighted means, non-blocking suites (§6, §7) | each needs receipts (design 04), the knowledge graph (designs 01, 13), a spend aggregate (design 04) or a judge, and the slice has none of them. With one metric, §7's mean is that metric |
| The judge, `JudgeUnavailable`, the eval `budget` and its exhaustion (§9) | the slice calls no judge and spends nothing itself. The candidate's own spend under eval is real, and the gate does not limit it (above) |
| The `EvalRun` kind, Postgres result rows, report artifacts, PR annotations, the `eval.completed` event, eval receipts | the verdict lives in Agent status |
| Nightly runs against the active revision (§4 step 6) | no trend consumer exists (design 20) |
| An EvalSuite watch, and any run-level deadline | a suite edit is noticed within 5 minutes (above). With no deadline, a paused run holds, which is fail-closed, and a run slowed by k slow tenants takes O(k) longer (A5, from the re-critique's n3) |
| `EvalSuite.spec.target` | the Agent's reference is the only binding (Q8) |
| Model targets (A1) | design 25 is a hypothesis under ADR-0030 |
| "At least one gate in prod", and `GatesBypassed=DevProfile` | nothing enforces either (design 02 §5), and the slice does not add them |
| More than one gate per Agent | Q4 |
| §6's flake policy: infra-failed cases retried once | Q3 |
| Re-evaluating the serving revision when a gate is added or its suite changes | `gates` is policy surface (above, "Promotion") |

**What the slice depends on.** Every item was owed when the slice was approved. Each item built since says so. Design 02's text carries none of design 02's items yet: the human decided on 2026-10-13 to build them under §1.1 and track design 02's text as #99.

- **Design 02**:
  - `status.eval`'s `suiteDigest`, `endpointPath`, `cardDigest`, `blocked`, `blockedFor`, `verdict`, `cases[]` (with `claim` and the `Sending` outcome) and `failed[]`. `blockedFor` carries the whole fail-closed reading, so an install without it would report `EvalWaitingForAuth` for ever (A13, from the tenth critique's MAJOR 1). Built in build step 1, PR #103;
  - the Agent reconciler's check that the installed Agent CRD carries those fields, with `GatesPassed=False/EvalRecordNotKept` and `EvalRecordUnverifiable`, and `apiextensions.k8s.io/v1` registered in the operator's scheme for it (step 11). Built in build step 5, PR #110;
  - `spec.gates` capped at one entry by CEL, with a refusal that names the fix (Q4). Built in build step 1, PR #103;
  - the gate branch of the rollout step answering from the four promotion conditions, in place of today's hold with no exit, with every `GatesPassed` reason set inside the switch. Built in build steps 5 and 6, PRs #110 and #112;
  - **a gated candidate needs a recorded card to promote** (Q10). This is new enforcement: before it a card failure never withheld traffic, and on an ungated Agent it still does not. Built in build step 5, PR #110;
  - the pin rule: a pin to an ineligible digest is refused (Q5). Built in build step 8, PR #119;
  - the card fetch refusing redirects (§3.4), in a separate change. Built by PR #38, outside this slice (step 4, #101);
  - the `GateRef` field comment in `api/v1alpha1/agent_types.go`, which says "At least one is required in prod". Nothing enforces that bound, as design 02 §5 already says. Built in build step 1, PR #103: the comment now says nothing requires a gate;
  - the comment on `authHoldsPromotion` that a pin "selects a revision that already served", which nothing enforces (`resolveReleasePin`). Built in build step 6, PR #112: the comment, now on `i1Hold` in `sendpredicates.go`, says it is not enforced;
  - §3.3's rollout steps 3 to 5, which describe a candidate-only route, canary steps and deletion on a fail, amended to name this slice. Design 02 is itself not approved, and this design does not edit it.
- **Design 07**:
  - **A5.4's amendment**: the operator's ingress to agent Pods carries eval tasks as well as the card fetch, and "the eval runner arrives through the gateway" is no longer the slice's premise;
  - the admission policy Q6 recommends, which the human chose, (c), on 2026-09-14 ("Decisions of the human", below). Built in build step 9, PR #125: `assayd-evalsuite-writers`, in `charts/assayd/templates/admission.yaml`;
  - a chart test that keeps the operator's `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` at 25 s or more, so a case in flight at shutdown can finish and record (step 10). Built in build step 2, PR #104;
  - the chart carrying the `EvalSuite` CRD (Q7). Built in build step 6, PR #112: `charts/assayd/crds/assayd.dev_evalsuites.yaml`, copied from `config/crd/` by `make verify`;
  - the Agent CRD's new status fields applied on upgrade, beside the EvalSuite CRD, because `helm upgrade` never updates `crds/`. Until they are applied, a gated Agent holds under `EvalRecordNotKept` (step 11). Still owed: the e2e harness applies them, and nothing does on a real `helm upgrade`; tracked as #114;
  - RBAC for `get` on `evalsuites`, and nothing more: suites are read uncached, by `get` alone, and no informer lists or watches them (A5, from the re-critique's n6). The eval controller's merge patch needs no new verb: the operator's role already grants `get, update, patch` on `agents/status` (`internal/controller/agent_controller.go`). Built in build step 1, PR #103;
  - the e2e harness applying the CRD, because `helm upgrade` never updates `crds/`. Built in build step 6, PR #112: the harness applies the whole of `charts/assayd/crds/`, which now holds the CRD;
  - two env knobs on `test/responder`: one that changes its answer's prefix, and one echoed into the task's metadata, so the e2e can tell a failing revision and two passing ones apart. Built in build step 10, PR #130: `ANSWER_PREFIX`, read verbatim with `os.LookupEnv`, replaces the answer's `echo: ` prefix, and only an unset variable keeps `echo: `, so an empty value is an empty prefix; `ANSWER_MARKER` is reported in the task's metadata as `marker` only when non-empty, and never in the answer. With neither set the responder answers as before (`test/responder/main.go`, pinned by `TestTheAnswerPrefixAndTheMarkerAreKnobs`, which, like the rest of that package's tests, runs in neither `make test` nor CI, #129).
- **This design**: the `EvalSuite` Go type and its generated CRD; the eval controller; `gatewayAuthPolicies` and `foreignTrafficPolicies` moved, unchanged, to where both controllers can call them (A10), with the two additive error fields build step 7 later gave `gatewayAuth` ("What clause 5 costs"); `GracefulShutdownTimeout` set explicitly to 30 s in `cmd/operator/main.go`, with a unit test (A7); **a field on the eval controller for the requeue intervals above**, on the pattern `AuthTransactionDeadline` already sets — a constant in the code, and `AgentReconciler.AuthDeadline` beside it "exists for tests" (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`) — where the zero value resolves to `AuthProbeInterval`, `CardRetryInterval` and `CardDriftInterval` on the branches that return them, with a unit test of that resolution. Two owed rows shorten one of those intervals — the suite edit under a held candidate, and the read-back — and each interval is a Go `const` that no test can reach: without the field those two would have to wait five real minutes or not exist (A15, from the eleventh critique's MAJOR 2; the count corrected by A17, from the twelfth critique's m1, which A15's own re-mechanisation of the two card rows had left stale); the operator's A2A client, with no redirects and with an injection seam like `CardClient` and `AuthProbe`, so envtest can stand in for the candidate; and the tests below. Built: the `EvalSuite` type and CRD in build step 1; `GracefulShutdownTimeout` in build step 2; the A2A client, `internal/evalclient`, whose seam is `Client.HTTP`, in build step 3; the move onto `gatewayPolicyReads` in build step 4; the eval controller and its requeue field, `EvalReconciler.RequeueAfter`, in build step 6; and the exemption in build step 7. The inventory records which tests below are built.
- **Design 03**: nothing. Nothing in this section changes design 03. The slice calls two of design 03's reads, `gatewayAuthPolicies` and `foreignTrafficPolicies`, unchanged but for two fields of `gatewayAuth` and the two assignments in `gatewayAuthPolicies` that fill them, which build step 7 added for clause 5's hold and which design 03's readings do not read. Moving them to where the eval controller can call them is a move, with those two additive fields, and no change to what design 03 reads (A10, A21.14). The slice relies on §3.3.3's rule for a promotion during a J2 or K2 `Lock`, which re-asserts the route, and on the I1 hold, both built by design 03's slice PRs (A71, A72). **It now relies on the same rule for a missing-policy `Lock` too**, in the conditional form design 03 A77 gives it — the route re-pointed at `status.activeRevision` at `ProbingAfter`, while `status.cards[]` records a digest for that revision — and on A77's route gate, which stops any `Lock` taking a **`ProbingAfter`** answer until the assayd Gateway reports `Accepted` and `ResolvedRefs` on the route at its current generation. It is that stage's gate and no other: J2's and K2's single `ProbingBefore` request is not gated, and design 03 A78 narrowed eight places that said otherwise. Neither is owed: the human decided the re-point on 2026-09-15 (ADR-0034 Amendment 6), and PR #48 implemented A77 and A78. Both are amendments to design 03's own approved first slice, taken through design 03's process, which is the route Q13 (a) named, so this adds no new dependency on an unapproved design. The residue the slice still carries is stated under "Who runs the eval": neither the route's revision nor `status.activeRevision` carded. *(Corrected by A19. A8 wrote "It does not rely on that rule for a missing-policy `Lock`, whose route does not follow promotion", and A9 added that removing the wedge would take a design 03 amendment "outside this slice".)*

#### What design 16 says that the slice cannot keep

- **"Candidate deleted" on a fail** (§4 step 5; design 02 §3.3, rollout step 5) cannot be implemented as written. The reconcile is level-triggered, and the desired spec still names the deleted revision, so the next pass re-creates the workload and runs the eval again. The slice holds the candidate instead (Q2).
- **D2's "rerun-shopping structurally impossible"** does not follow from binding digests. Binding makes a rerun attributable, not impossible: §6's own flake policy retries infra-failed cases, and nothing in this design stops a second run on the same digests. ADR-0006 names retry-until-pass as a bias amplifier, and says handling it is unbuilt. The slice does not make the claim true either. Its record of failed pairs refuses an automatic re-run and an edit-free re-run of the same pair, for the last 10 failed pairs. A re-run still follows any visible edit of the suite. *(Corrected by A4, from the critique's M1. A3 said the slice made the claim true.)*
- **"Through the production path"** (§1) and **"the gate controller never proxies eval traffic"** (§4 step 2) both assume the candidate header route and a per-run identity, and neither exists. On Q1's recommended answer, the operator is itself the source of eval traffic.
- **Two references join a suite and an Agent**: `EvalSuite.spec.target.agentRef` (§3), and the Agent's `spec.gates[].evalSuiteRef` (design 02). Nothing says what happens when they disagree (Q8).
- **`threshold: 0.85` is a float** (§3), and controller-gen refuses float fields unless `allowDangerousTypes` is set. The slice uses a decimal string.
- **§10's first sentence** still gives the eval Job "the candidate-route credential (gate-controller SVID)". That is the identity model r1's MAJOR withdrew, and r2 asked for the sentence to be struck before ADR-0024 (`reviews/16-review.md`, R2-a). It is still there. It is outside the slice, and is recorded here rather than edited.

#### Decisions of the human (2026-09-14)

The human approved the first slice on 2026-09-14, answering each question as recommended:

| Question | Decision |
|---|---|
| Q1, who sends eval requests | (a′): a separate eval controller in the operator's manager |
| Q2, what a failed verdict does | (a): hold the candidate at zero traffic |
| Q3, what stops a failed pair being re-run | (a): the bounded record of failed pairs |
| Q4, one gate per Agent | (a): a CEL cap at admission |
| Q5, which pinned rollback is refused | (a): on a gated Agent, refuse the current candidate and a failure against the current suite |
| Q6, who may write an EvalSuite | (c): an admission policy that admits a suite write only from a principal who may update Agents |
| Q7, does the chart install the EvalSuite CRD | (a): in `crds/` |
| Q8, the suite's schema and binding | (a): the strict subset of §3 |
| Q9, does removing `spec.gates` release a held candidate | (a): yes |
| Q10, does a gated candidate need a recorded card to promote | (a): yes |
| Q11, a case claimed and never recorded | (a): at most once, recorded as `Interrupted` |
| Q12, does the eval keep waiting past a transaction's deadline | (a): yes, keep waiting |
| Q13, the missing-policy wedge | (a): accept it in this slice, with its exits |

Each question below keeps its text and its alternatives, as the record of what was decided against.

- **Q1 — Who sends eval requests, and from where?**
  - (a) The Agent reconciler itself, to the candidate's Service, one case per pass. No new controller. The costs, which A3 understated: a case holds the operator's only reconcile worker for up to 10 s, on top of that pass's card fetch (5 s) and `-auth` probe (5 s). Any tenant can trigger it, with an image that sleeps on `POST /message:send`, or by re-digesting a suite against a held candidate. With k such Agents, every other Agent's pass waits about k × 10 s, and another tenant's `Lock` can miss its 4-minute deadline and page. It also contradicts the rule the card fetch already follows, that one agent must not delay every other agent's reconcile. Choosing (a) would need a global bound on eval concurrency, and it would still take a worker away from Agents.
  - (a′) A separate eval controller in the same manager, with its own queue and two workers, writing only `status.eval` by merge patch, while the Agent reconciler only reads the verdict. It adds no pod, no image, no ServiceAccount and no verb, because the role already grants `patch` on `agents/status`; it needs only read on `evalsuites`, which every option needs. A slow tenant delays only other evals, by an amount that grows with the number of slow tenants, and never another Agent's reconcile, `Create` or `Lock`. On the evaluated Agent itself, the eval waits for a `Create` or `Lock` (above). The costs: two writers of one status object, kept apart by field ownership and optimistic locking (above); and the operator still becomes an A2A client, with no gateway on the path.
  - (b) A Job in the run namespace, to the candidate's Service. The operator holds nothing while a case runs, and cases can run longer than 10 s. The costs: a runner image to build, sign and pin; Job RBAC; a pod in a namespace where the operator is today the only pod author (design 02 §3.5's `pods-by` label); and a new ingress allow rule.
  - (c) Through the gateway, on design 03's candidate header route. This keeps §1's promise. The costs: design 03 work outside its approved slice, and an eval principal, which needs design 06 or a key the operator holds.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a′).** It removes (a)'s cross-tenant starvation at the cost of a second controller in a process that already runs one, and keeps the smallest real gate. (c) is the next step, once design 03's candidate route is approved. *(Changed by A4, from the critique's M2. A3 recommended (a).)*
- **Q2 — What does a failed verdict do to the candidate?** (a) Hold it at zero traffic, with its workload kept for inspection, until a new generation or a new suite digest. (b) Scale it to zero replicas, and keep its material. (c) Delete it, as §4 step 5 says.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a).** (c) loops, as above. (b) saves capacity, but a candidate at zero replicas cannot be inspected, and it needs a scale rule the rollout step does not have. (a)'s cost: the held candidate stays a target for Q6's invocation grant, and for a pin (Q5).
- **Q3 — What stops a failed pair from being re-run?**
  - (a) A bounded record: `status.eval.failed[]` holds the last 10 failed pairs, and a recorded pair never starts a run again. This refuses the automatic re-run, and the edit-free one: reverting a failed revision B and re-applying it.
  - (b) (a), plus §6's flake policy: a case that failed as `Unreachable` is retried once before the verdict, and only `Mismatch`, `TaskNotCompleted` or `ProtocolError` is final. `Interrupted` is never retried under (b), because the candidate may have received it (A6, from the third critique's m3).
  - (c) An explicit re-run field on the Agent.
  - (d) No record. Only the automatic re-run is refused, and A→B→A→B re-runs B.
  - What none of them stops, stated plainly: **a re-run always follows a visible edit of the spec or the suite, and nothing prevents retry-until-pass by edits.** A one-character change to the suite gives a new pair. So does a later failure that evicts the pair from the record.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a).** ADR-0006 names retry-until-pass as the one property that distinguishes this gate, and says handling it is unbuilt. (a) makes every re-run a visible edit, which is the most the slice can promise. The known costs: a network blip, or a Gateway-level policy or gateway fault that fails a tool call in the candidate's own egress, fails a good revision for that pair. Its owner must then edit the suite to re-run it. (b) is the natural next amendment, once non-determinism is handled on purpose. *(Restated by A4, from the critique's M1 and M5. A3 said (a) made a verdict final for its pair, and nothing enforced that.)*
- **Q4 — One gate per Agent: how is it enforced?** (a) CEL caps `spec.gates` at one entry at admission. (b) Accept several, and hold any Agent with more than one, reason `MultipleGatesUnsupported`.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a)**, because an error at apply time names the fix. An Agent stored with several gates before the cap keeps them under CRD validation ratcheting, and holds as in (b).
- **Q5 — Which pinned rollback is evaluated, and which is refused?**
  - (a) **Only while a gate is declared**, a pin is refused when its digest is the current candidate's, or appears in `status.eval.failed[]` paired with the **current** suite digest. The current candidate is the revision the current spec mints, its digest computed on each pass with the content of its env sources, as the rollout step computes it, whether or not a pass has recorded it in `status.candidateRevisionDigest`; so a revision the spec is reverted to and pinned is refused in one write or in two, and a pinned revision that the rollout records as the candidate while it comes up is not its own candidate, because the spec mints another. A pin that names the revision serving is never judged, so it is never refused while it serves: a refusal returns before the rest of the pass, so refusing the revision that serves would roll nothing back and would stop the pass on every reconcile. The refusal goes through the existing unresolvable-pin path, reason `PinnedRevisionNotEligible`: nothing is rolled back, and the current release is untouched. On a gated Agent the pinned revision's retained material is verified first and the rule applied after, both before the collision guard, the supersession and the rollout switch, so a pin whose material is gone reads `ReleasePinUnresolvable` and is never told to edit a suite; on an ungated Agent, or with the EvalSuite CRD absent, a pin's material is verified after the supersession, as before Q5. The refusal sets `Degraded=True/PinnedRevisionNotEligible` and phase `Degraded`, leaves `Ready` as it was, and returns `RequeueAfter: CardDriftInterval`, where the unresolvable-pin path otherwise returns no requeue, because a suite edit can lift it and nothing watches suites; each pass that judges a pin reads the suite uncached, as the gate branch does (`pinRule`). Like every refusal on that path it returns early, so while it stands the serving revision's route, `-auth` and drift convergence stop; a suite edit can refuse a pending pin, so a suite writer can start that freeze, which since Q6 (c) every suite writer could already cause, as an Agent editor, with an unresolvable pin. A pin refused on `failed[]` after the rollout recorded it as the candidate while it came up is still the recorded candidate, and the eval controller holds it, so nothing is evaluated; that pair has failed already. Any other pin promotes, not evaluated, with `GatesBypassed=True`, reason `PinnedRollback`. Nothing about a pin is stored: the rule is derived on every pass from what that pass reads — the spec, status, the content of the spec's env sources, and the suite — and reads no stored judgement and no condition, so a pin admitted and not yet serving is judged again on each pass, and a suite edited to one it failed refuses it before it serves (`pinIneligible`, `pinRule`, `internal/controller/pinrule.go`, built in build step 8, PR #119). A pending pinned rollback is not promoted while a source of the current spec is unresolved, because the pass returns at `EnvSourceUnresolved` before the pin step. On an Agent with no gate, pins behave exactly as today, and no `GatesBypassed` is set. *(Narrowed by A5, from the re-critique's N5. A4 also refused any digest in `supersededCandidates` and any failure against any suite. That refused revisions that had served: one that failed an older suite and later passed, and a served revision that was re-entered as a candidate and superseded.)*
    - What (a) still refuses that served: a revision that is the current candidate again after serving earlier, and a served revision that has since failed the suite now in force. The second is arguably right, because it fails the suite the owner declared. The break-glass for both is to remove `spec.gates` and then pin. Q9's recommended answer allows that, and it is recorded under `GatesPassed=False/NoGatesDeclared`. Of a served revision, (a) refuses only one that is not serving: a pin that names the revision serving is never judged, whatever that revision has since failed. Such a revision serves on, fail-open, and `GatesBypassed`'s serving form states what `status.eval` records of it, its `Failed` verdict, suite digest and `failed[]` pairs included; it does not say whether that suite digest is the suite in force, because the serving form reads status alone and no suite.
    - What (a) still admits that never served: a retained candidate that failed only an older suite, or whose failure was evicted from the 10-entry record. It also admits, unevaluated, three shapes of superseded candidate with no final verdict: one pinned in a write after the one that superseded it, since it is neither the spec's revision nor in `failed[]`; one pinned in the same write that edits the spec away from it (`TestAPinToACandidateTheSpecHasLeftIsAdmitted`); and a refused current candidate once an edit to a referenced ConfigMap or Secret mints a new one with no write to the Agent (`TestASourceContentEditLiftsACandidateRefusal`). The other way round, a pinned revision admitted and coming up is refused if the spec is then edited to mint that same revision, because it is judged again on each pass.
    - **When there is no current suite digest**, because the suite is `EvalSuiteNotFound` or `EvalSuiteUnreadable`, only the current-candidate refusal applies, and a digest in `failed[]` is admitted. Refusing would err closed, but it would let a deleted or unreadable suite block every rollback during an incident, which is when a rollback is needed. Under `GatesSkipped=EvalSuiteCRDAbsent` the rule does not apply at all, because nothing is gated. *(A6, from the third critique's m7.)* Any failure to learn the suite digest counts here — NotFound, any other read error, or a suite that cannot be digested — and the admission's message says the suite could not be read and `failed[]` was not consulted; if the error spans the pass that promotes the pin, the admission is permanent, because a serving pin is never judged again. On an Agent CRD too old to keep `status.eval` (`EvalRecordNotKept`), which prunes `failed[]`, no pin is refused on `failed[]` either; the current-candidate refusal still applies.
  - (a″) (a), plus a bounded record of served digests, `status.eval.served[]`, so that a digest that was ever active is always admitted. That removes (a)'s first false refusal. It also admits a served revision that now fails the suite in force, and it adds a field.
  - (b) Every pin is evaluated like a candidate. That delays a rollback during an incident by a whole run.
  - (c) Only a revision with a recorded pass is served. That needs `status.revisions[]`, which is not on the CRD (ADR-0031 Amendment 1).
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a), and (c) once `status.revisions[]` exists.** Instant rollback is ADR-0019's reason for retaining revisions, and ADR-0031 Amendment 1 already records the gate-evidence check as unbuildable. A3's (a) promoted every pin, and would have served the candidate that just failed, under a reason that calls it a rollback. By this draft's reading, today's code holds a pinned rollback under a declared gate with no exit. That sentence describes the code when the slice was approved: between build steps 6 and 8 a pinned rollback on a gated Agent was evaluated like a candidate, and since build step 8 an admitted pin promotes with no run and a refused one is reported. *(Changed by A4, from the critique's M7.)* A5's (a) keeps instant rollback for every revision that is neither the candidate under evaluation nor a failure against the suite in force, with no new field, and leaves ungated Agents untouched. (a″) is the alternative if the human judges the served-candidate refusal too costly.
- **Q6 — Who may hold the grant an EvalSuite write carries?** An EvalSuite write sends the held candidate up to 20 unauthenticated messages per edit, drives its real tools and LLM spend with no budget, reads one bit back per case, and can pass a revision by weakening the suite (above).
  - (a) Anyone with RBAC on EvalSuites in the namespace, and the slice states the grant.
  - (b) An admission policy reserves EvalSuite writes to named principals, as `assayd-api-keys` reserves key sets.
  - (c) An admission policy admits an EvalSuite create or update only when the requester may also `update` `agents` in that namespace. It is written as a ValidatingAdmissionPolicy using Kubernetes' CEL `authorizer` library. It applies in every namespace, with no `namespaceSelector`, because a suite lives in its Agent's namespace and those carry no label of assayd's to select on. Its one validation is `authorizer.group('assayd.dev').resource('agents').namespace(request.namespace).check('update').allowed()`, with `failurePolicy: Fail` and a `Deny` binding, and its refusal names the namespace and the fix: grant `update` on `agents.assayd.dev` there, namespace-wide, or ask an Agent editor there to apply the suite. It exempts no one, and no clause names the operator: every principal that may `update` Agents namespace-wide passes it, the operator included, whose ClusterRole updates every Agent, and RBAC alone, `get` and nothing else on `evalsuites`, keeps the operator from writing a suite (`TestTheOperatorOnlyReadsEvalSuites`). The subjects of the operator's ClusterRoleBinding are pinned by no test, so a binding of the operator to a built-in role such as `cluster-admin` passes every chart test (#127). Built in build step 9, PR #125 (`charts/assayd/templates/admission.yaml`). An Agent editor already controls the candidate's image, so the grant adds nothing an Agent editor lacks. Deletes stay RBAC's, as they are for the policies design 07 already reserves. Every create and update is judged, whatever it changes — metadata-only edits by users and GitOps tools included — where the other four reservations admit an update that leaves what they reserve alone. The garbage collector, which removes a deletion finalizer by update, holds `update` on every resource and so passes where kube-controller-manager runs with `--use-service-account-credentials`; T61 measures that on envtest with bootstrap RBAC alone, and it is unverified on k3s and on managed control planes, where a failure stalls a foreground delete, failing closed. A principal granted `patch` on Agents and not `update` is refused, and the refusal names the fix. The operator does not fail closed when `assayd-evalsuite-writers` is absent: it neither detects nor reports the missing policy, where design 07 A5.9's reservations make it refuse with `LabelAuthorityAbsent` (#100).
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (c).** It closes the gap between EvalSuite writers and Agent editors at apply time, with one admission policy and no reviewer workflow. (b) needs a principal list someone keeps. (a) leaves a namespace-wide grant of unauthenticated invocation. The re-critique measured (c) on envtest v1.36.2. It used a ValidatingAdmissionPolicy with `authorizer.group('assayd.dev').resource('agents').namespace(request.namespace).check('update').allowed()`, `failurePolicy: Fail` and a `Deny` binding. A user who may only write suites was denied. A user who may `update` Agents only under `resourceNames` was also denied, which errs toward refusal: an editor scoped to named Agents cannot write suites. A user who may `update` Agents namespace-wide was admitted. (c)'s minimum Kubernetes version is the chart's floor, 1.30, where ValidatingAdmissionPolicy is GA and carries the `authorizer` variable. It is unmeasured on 1.30; a CI leg on 1.30 is owed (#134). T62 measures it in CI's e2e jobs, which on PR #125 ran on k3s v1.35.5-k3s1 under k3d and on kind v1.35.0, and it was run locally on k3s v1.33.6, unmutated and with the binding dropped (below). *(A5, from the re-critique's n1.)* *(Reframed by A4, from the critique's M8. A3 framed Q6 as passing a revision by weakening its suite, and recommended (a).)*
- **Q7 — Does the chart install the EvalSuite CRD?** Its presence is design 02's switch for whether declared gates hold. Helm installs `crds/` only at install time, and never templates it: `helm upgrade` neither adds nor updates a CRD there.
  - (a) Ship it in `crds/`. A fresh install enforces a declared gate. An upgraded install keeps `GatesSkipped=EvalSuiteCRDAbsent` until an administrator applies the CRD by hand, as the e2e harness already does for the Agent CRD.
  - (b) Ship it as a separate manifest that an administrator applies to turn the tier on, on fresh installs and upgrades alike.
  - (c) Ship it under `templates/`, behind a value. `helm uninstall` then deletes the CRD, and every EvalSuite with it.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a).** An Agent that declares a gate has asked for one, and a fresh install should honour that. On upgrade, (a) and (b) behave the same, so the choice only matters for fresh installs. *(Costs corrected by A4, from the critique's m5. A3 said every Agent that declared a gate would stop promoting ungated on upgrade, and `helm upgrade` does not install the CRD.)*
- **Q8 — The suite's schema and binding.** (a) The strict subset of §3 above, with a decimal-string `threshold`, no `target`, and the Agent's `evalSuiteRef` as the only binding. (b) A flat schema for the slice alone (`cases`, `minPassed`), migrated when the full schema lands. (c) Keep `target` too, and refuse a run when it does not name the Agent.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a).** (b) buys a simpler first schema at the price of a migration. (c) adds a second reference that can only disagree with the first.
- **Q9 — Does removing `spec.gates` release a held candidate?** *(New in A4, from the critique's M7.)*
  - (a) Yes, as today. `gates` stays on the policy surface. When the removal promotes a candidate whose recorded verdict was `Failed` or `Running`, the message of `GatesSkipped=True/NoGatesDeclared` says so, and names that verdict and its digests.
  - (b) A candidate recorded while a gate was declared must still pass it. Removing the gate affects only revisions minted afterwards. That needs a status marker on the candidate, and the owner can still reach an ungated revision with one more spec edit.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a).** Anyone with `agents/update` can already change the image, so a gate is not a control against the Agent's own editor. (b) costs a status field and buys one extra edit. "At least one gate in prod" is the real control, and it needs the admission binding design 02 §5 records as unbuilt.
- **Q10 — Does a gated candidate need a recorded card to promote?** *(New in A4, from the critique's M6.)*
  - (a) Yes: the fourth promotion condition. This is new enforcement, because today a card failure never withholds traffic. A gated Agent whose card will not validate holds, `EvalAwaitingCard`, until it does.
  - (b) No: promote on the verdict alone. A revision promoted with its card digest cleared leaves a `Lock` on that Agent unable to attribute its `401`, so it fails closed into `AuthLockUnverified`.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a).** The run already needs the card, for its path. Requiring it again at promotion costs nothing a gated Agent had, and makes the `Lock`'s attribution guarantee true when the promotion happens.
- **Q11 — A case that was claimed and never recorded: send it again, or fail it?** *(New in A5, from the re-critique's N1.)* A claim with no outcome means the eval controller stopped between sending and recording. Nobody knows whether the candidate received the request.
  - (a) At most once. The case is recorded as failed, reason `Interrupted`, and never sent again. It counts against the threshold, so a failing verdict it causes is recorded in `failed[]` like any other (Q3), and the owner must edit the suite to re-run.
  - (b) At least once. The case is sent again, with a fresh `messageId`. A candidate that received the first request then runs the task twice, with its tool calls and spend twice.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a).** A case is a real task with real side effects, and the slice does not assume any agent de-duplicates a re-sent message. Only a hard stop leaves a claim with no outcome, because a graceful stop lets the case in flight finish (step 10). A hard stop mid-case is rare, and its cost under (a) is one suite edit. *(Corrected by A6, from the third critique's P2. A5 called any restart rare, and a rolling update is not.)*
- **Q12 — Does the eval keep waiting for a `Create` or `Lock` that is past its deadline?** *(New in A6, from the third critique's P1.)* A transaction past its deadline stays in its stage and keeps working (design 03 §3.3.3). It has already paged, and the Agent is `Degraded`.
  - (a) Yes. No case is claimed while any `Create` or `Lock` is recorded, whatever its deadline. The eval waits, indefinitely if the transaction never finishes, and `EvalWaitingForAuth` says when the deadline passed.
  - (b) No. Once the deadline has passed, cases are claimed again. The stuck transaction then competes with eval patches, and each overlap costs it a pass. A pass during the transaction can then promote a new revision, which clears a `Lock`'s `beforeObserved`.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a).** A transaction past its deadline is an incident that is already paged. Promoting a new revision beside it changes what the gateway serves while the incident is open. (a) is fail-closed and simple. Its cost is that an Agent with a wedged `Create` or `Lock` gets no new revision until an administrator clears the cause. A J2 or K2 `Lock` that cannot attribute its `401` for want of a serving card, and is under no Gateway-level or foreign-traffic hold, is exempt from the wait (A7, narrowed by A8 and A10), so Q12 does not reach it. One held by such a policy is not exempt, before its deadline or after it, because the exemption reads the hold itself and not a condition reason (A10, from the seventh critique's M1). A missing-policy `Lock` whose route names a revision with no recorded card is not exempt. Since design 03 A77 a new revision **does** clear it, once that revision is `status.activeRevision`, its card digest is recorded, **and a pass reaches the re-point at `ProbingAfter`** — Q13's statement of the same behaviour, below, carries that last condition, and this one now matches it. What no new revision clears is the residue in which `status.activeRevision` has no recorded digest either, or in which a foreign `traffic` policy on `<agent>-serving`, a fresh NACK or a `Lock` short of `ProbingAfter` keeps every pass above the re-point. Its policy is in place, and only the proof of its `401` is missing. Its two exits — an administrator deleting `<agent>-serving`, and a revert — stand whichever answer Q12 takes, and since A77 the state also ends with nobody acting once `status.activeRevision`'s card records **and a pass reaches the re-point** (A8 and A9, narrowed by A19). **Q12's decision does not move**: (a) still holds the eval for any recorded `Create` or `Lock`, and the exemption still excludes re-creations. And the rule holds the eval only for a recorded transaction, not for an incident. A served Agent with no transaction recorded, under W1 (design 03 A75) or beside a foreign traffic policy, is evaluated and promoted while that incident is open, as an ungated Agent is (A11, from the eighth critique's m4). *(Corrected by A7, from the fourth critique's R1. A6 said this cost was already true under A75's hold. That was true only because of this slice's own rule: an ungated Agent under A75's hold still promotes.)*
  - **The cost recorded with the decision.** The wait covers recorded transactions, not incidents. A served Agent with no transaction recorded, under W1 (design 03 A75) or beside a foreign traffic policy, is evaluated and promoted while that incident is open, as an ungated Agent is. An Agent with a wedged `Create` or `Lock` gets no new revision until an administrator clears the cause.
- **Q13 — Is the missing-policy wedge accepted in this slice, or is it removed first?** *(New in A9, from the sixth critique's "Noted, not counted". Revised by A10, from the seventh critique's m3 and m4.)* The wedge is stated under "Who runs the eval". On a gated Agent, a missing-policy `Lock` whose route names a revision with no recorded card cannot finish. The Agent pages and is `Degraded`, and no candidate is evaluated, while the route's policy is in place and enforcing, but unproven.
  - **Its trigger is not always two events at once.** A card failure never withholds traffic (design 02 §3.4). So a revision can serve with no card ever recorded, if it was served ungated, served before the gate was added, or promoted by a pin or by removing the gate (Q9). On such an Agent a deleted policy alone produces the wedge, and no revert ends it.
  - **Leaving it standing can cost an outage.** When the route and the active revision are apart, garbage collection deletes R and its Service once three more revisions are minted. Authenticated callers then fail. *(Narrowed by A19, and not removed. Since design 03 A77 a carded `status.activeRevision` ends the apart state, but only once a pass reaches the re-point at `ProbingAfter`; the outage survives wherever none does — a foreign `traffic` policy **on `<agent>-serving`**, which breaks the pass at the foreign check with the stage left at `ProbingAfter`; a fresh NACK; or a `Lock` short of `ProbingAfter`, which one stored **at** `<agent>-auth` produces by failing the intact read — and wherever a failed drift re-read clears that digest first. The enumeration is corrected here by A19's fifth draft: `reviews/16-a19-critique3.md`'s MINOR 3 was applied at "Leaving it standing" and not at this bullet, and §14 recorded it applied at both.)*
  - (a) Accept it in this slice, with its exits stated: an administrator deletes `<agent>-serving`, or, for a transient card failure, the spec is reverted to the route's revision. Put a design 03 amendment through design 03's own process, separately.
  - (b) Amend design 03 first, so that the missing-policy `Lock` re-asserts the route on `status.activeRevision`, as J2's and K2's do, and make this slice's approval depend on that amendment.
  - (c) Amend design 02 §3.4 so that the Agent reconciler also re-reads the card of the revision the serving route names while a `Lock` waits on it. That ends the wedge for a transient card failure on a gated Agent, without touching design 03. It does not help a card that never validates.
  - **Decided on 2026-09-14, as recommended: (a), unchanged.** The administrator's exit works in every case today, including a card that never validates, with no change to another design. The wedge exists without a gate too, so it belongs to design 03 whatever this slice does. (b) is the change that removes it, and it should be made through design 03's own critique rather than from inside this slice. (c) covers only the transient case. The costs (a) accepts are the page, the `Degraded` Agent, no new revision until an administrator acts, and, in the apart case, an outage once three more revisions are minted.
  - **Recorded with the decision: the real fix is design 03's.** The design 03 amendment that makes the missing-policy `Lock` re-assert the route on `status.activeRevision`, as J2's and K2's do, remains the fix that removes the wedge. It goes through design 03's own process, and this decision does not approve it.
  - **Since the decision, and not a change to it (A19).** That amendment landed as **design 03 A77**: the human decided the re-point on 2026-09-15 (ADR-0034 Amendment 6), it went through design 03's own critique, and PR #48 implemented it with A78 beside it. It is **conditional** — the route is re-pointed only while `status.cards[]` records a digest for `status.activeRevision` — so it narrows the wedge rather than removing it. Two sentences above are therefore the record of what was expected on 2026-09-14 rather than of what shipped: the one calling A77 "the fix that removes the wedge", and this question's own framing, "a missing-policy `Lock` whose route names a revision with no recorded card cannot finish", which after A77 it can, whenever `status.activeRevision` is carded and a pass reaches the re-point. Both keep their text, as question text does. Decision (a) stands unchanged: the wedge is accepted in this slice, with its exits, which are still the administrator's delete and the revert — beside them the state now also ends with nobody acting once `status.activeRevision`'s card records and a pass reaches the re-point. What A19 changes is the description of what (a) accepts, and nothing about Q1 to Q13.

#### Tests the slice owes

Every case carries a mutation that must kill it. A mutation that does not compile is INVALID, not KILLED (AGENTS.md rules 1 and 3). A case whose assertion survives the deletion of its subject proves nothing (rule 1), so each row below names how its state is built when that is not obvious. Which rows are built, and by which test, is `docs/research/design16-slice1-inventory-2026-10.md`'s record; this table stays the specification.

| Layer | Case | The mutation that must kill it |
|---|---|---|
| unit | the outcome table, one case per row, against a recorded response of each shape | accept any task state as completed, so a `TASK_STATE_FAILED` case passes |
| unit | the threshold rule at its boundary: 2 of 3 passes `"0.666"` and fails `"0.667"`; and every (passed, total, threshold) an admitted suite can produce, checked against exact rational arithmetic | `≥` changed to `>`, which the boundary pair does not kill — `TestThresholdRuleAtItsBoundary` passes under it — and the exhaustive check does, at `Passes(1, 1, 1000)`; or a rounding form such as `round(score × 1000) ≥ k`, which the boundary pair kills. A float comparison in place of the integer one is not a mutation this row can name: each of the five float forms #105 item 1 measured — `passed/total ≥ t`, `passed ≥ t × total`, `passed/total × 1000 ≥ k`, a float32 quotient, and the threshold read with `strconv.ParseFloat` as thousandths — agrees with exact arithmetic at every point an admitted suite can produce, so it is an equivalent mutant and no test kills it (A20.29, A20.39) |
| unit | the suite digest: stable under key order and case order; changed by each of a message, an expectation, the threshold and the timeout | leave one of those four out of the digest |
| unit | the request: the `A2A-Version` header, `ROLE_USER`, a fresh `messageId` per case, no `contextId`, and no `configuration` | send a `contextId`; or omit the header |
| unit | the endpoint join: `""`, `/`, `/a2a` and `/a2a/` give `/message:send`, `/message:send`, `/a2a/message:send` and `/a2a/message:send`; a URL with a query, or with a fragment, is `EvalBindingUnsupported`; the card URL's host is never dialled | skip the trailing-slash trim; or dial the card URL's host |
| unit | a redirect: a stub answering `307` to a second stub that counts hits. The second stub sees zero, and the outcome is `ProtocolError` | the default `http.Client`, which follows the redirect |
| unit | a timeout: a stub that sleeps past `caseTimeoutSeconds` yields `Unreachable` within `caseTimeoutSeconds` plus 1 s | no deadline on the request |
| unit | the 1 MiB cap: a 1 MiB + 1 byte body is `ProtocolError`, and at most 1 MiB + 1 byte is read | read the body without a limit |
| unit | admission's echo refusal: a case whose message contains its `contains`, or equals its `equals`, is refused; the minimal example is admitted | delete the rule |
| envtest | a gated Agent's second revision holds, `EvalRunning`, while the first serves; it promotes only after every case has an outcome and the verdict is `Passed` | make the gate branch ignore the verdict, so the candidate promotes before its last case |
| envtest | a failing case holds the candidate with `EvalFailed` naming it; the active revision is unchanged over later passes; a case is never re-sent after its outcome is recorded | count `Unreachable` as a pass; or clear recorded outcomes on each pass |
| envtest | the collision pair: `status.activeRevision` is an unrelated revision A; the desired revision is the malicious member M of `internal/revision/collision_test.go`'s pinned pair, recorded as the candidate; `status.eval` records `Passed` for the safe member S's digest, under the shared name. S is then neither active nor candidate, so the workload collision guard (`collisionAgainstStatus`) does not refuse first. The eval controller is not started in this test, so it cannot start its own run for M and overwrite `status.eval` (A5, from the re-critique's n5). M does not promote (§3's owed case, A2) | compare `status.eval.revision`, the name, in place of the digest |
| envtest | a verdict for a suite digest that no longer matches the suite promotes nothing, and a new run starts | drop the suite-digest check |
| envtest | the card cleared after the last case, as a failed drift re-read clears it: the candidate does not promote, and reads `EvalAwaitingCard` | drop the fourth promotion condition |
| envtest | finality: revision A active; B fails; the spec reverts to A, then re-applies B. The stub sees no case for the second B, which reads `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`. An edit of the suite then starts a run: the stub **holds the first case** while the test reads `status.eval`, which carries `verdict: Running` with no `score` and no `at`; the stub is then released and sees all three of the new suite's cases, and the new verdict is computed from that run's outcomes only. The hold is what makes the empty-`score` window the test's own, as the wedge row below holds C's last case (A17). The second B reads `EvalPairAlreadyFailed` and not `EvalFailed` because the clear of a superseded `Failed` record (step 7) takes the first B's record when the spec reverts to A | no `failed[]` record; a record keyed on the revision digest alone, which the suite edit catches; keep `cases[]` across a suite digest change, which the stub's hit count catches; or leave `score` standing at run start, which the held read catches |
| envtest | the `failed[]` cap: an eleventh failed pair evicts the oldest, and the evicted pair can run again | no cap; or eviction of the newest |
| envtest | a spec edit after the first of three cases abandons the run, and the new candidate starts at its first case | key recorded outcomes on the case id alone |
| envtest | a candidate made unavailable after one of three cases reads `EvalPaused` and is sent nothing; made available again, it is sent the second case and not the first | send cases to an unavailable candidate; or restart the run at the first case |
| envtest | a candidate under an I1 hold is sent no case, and reads `EvalHeldForAuth` | move the gate branch before the I1 hold |
| envtest | a new gated Agent has no `HTTPRoute` while its first revision runs or fails, and gets `Create`'s prepared route only after a pass | have the emitter follow the desired revision in place of the active one |
| envtest | a served API-key Agent whose re-created route is in `Create` when its gated candidate passes: `Create` reaches `Served` and publishes onto the new active revision | publish onto the revision recorded when `Create` started |
| envtest | an operator restarted after two of three cases sends only the third | keep outcomes in memory in place of status |
| envtest | no case is sent before the candidate's card is recorded; a card fetched at run start whose digest differs from the recorded one starts no run; a card with no usable interface reads `EvalBindingUnsupported` | drop the digest match at run start; or drop the card precondition |
| envtest | the two causes of `EvalAwaitingCard`, told apart by their messages: with the candidate's card recorded, a run-start fetch refused at the connection reads `GatesPassed=False/EvalAwaitingCard` whose message names the error class and no digest, while a run-start card that parses and whose digest differs from the recorded one reads the same reason with a message naming the mismatch. Neither starts a run (A17) | one message for both causes, which names a comparison the refused connection never reached; or a distinct reason, which adds vocabulary the table does not carry |
| envtest | `EvalSuiteNotFound` for a missing suite; `EvalSuiteUnreadable` when the API reader returns an error that is not NotFound, injected through the reader | map every read error to `EvalSuiteNotFound` |
| envtest | a suite edited under a held candidate with a final verdict starts a new run within one `CardDriftInterval` (the eval controller's requeue field shortened in the test), with no Agent event | no requeue while a verdict is final |
| envtest | the EvalSuite CRD installed after the manager starts: the manager starts without it, and a gated Agent holds once discovery sees the CRD, with no restart | a static EvalSuite watch. On this controller-runtime that does not fail the manager's start: it fails the manager once its source has waited `CacheSyncTimeout` for the absent kind, so the row shortens that timeout to 2 s and keeps the CRD absent past it (A20.30, measured) |
| envtest | pins, on a gated Agent: a pin to a retained, served revision promotes with `GatesBypassed=PinnedRollback`. B fails suite S1, passes S2 and serves, then C serves, and a pin to B promotes. A pin to the current candidate, and one to a digest that failed the current suite, are each refused, `PinnedRevisionNotEligible`, and the active revision is unchanged. On an Agent with no gate, a pin to the same failed digest promotes, with no `GatesBypassed` | no eligibility check; eligibility keyed on `failed[]` alone, which B's pin catches; or the rule applied to an ungated Agent |
| envtest | removing `spec.gates` while a failed candidate is held promotes it, and `GatesSkipped`'s message names the `Failed` verdict and its digests (Q9 (a)) | a message that does not name the verdict |
| envtest | starvation: a J2 `Lock` on one Agent reaches `Served` inside its deadline while two other Agents' stubs each sleep for `caseTimeoutSeconds` on every case | run the cases in the Agent reconciler |
| envtest | two writers: an eval patch racing an Agent reconciler pass never loses a recorded case outcome | write `status.eval` from the Agent reconciler, which the row kills only with each answer delayed, 300 ms, so that the Agent reconciler's passes land inside a case. Dropping the optimistic lock from the patch is not this row's: against it the mutation is equivalent, because one worker reconciles a given Agent at a time and every read before a write is live (M03b on #82), and the stale-reader row kills it (A20.31, measured) |
| unit | a stale reader: an Agent reader returns a status without case k's outcome after it was recorded; the stub sees case k once | claim nothing and trust the read; or drop the optimistic lock from the claim |
| envtest | the hit count: with the Agent reconciler running beside the eval controller, every case of a 5-case run is sent exactly once, by the stub's hit count; the Agent reconciler's status write is a no-op on each eval patch that only records a case | drop the claim; or make `EvalRunning` name k of N, which the no-op assertion catches |
| envtest | a claim with no outcome, left by a stopped eval controller: the case is recorded `Interrupted` and is never sent again (Q11 (a)) | re-send a claimed case |
| envtest | graceful shutdown: while the stub holds the first gated Agent's case for 2 s, an injected hook holds a second gated Agent's pass after its live read and before its `ctx.Err()` check. The test cancels the manager's context, then releases the hook. The first case records the stub's answer; the second Agent writes no claim; the next manager claims the next case and never re-sends the first. Merely queueing the second Agent would not reliably test the check, because a queued Agent gets no pass once the queue is shut down. A hook placed after the check, between it and the claim, would make a correct implementation claim | send on the reconcile context, which records `Interrupted` or `Unreachable`; or claim on the uncancelled context without the `ctx.Err()` check, which gives the held pass a claim |
| envtest | a handover, built with one manager: while the stub holds the case, the test itself writes `Interrupted` at that case's entry, keeping the nonce, as a new leader would. The stub then answers. The record's first attempt conflicts, its re-read finds `Interrupted`, and it drops, so the case stays `Interrupted` | drop the `outcome == Sending` condition from the record step |
| unit | an ambiguous claim: the claim patch commits and its response is a 5xx; that pass sends nothing, and the next records `Interrupted` | send after a claim whose response was lost |
| chart | the operator's `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` is 25 s or more | set it to 10 |
| unit | the operator's manager options set `GracefulShutdownTimeout` explicitly, at 25 s or more | set it to 10 s |
| unit | the eval controller's requeue field at its zero value resolves to `AuthProbeInterval` on a pass waiting on a transaction or clause 5's hold, to `CardRetryInterval` after a failed run-start fetch, and to `CardDriftInterval` on a paused candidate or a refusal that read a card | resolve every branch to one interval, which the three-way assertion catches |
| envtest | a refused `Adopt`: a gated Agent whose `status.auth` holds `{kind: Adopt, stage: Refused}` evaluates and promotes its candidate, and the route's one `backendRef` then names the promoted revision's Service | key the predicate on `status.auth.transaction != nil`; or skip `ensureServingRoute` in `refuseAdopt`, which the `backendRef` assertion catches |
| envtest | a J2 `Lock` that can finish only through a promotion: a gated Agent served under `auth: none`, whose serving revision R's card digest is first cleared by a failing re-read while R is still desired (the re-read made due by back-dating `status.cards[]`'s `fetchedAt` past `CardDriftInterval`, as `test/envtest/card_test.go` does), is then edited to `apikey`. After the edit R is not desired, so its card can be cleared only before it. `ProbingBefore` finds no recorded card to match, so `beforeObserved` stays false, and the `Lock` reaches `ProbingAfter` with a `401` it cannot attribute. The J2 edit's revision, whose card validates, is evaluated and promoted. The route's `backendRef` then names it, and the `Lock` credits its `401` on its recorded card and reaches `Served` | wait on every `Lock`, which leaves the `Lock` at `AuthLockUnverified` and the candidate at `EvalWaitingForAuth`; or skip `runLock`'s re-assertion of the route, which the `backendRef` assertion catches |
| envtest | the missing-policy wedge, built in this order. (1) With R served under `apikey` and still desired, clear R's card with a failing card stub, the re-read made due by back-dating `status.cards[]`'s `fetchedAt` past `CardDriftInterval`, as `test/envtest/card_test.go` does. (2) Delete `<agent>-auth`, and wait for the `Lock` to reach `ProbingAfter` with a `401` it cannot attribute. (3) Only then mint the candidate C, available and carded. The stub sees zero hits. The route's `backendRef` still names R. `<agent>-auth` is present, and the Agent carries `PolicyApplyIncomplete=True/AuthPolicyMissing`, `Ready=False` and phase `Degraded`. This is the **residue** design 03 A77 leaves: the route names R, `status.activeRevision` is R, and neither is carded, so the re-point does not fire. `EvalWaitingForAuth`'s message names R, names both exits and which of them this state admits — the route names `status.activeRevision` and C is pending, so it names the revert — and says that `status.activeRevision`'s own card recording would also end it, with no card fetch running for that revision while C is desired. The test then deletes `<agent>-serving`: a route re-create `Create` displaces the `Lock` and publishes on `status.activeRevision`, and once it is `Served` the run starts | exempt the missing-policy `Lock` by dropping the J2-or-K2 clause, which the zero-hit assertion catches; the generic message; or name the administrator as the only way out, which design 03 A78 measured false and which the card-recording assertion catches |
| envtest | **the re-point ends the wedge with nobody acting** — design 03 §8.1 case 18 (a) from the gate's side. R's card is cleared while R is desired; then C is minted and runs. The stub holds C's last case while the test deletes `<agent>-auth`, so the `Lock` is recorded before the verdict. The stub answers, and that case's record writes `Passed` (step 7). C is promoted with its card recorded, and with the `Lock` already at `ProbingAfter` the **same pass** re-points the route at C, as `TestACandidatesPromotionEndsTheWedgeWithNobodyActing` measures. The route gate then defers the request: the write bumped the generation, so that pass takes no answer. Once the Gateway reports at the new generation, the `401` is attributed on C's card and the `Lock` reaches `Served` — no administrator, no revert. The next candidate D then runs and never reads `EvalWaitingForAuth` | never re-point, which leaves the `Lock` at `ProbingAfter` and D at `EvalWaitingForAuth`; hold the eval after the `Lock` is gone, which D's run catches; or credit in the pass that moved the route, which the no-answer assertion on that pass catches. "Re-point unconditionally" is the **next** row's, built on a state this one cannot reach |
| envtest | **the wedge with the route and the active revision apart, which design 03 A77 narrows and does not remove**, built in this order, because the order is the whole row. (1) R served under `apikey`, its card cleared by a failing re-read while R is still desired. (2) C minted, available and carded; the run starts, and the stub holds C's last case. (3) `<agent>-auth` deleted, so the missing-policy `Lock` is recorded; the test waits for it to reach `ProbingAfter`. (4) A foreign `traffic` policy is stored **on `<agent>-serving`, under a name of its own and not `<agent>-auth`** — `foreignTrafficPolicies` sees a policy only when it carries `spec.traffic`, targets `<agent>-serving`, and is not this Agent's. The name matters: one stored **at** `<agent>-auth` fails the intact read instead and rewinds the transaction to `ApplyingPolicies`, which is design 03 §8.1 case 18 (c)'s state and not this one. Here the stage stays at `ProbingAfter` and every pass breaks at the foreign check, one rung above the re-point (design 03 §3.3.3: such a pass "never reaches this step, and its route does not move"). (5) The stub answers, and that in-flight case's record writes `Passed` (step 7). (6) C is promoted, which a hold does not stop once the verdict is written ("Clause 5 stops new claims only"). The state is then apart **with C carded**: `status.activeRevision` is C, the route's `backendRef` still names R, and nothing moves over an observation interval. **The message names no exits here, and that is the assertion.** `status.cards[]` records a digest for `status.activeRevision`, so the third clause of "When it names the exits" fails even though the first two hold, and the next candidate D reads the generic `EvalWaitingForAuth` — the transaction's kind and stage — naming neither R nor a revert. Clause 5 is not evaluated at all, because a re-creation fails clause 1, so `status.eval.blocked` is empty; the foreign policy is named on the Agent by design 03's own `ForeignTrafficPolicy` report, not by this message. Three more revisions are then minted, and R is collected with its Service under a route that still names it — the outage "Leaving it standing" describes, which is the coverage this row exists for. **A variant reaches the apart state with an uncarded C, and it is where the exits are named**: no foreign policy; the `Lock` is left at `Converging`, where `lockMissingPolicy`'s fresh `<agent>-auth` reports no ancestors yet; C is promoted in that window; and a failing card stub clears C's digest by a drift re-read (`recordCardAttempt`) before the route and the policy are accepted. The `Lock` then reaches `ProbingAfter` with C uncarded, the route is left as found, and D's `EvalWaitingForAuth` names R and offers the revert to R, not to C | main build: name the exits whenever the route's revision is uncarded — dropping the `status.activeRevision` half of the condition — which the carded-C no-exits assertion catches; or move the re-point above the foreign check, which the "route still names R" assertion catches. **This row is the only pin on that ordering.** Design 03 A79 adds that design's §8.1 case 18 (i), which pins the same ordering from design 03's side and is owed and not yet built; once it is built, this row is no longer the only pin. Design 03 §8.1 case 18 (c) pins it only in the enlarged form — that case holds its `Lock` at `ApplyingPolicies` and never enters the `ProbingAfter` arm, so the narrow move is invisible to it — and case 18 (h) pins the route **gate** against A75's Gateway read, not the re-point against the foreign check. Moving the re-point above the intact read is the same mutation enlarged. Variant: re-point unconditionally, which the "route still names R" assertion catches; compute the message from `status.activeRevision` in place of the route's single `backendRef`, which names C where the spec names R and which the "names R" assertion catches; or name `status.activeRevision` in the revert, which the "revert names R" assertion catches |
| envtest | the exemption's edges: in the J2 row's state, no case is claimed while the `Lock` is at `ApplyingPolicies` or `Converging`. A J2 `Lock` at `ProbingAfter` with `beforeObserved` set and the serving card cleared is not exempt | exempt the `Lock` at any stage; or ignore `beforeObserved` |
| envtest | a J2 `Lock` in the J2 row's state, held at `ProbingAfter` by a Gateway-level policy, with the `Lock`'s deadline shortened in the test and allowed to pass. The stub sees zero hits while the policy stands: before the deadline, and after it, when `PolicyApplyIncomplete`'s reason has become `AuthLockUnverified`. Across that interval `GatesPassed` reads `False/EvalWaitingForAuth`: before the eval controller's first clause-5 pass, because `blockedFor` does not match, and after it, from `blocked`, whose message names the policy. So a correct build shows `EvalWaitingForAuth` from the moment the `Lock` reaches `ProbingAfter`. A variant with the Gateway's `get` refused through an injected reader also sees zero hits. Once the policy is removed, the run starts, passes and promotes, and the `Lock` reaches `Served` | drop the fifth clause, which the zero-hit interval catches; key it on `PolicyApplyIncomplete`'s reason, as A9 did, which the interval after the deadline catches; ignore an unreadable Gateway, which the variant catches; or a gate branch that checks only clauses 1 to 4 and shows `EvalRunning` while nothing is claimed, which the `GatesPassed` assertion catches; or read an unmatched `blockedFor` as no hold, which the interval's first moments catch |
| envtest | a J2 `Lock` in the J2 row's state beside a foreign traffic policy that targets `<agent>-serving`, with the deadline shortened and passed. The stub sees zero hits while the foreign policy stands, before the deadline and after it, and `GatesPassed` reads `False/EvalWaitingForAuth` throughout, as in the Gateway-level row. In a variant, the error is injected into the eval controller's reader only, once the `Lock` is recorded at `ProbingAfter`. It fails the run namespace's policy list with a Forbidden or a 5xx error, never NotFound, and lets the Gateway namespace's list succeed. Design 03's reader is left alone: `gatewayStep` returns on a failed foreign list before `authStep`, which would stall the `Lock` short of `ProbingAfter`, where clause 2 already keeps the eval off, and the mutation would survive. That variant also sees zero hits. A NotFound would read as no foreign policies, as design 03 reads it, so the suite's existing `policiesUnlistable` double, which returns NotFound, cannot build this variant. Once the foreign policy is removed, the run starts | drop the foreign-policy half of the fifth clause, which the zero-hit interval catches; read a failed foreign list as no hold, which the variant catches; or a gate branch that checks only clauses 1 to 4, which the `GatesPassed` assertion catches |
| envtest | an orphaned claim of the last case while a `Lock` is recorded, built as the handover row is: the test itself writes the last case's claim with no outcome. The `Lock` is a missing-policy `Lock`, which is never exempt, on a route whose revision R has its card recorded, so that it can reach `Served`. The probe oracle withholds the `401` to keep it short of `Served`. Over an observation interval, no `Interrupted` is recorded and no verdict is written. The oracle then answers, the `Lock` reaches `Served`, and `Interrupted` is recorded | record `Interrupted` without the no-transaction predicate |
| envtest | R's name in the wedge message, in the wedge state with **C a held candidate**, so `status.activeRevision` is still R and no re-point moves the route. (Design 03 A77 §11 asks which of two readings this row means; it is this one, and it builds. The other reading, where C has been promoted, is the state the withdrawn "apart" row needed.) Once C is desired, the test deletes R's Service, which is not re-created because `ensureService` runs only for the desired revision. `EvalWaitingForAuth` still names R, taken from the `backendRef`. The no-`backendRef` fallback has no row. It is reachable only in the one stale pass before `runLock` swaps the `Lock` for `recreateRoute`, so a test that reads the Agent afterwards cannot see it | take R's name from the Service, which the deleted-Service case catches |
| envtest | a `Lock` past its deadline (the deadline shortened in the test) keeps the eval waiting, and `EvalWaitingForAuth` names when the deadline passed (Q12 (a)) | claim cases once the deadline has passed |
| envtest | `cardDigest`: once a run starts, `status.eval.cardDigest` equals the digest `status.cards[]` recorded for the candidate when the run-start card was accepted | record the digest of a later fetch; or record nothing |
| envtest | a pin while the suite is missing: a digest in `failed[]` is admitted, and the current candidate's digest is refused; with the EvalSuite CRD absent, every pin behaves as today | refuse on `failed[]` when there is no current suite |
| envtest | the read-back, on its own: the eval controller runs alone, without the Agent reconciler, whose own card re-reads would hit the stub and change the resourceVersion. An Agent CRD without the new `status.eval` fields is installed, so the API server prunes them from every write, as a stale install would. Stripping them from the patch response instead would be unfaithful: the API server would still store them, and a correct implementation would move on to a claim. The stub sees zero cases. Over the first 10 s from the manager's start, with the eval controller's requeue field set to 2 s, the stub serves **at least 3 and at most 6** card fetches, and the Agent's resourceVersion does not change after the first run-start write. The floor is what a missing requeue fails: a build that returns none fetches once and goes quiet, which is inside every upper bound (A17) | drop the read-back, and the stub sees case 1 again and again; return `Requeue: true`, whose rate-limited retries start at 5 ms and exceed the ceiling; no requeue after a failed read-back, which the floor catches; or write `at` at run start, which the resourceVersion bound catches |
| envtest | the schema check, on its own: an Agent CRD installed without the new `status.eval` fields — the set includes `blockedFor` — reads `GatesPassed=False/EvalRecordNotKept`, naming the remedy. A CRD carrying every other new field but not `blockedFor` reads the same, and not `EvalWaitingForAuth`. A CRD read that fails, with nothing cached, reads `EvalRecordUnverifiable` | drop the schema check, which leaves the Agent naming no cause; omit `blockedFor` from the checked set, which the `blockedFor`-only CRD catches; or treat a failed read as a pass |
| envtest | `status.eval.blocked`: a card whose only interface URL carries a query reads `EvalBindingUnsupported`, and a run-start card that differs from the recorded digest reads `EvalAwaitingCard`; the Agent reconciler sets each from `blocked` | the Agent reconciler ignores `blocked` |
| envtest | a J2 `Lock` in the exempt state, with no hold standing, whose candidate's card is valid but has no usable `HTTP+JSON` 1.0 interface, for example only a JSON-RPC 1.0 interface. `GatesPassed` reads `False/EvalBindingUnsupported`, and over 10 s the Agent's resourceVersion does not change | clear `blocked` whenever clause 5 finds no hold, which the resourceVersion bound catches; or show `EvalRunning` when `blocked` is not the hold, which the reason catches |
| envtest | a stale hold: a J2 `Lock` in the Gateway-level row's state has `blocked` recording `EvalWaitingForAuth`. A spec edit then abandons the `Lock`, and once the new candidate's run starts, `blocked` is empty and `blockedFor` names the new pair | clear `EvalWaitingForAuth` only when clause 5 is next evaluated, which leaves it standing here |
| envtest | a suite reordered mid-run: after the first of three cases, the suite's cases are rewritten in reverse order, with the same digest. The stub sees each case exactly once, and the verdict counts all three | pick the next case by position |
| envtest | the generated EvalSuite CRD installs on the envtest API server, at message 4096 and expectations 512; the echo rule admits a `contains` case and an `equals` case, and refuses both echoes | raise either expectation bound to 4096, which the API server refuses as over the CEL cost budget; or drop a `has()` guard, which refuses every well-formed case |
| envtest | Q6 (c), with impersonation, as `test/envtest/admission_reservation_test.go` does: a user who may only write suites is denied; one who may `update` Agents only under `resourceNames` is denied; one who may `update` Agents namespace-wide is admitted | drop the policy binding |
| e2e, k3d | Q6 (c) on the e2e clusters: the same three users, with the same results. Built in build step 9 (`TestOnlyAnAgentEditorWritesAnEvalSuiteOnTheE2ECluster`); it runs in CI's e2e jobs, on k3s v1.35.5-k3s1 under k3d and on kind v1.35.0 on PR #125, and passed once unmutated and failed once with the binding dropped on k3s v1.33.6, locally | drop the policy binding |
| envtest | a gate added over a serving revision with no verdict reads `GatesPassed=False/ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated`, never `EvalPassed`; removing the gate after a failed candidate flips `GatesPassed` to `False/NoGatesDeclared` | assert `EvalPassed` whenever the promotion branch runs; or leave `GatesPassed` unasserted on removal |
| envtest | generation lag: the eval controller runs without the Agent reconciler, so the old candidate stays recorded after a spec edit; the eval controller sends it nothing | drop the generation predicate |
| envtest | admission refuses two gates, an empty case list, duplicate case ids, a threshold of `"0"`, and two expectations on one case | delete each CEL rule in turn; each deletion must fail its own case, and only its own |
| envtest | J2 on a gated Agent, with a stub that answers each case after 1 s. The probe oracle withholds the `401`, which holds the `Lock` at `ProbingAfter`, until the candidate is available and its card recorded. The stub sees zero hits across that interval. The oracle then answers, the `Lock` reaches `Served` inside its deadline, and the run starts, passes and promotes | drop the `Create`-or-`Lock` predicate, which the zero-hit interval catches |
| envtest | a missing-policy `Lock` recorded when a `Passed` candidate is promoted, with the card of the route's revision R recorded. Built so that the `Lock` comes before the promotion: the stub holds candidate C's last case while the test deletes `<agent>-auth`; the `Lock` is recorded; the stub answers, and that in-flight case's record writes the `Passed` verdict (step 7).
