The probe oracle withholds the `401` until the test has seen `status.activeRevision == C` with the `Lock` still recorded, so the `Lock` cannot reach `Served` before the promotion; the oracle then answers. The next pass promotes C while the `Lock` is recorded. **Since design 03 A77 the route does not wait for `Served`**: the re-point reads `status.activeRevision`, not the revision the route names, so with C carded — which Q10's fourth condition guarantees on a gated Agent — and the `Lock` already at `ProbingAfter`, the promoting pass itself moves the `backendRef` to C while the `Lock` still runs. That pass takes no answer, because its own write bumped the generation the route gate reads; the next one credits the `401` on **C's** card, and the `Lock` reaches `Served` with the `backendRef` never returning to R. R's card being recorded changes nothing, because nothing reads it. *(Rewritten by A19. A9 built this row on "while the `Lock` runs, the route's `backendRef` still names R, and the `401` is credited on R's card"; A77 makes every assertion in it and both of its mutations unreachable, and A77's own inventory did not list it.)* | re-point only after `Served`, which the mid-`Lock` `backendRef` assertion catches; `cardAttributes` returning the first entry of `status.cards[]` with a non-empty digest whatever backend the route names, which is its pre-A77 shape — R's and C's cards are recorded with different `fetchedAt`s, and the assertion is that the served `GovernanceSkipped` message names **C's**, which is the only place the credit's source is observable; or hold promotion while a `Lock` is recorded, which the promotion assertion catches |
| e2e, k3d | `TestARevisionIsEvaluatedBeforeItTakesTraffic`: a gated API-key Agent, with a suite whose case sends `hello` and expects `echo: hello`, passes and is published; its first revision carries marker `first`, and the requests through the gateway send `probe`, answered `echo: probe`. An edit to the responder's answer prefix, `ANSWER_PREFIX=held: `, mints a revision that fails and holds, and for 60 s every answer through the gateway is the first revision's: 31 requests, each followed by a 2 s sleep, from one probe Pod, each with `--max-time 10`, inside the probe's 3-minute ceiling, each HTTP 200, a completed task, `echo: probe` and marker `first`. Because the requests take time on top of the sleeps, the window is at least 60 s, and at a sustained latency above about 3.8 s a request, the probe misses its ceiling and the row fails, closed. An edit that restores the prefix and changes the metadata marker, to `third`, passes. Once the route's single `backendRef` names it, the row waits, within 3 minutes and with a new probe Pod on each attempt, for the first answer that carries `third`, and then 20 consecutive answers through the gateway, sent after that one from another Pod, carry the new marker; answers between the route naming the revision and the first that carries `third`, which the proxy's lag behind the route object allows, are not counted. The row also asserts that `status.eval` records `Passed` for the first and third revisions and `Failed` for the second, with its case `hello` recorded `Failed`; `GatesPassed=True/EvalPassed` on the first and third once active, and `False/EvalFailed` on the second; phase `Held`, the first revision active and the second the candidate while it holds; and the route served under `apikey`, naming the first revision once published and again after the hold window. It does not sample the route while the first revision's eval runs: that the route stays unpublished until the promotion is structural, not measured, because every `ensureServingRoute` call names `status.activeRevision` and no `Create` is entered before there is one. It writes its suite as the cluster administrator, so it does not exercise Q6 (c), which T62 does. Built in build step 10, PR #130 (`test/e2e/gate_test.go`) | make the gate branch always pass, which the first verdict wait catches, since the first revision is then promoted with no verdict, before any failing prefix exists; make the matcher always pass; or send the eval's cases to the active revision's Service, which the second revision's verdict catches |
| e2e, k3d | `TestAGatewayLevelPolicyHoldsTheCreateAfterTheGateHasPassed`: a Gateway-level auth policy standing while a gated Agent's candidate passes: the candidate is promoted, and `Create` holds, unpublished, reason `GatewayAuthPolicy`. The policy is hand-authored from `compiler.AuthPolicy`'s output, API-key authentication and a CEL authorization rule — the rule is what makes design 03 A75's W1 count it — as an `AgentgatewayPolicy` targeting the assayd Gateway's serving listener, applied by `applyGatewayLevelAuthPolicy` (`test/e2e/gatewayhold_test.go`), which `TestAGatewayLevelAuthPolicyHoldsANewAgent` shares. The row asserts the `Create` held at `ProbingAfter` on a `401`, `PolicyApplyIncomplete=GatewayAuthPolicy` naming the policy, the promotion standing and the route with no `backendRefs`; and, once the policy is removed, the route published on the promoted revision, an anonymous request refused `401`, and a keyed one answered `200`, a completed task carrying the agent's marker `gwgated`. Under the credit mutation, `got401 && !held` → `got401`, the `Create` reaches `Served` and W1 raises the same `PolicyApplyIncomplete=GatewayAuthPolicy` on the served Agent, so the reason alone does not kill it; the transaction and route checks do. The row does not reach clause 5 of the exemption: a `Create` is never exempt, and a new gated Agent records no transaction while its first revision's eval runs, so clause 5's hold removed survives this row and T68 alike. No e2e row pins clause 5; envtest does, six of its tests failing with the hold removed, as PR #141 measured after A24.2 replaced two rows that reached clause 5 on a final-verdict or failed-pair pass, T47's two, T48's two and T58 among them. Every input clause 5 reads is an API object envtest's API server holds, and no e2e row is added for it. Like T68, it writes its suite as the cluster administrator (`applyGateSuite`), so it does not exercise Q6 (c). Built in build step 10, PR #130 | promote only when the route is published; or credit `Create`'s `401` under the Gateway-level policy, which the transaction and route checks catch |

## 2. Doctrine & charter gates

- **Plane**: slow (gate controller = part of the agent-operator; the *runner* is a provider slot, fast-plane images; datasets/metrics/thresholds are data).
- **Pods**: 0 standing — eval runs are **Jobs**; nightly runs are CronJobs. **Stateful deps**: Postgres (eval results — substrate per ADR-0002), object store (dataset + report artifacts). ✓
- **Primitives**: Resource (CRDs), Artifact (datasets/reports as content-addressed OCI artifacts), Agent (the runner drives real A2A tasks), Event (`eval.completed`). ✓ **EvalRunner joins the charter's reserved-slot list as shipped** (architecture §15 erratum, like IdentityProvider before it).

## 3. The EvalSuite CRD

```yaml
kind: EvalSuite
metadata: {name: pa-regression, namespace: claims}
spec:
  runner: {type: deepeval}                # evalrunner/v1 slot: deepeval | inspect | byo image
  target: {agentRef: pa-reviewer}         # what this suite gates (or modelRef, P5)
  dataset:
    goldenSetRef: pa-golden-v3            # curated + ontology-derived (§5); content-addressed artifact
    fromSessions: {sample: 200, since: 7d, strata: {by: hop_outcome}}   # design 17 sampler
  metrics:
    - {name: task_completion, threshold: 0.85}
    - {name: tool_correctness, threshold: 0.9}
    - {name: faithfulness_to_kg}          # mechanical: citations verified via kg.cite (§6)
    - {name: cost_regression, maxIncrease: 15%}
    - {name: custom_geval, judge: {model: gpt-x@pinned, promptRef: pa-judge-v2}}   # LLM-judge, pinned
  gate: {minScore: 0.85, blocking: true}
  budget: {tokensPerRun: 500k, usdPerRun: 5}    # the eval principal's own budget (ADR-0028 pattern)
status:
  lastRun: {report: oci://…@sha256:…, datasetDigest: …, judgeDigest: …, scores: {…}, verdict: pass}
  conditions: [DatasetReady, LastRunPassed]
```

**A revision reference is `{name, digest}` everywhere in this design (A2).** Design 02 A37 separated the two: the ten-character `name` is what a workload is called and what `kubectl get agents` prints, and the **full digest** is the identity. Forty bits is not a security boundary against an attacker-controlled projection — a chosen collision between a safe and a malicious spec was found in **1.2 seconds** — and this design is where that distinction earns its keep, because a verdict here is what grants production traffic.

Comparing a bare `revision` name would let a colliding projection be promoted on the verdict a different one earned, and that decision is made **before any workload is inspected**, so design 02's workload-level collision guard never sees it. The typed reference is therefore carried through every step below: the EvalRun input, the report's bound digests, `GatesPassed`, the candidate route's admitted principal, the events, and the receipt attribution. A step that carries only the name is a step where the gate can be spent on the wrong revision.

**Owed:** the `EvalSuite`/`EvalRun` CRDs do not exist yet, so this is a contract for the types when they are written rather than a description of a schema. Design 02 already carries the digest in `status.evalStatus.revisionDigest` and `status.cards[].revisionDigest`, which is the half that could be built without them (design 02 A50).

## 4. The gate flow (with design 02's rollout machine)

1. Candidate reaches `Held` (02 §3.3) → gate controller sees `gates:` on the Agent CR → `DatasetReady?` (build/refresh if stale, §5) → launches the **eval Job**. The controller reads `status.candidateRevisionDigest`, not `status.candidateRevision`, and the EvalRun records both (A2).
2. The Job drives **real A2A tasks through the gateway** at the candidate header-route. **Identity model (r1 f1)**: SVIDs are per-pod attested identities, never lent — the eval Job pod gets **its own SVID** via the existing label template (`spiffe://…/eval/<suite>/<run>`), and the compiler adds that principal to the candidate route's **admitted set at Job launch, removing it at Job end** — a narrow, temporary, compiled grant (the design 01 A2 pattern applied to routes; recorded as a design 03 row + ADR-0028 note). The gate controller never proxies eval traffic. **Runner trust bar** (runners are third-party pack images): signed image required (18), and the eval principal's compiled reachability is exactly {candidate route, pinned KG version, judge LLM egress} — fail-closed; the budget bounds the blast radius. Eval traffic exercises the identical path as prod, and every eval task produces **receipts** (§6).
3. Runner writes the structured report (per-case results + scores) → report artifact (content-addressed) + Postgres rows + CR status; verdict per §7.
4. **pass** → controller signals the operator, naming the **digest** it evaluated: the operator promotes only if that digest still matches its own candidate, so a verdict cannot be spent on a revision that changed underneath it (A2). Weight shift begins (canary steps). **Canary progression is judged on golden signals (SLOs), not re-evaluation** — the eval gate runs once, pre-canary; live-traffic degradation is the drift/SLO machinery's job (10/20). Stated to kill scope creep.
5. **fail** → candidate deleted (02), report linked in status + PR annotation; `GatesPassed=False` with the failing metrics named.
6. **Nightly**: CronJob runs the full suite against the *active* revision → trend rows (drift feed, 20); never gates.

## 5. The dataset builder

Datasets are **content-addressed artifacts** — a report always names the exact dataset digest it ran against (reproducibility is a first-class property):

- **Ontology-derived seeds**: design 12 §3.8 — one case per probe, `via` + matchers carried verbatim. These are *floor* cases (the graph's known answers, asked through the agent).
- **Curated cases**: `golden/` files in the agent's repo (the design-09 golden-task test is the seed), promoted via `assayd eval promote <session>`.
- **Session-derived cases** (17): stratified sample of recorded production tasks (inputs replayed; recorded outcomes as reference). Sampling is pinned at build time — the dataset artifact embeds the chosen sessions, so reruns are stable.
- Builder runs as part of the eval Job's first step (no standing pod); rebuilt when refs change or `since:` windows roll; `DatasetReady=False` names what's missing (e.g. zero sessions matching strata — loud, not empty-pass).

## 6. Metric semantics (the honest column)

| Metric | Source | Nature |
|---|---|---|
| `task_completion` | runner judgment per case (DeepEval agentic metric / matchers for ontology-derived cases) | judged (LLM for open cases, mechanical for `via`-matched) |
| `tool_correctness` | **receipts of the eval run** — normative v1 variant: **target-sequence match** (works at any capture level); argument-aware matching available when the eval posture captures bodies | mechanical |
| `faithfulness_to_kg` | every citation in the agent's answer resolved via `kg.cite` on the *pinned graph version*; uncited claims counted | mechanical |
| `cost_regression` | eval-run receipts vs the active revision's **trailing 7-day median per-task cost** (from the design-04 aggregate; window/statistic stated in the report) (r1 f4) | mechanical |
| `custom_geval` etc. | LLM-judge with **pinned model + versioned prompt**; judge config digest in the report | judged |

**Eval-principal posture is pinned independently of the agent's** (r1 f2): capture `full` on eval-principal traffic (reports store at receipt capture rules) and KG scope mirroring the target agent's — compiled with the temporary grant, so `kg.cite` resolves and argument-aware checks are possible. Judge calls go through the gateway under the eval principal (receipted, budgeted). **Flake policy**: infra-failed cases retry ×1; semantically-failed cases never retry (that's the signal). Agent stochasticity is bounded by convention (templates default eval-mode temperature) but not assumed: the verdict rule (§7) tolerates case-level noise via the threshold, not reruns-until-green — rerun-shopping is structurally impossible because the report binds (dataset digest, judge digest, candidate revision).

## 7. Verdict rule

`gate.minScore` applies to the **weighted mean of metric scores** (equal weights v1); each metric's own `threshold` is a hard floor — one floor breach fails regardless of the mean. Blocking suites hold the rollout (02); non-blocking suites annotate only. A suite with zero cases **fails closed** (`DatasetReady=False` — an empty eval must never pass an agent).

## 8. The `evalrunner/v1` slot

A runner is a Job image implementing: input `(dataset artifact ref, target endpoint + credentials, metric config, budget)` → output `(report JSON schema evalreport/v1: per-case {id, input_digest, outcome, per-metric scores, receipts refs}, summary scores)`. DeepEval adapter first (its `evaluate()` API maps directly); Inspect AI second; `byo` = any image honoring the contract. **`evalrunner/v1` and `evalreport/v1` join the `assayd-contracts` ledger** (design 07 §4 — r1 f3; the ledger carries every socket contract, and doctor/upgrade checks cover them). Conformance: a fixture dataset + mock agent where expected scores are known; parity across runners on the mechanical metrics (judged metrics are runner-specific by nature — documented, not hidden).

## 9. Failure modes

| Failure | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Runner Job crash | Job restart; per-case idempotency by case id (re-running completed cases is safe — results content-addressed) |
| Judge model unavailable | Judged metrics `error`, mechanical metrics complete; verdict **fails closed** with `JudgeUnavailable` named |
| Eval budget exhausted | Run halts; verdict fail-closed (`budget`); operator alert — an eval that can't afford to finish never passes anyone |
| Dataset refs missing/stale | `DatasetReady=False`, rollout stays Held, loudly |
| Candidate crashes under eval | Receipts show it; verdict fail with the crash cases named — that's the gate working |
| Nightly run fails | Trend gap marked; never affects serving |

## 10. Security

Eval Jobs hold: the candidate-route credential (gate-controller SVID), read access to the pinned KG version, the eval budget. They never hold prod user tokens (synthetic principals: `eval:<suite>@<candidate>` in receipts). Reports may embed prompts/outputs → stored at the receipts capture level, same redaction rules (ADR-0014).

## 11. Testing

Fixture agent (scripted A2A responder) + fixture suite: pass/fail/floor-breach/empty-dataset/judge-down/budget-exhausted paths; report reproducibility (same digests ⇒ same verdict); e2e on k3d: the flagship demo — `assayd deploy` streams `HELD → eval 0.89 ✓ → canary 10% → 100%`, then a deliberately-broken revision fails with the report in the PR annotation.

## 12. Decisions for async review

- **D1 — The eval gate runs once, pre-canary; canary progression is SLO-judged** (no re-eval mid-rollout) — scope pinned.
- **D2 — Datasets and reports are content-addressed artifacts**; a verdict always names (dataset, judge, revision) digests — rerun-shopping structurally impossible.
- **D3 — Fail-closed everywhere**: empty dataset, judge down, budget out ⇒ the candidate does not pass.
- **D4 — EvalRunner is a provider slot** (`evalrunner/v1`), DeepEval first, conformance with cross-runner parity on mechanical metrics.

## 13. Resulting ADRs

ADR-0024 (P3) after critique PASS. ADR-0024 Amendment 1 (2026-09-14) records the human's approval of the first slice (§1.1), and what it changes in ADR-0024.

## 14. Amendments

- **A1 (2026-08-20, from design 25 r1 f1)**: the **model metric family** joins the catalog (metrics are target-kind-scoped): `golden_quality` (mechanical on `via`-shaped sets, judged otherwise), `latency_p95` / `throughput` under a declared load profile, `refusal_safety_rate`, `cost_regression_per_1k_tokens` (never per-task). Model datasets = curated sets + optionally prompts *extracted* from agent sessions; raw A2A session cases are agent-only. The flow, fail-closed rules, and contracts are shared unchanged.
- **A2 (2026-09-01, from design 02 A50)**: every revision reference in this design is `{name, digest}`, and the **digest** is what decides. Design 02 A37 established that the ten-character name is a name — a chosen collision against its forty bits took 1.2 seconds — and A50 carried the digest into `evalStatus` and `cards[]` but could reach no further, because this design owns the component that actually grants production traffic. A gate controller comparing `evalStatus.revision == H` can promote a colliding projection on the verdict another one earned, and it makes that decision **before any workload exists to inspect**, so design 02's workload-level guard is not in the path. The reference is carried through EvalRun input, the report's bound digests, `GatesPassed`, the candidate principal, events and receipts; promotion re-checks the digest so a verdict cannot be spent on a revision that changed underneath it. The `EvalSuite` and `EvalRun` CRDs do not exist yet, so this is the contract their types must satisfy rather than a description of a schema — **owed at implementation**, and §8 owes the case that proves a verdict for one digest cannot authorize a colliding one.
- **A3 (2026-09-14, ADR-0030 build step 4) — a first-slice draft, and the Status line corrected.** Adds §1.1, a narrow gate the human can approve on its own, drawn the way design 03 §1.1 drew design 03's. An Agent names one EvalSuite. Its candidate revision is evaluated before it takes any traffic, then promoted wholesale or held. There are no canaries and no traffic split. Each of the suite's cases is sent by the operator as one A2A 1.0 `SendMessage` to the candidate's Service, one case per reconcile pass, and compared mechanically with a `contains` or `equals` expectation. The verdict is bound to the full revision digest and a suite digest. Promotion re-reads both in the pass that promotes. §1.1 also states how the slice composes with design 03's `Create`, its `Lock`, the I1 hold and A75's Gateway-level hold. It gives the conditions, reasons and phases that result, what is in the slice and what is out, what the slice depends on (every item owed), and the tests owed, each with its mutation.
  - **Nothing is decided.** Q1–Q8 in §1.1 are the human's: where eval requests go, what a failure does to the candidate, whether a verdict is final, how one gate per Agent is enforced, whether a pinned rollback is evaluated, who may write a suite, whether the chart installs the CRD, and the suite's schema and binding. The slice is drawn on each question's recommended answer, and says so where an answer would change a rule.
  - **Six statements in this design cannot be kept at the slice's scope**, and §1.1 lists them with their reasons. A deleted failing candidate would be re-created by the next reconcile. Binding digests does not make rerun-shopping impossible. "Through the production path" needs a candidate route and an identity that do not exist. Two references join a suite and an Agent. `threshold` is a float, which controller-gen refuses. §10 still carries the identity model r1 withdrew, which r2's residual R2-a asked to strike before ADR-0024 and nobody did.
  - **The Status line said "approved".** `docs/designs/README.md` said "awaiting user approval", and under ADR-0030 the README arbitrates and a PASS is not an approval. The line now says "not approved". The text it replaced is quoted in the Status line, rather than deleted.
  - Nothing in A3 is implemented. No review file was edited.
- **A4 (2026-09-14, answering `reviews/16-a3-critique.md`: REVISE, 0 BLOCKER, 8 MAJOR, 9 MINOR) — §1.1 revised.** Every finding is answered in §1.1, and each changed sentence is tagged there. Nothing is decided: every change to a question changes its options and its recommendation, and the human chooses. Nothing in A4 is implemented.
  - **M1, finality.** A3 said the slice made a verdict final for its pair. One status slot cannot remember a pair once its run is superseded, so reverting a failed B and re-applying it re-ran B. A4 adds `status.eval.failed[]`, capped at 10 with the oldest evicted, and a recorded pair never runs again (`EvalPairAlreadyFailed`). Q3 is restated: a re-run always follows a visible edit, and nothing prevents retry-until-pass by edits. The D2 bullet in "cannot keep" is corrected to match. An envtest of A→B→A→B, and one of the cap, are owed.
  - **M2, the single worker.** Q1 gains (a′): a separate eval controller in the same manager, with its own queue and two workers, writing only `status.eval` by merge patch under an optimistic lock, while the Agent reconciler only reads the verdict. (a′) is now the recommendation, and (a)'s costs are stated in full: cross-tenant starvation, and another tenant's `Lock` missing its 4-minute deadline. A starvation envtest is owed.
  - **M3, the path.** The path is chosen once per run, from a card fetched at run start whose digest equals the recorded one, and it is recorded in `status.eval.endpointPath`. The join is specified: `url.Parse(...).Path`, trim one trailing `/`, append `/message:send`; a query or a fragment is `EvalBindingUnsupported`. The field is added to design 02's owed list.
  - **M4, redirects.** The eval client follows no redirect, and any `3xx` is `ProtocolError`, as `internal/controller/authprobe.go` already does. The card fetch's same hole is recorded as owed to design 02 §3.4, in a separate change.
  - **M5, the gateway.** The candidate's own egress crosses the gateway, so a Gateway-level policy or a gateway fault can fail a case. Q3 carries that cost. A3's "changes no case's outcome" is corrected.
  - **M6, the card.** A fourth promotion condition requires a recorded card digest in the pass that promotes. It is named as new enforcement, and added to design 02's owed list. It becomes Q10, because design 02 §3.4 says a card failure never withholds traffic. The `Lock` composition bullet is corrected.
  - **M7, pins and removed gates.** Q5(a) now admits a pin only when its digest is not the current candidate, not in `failed[]` and not in `supersededCandidates`, and refuses any other as `PinnedRevisionNotEligible`. The residual of the two capped records is stated. That removing `spec.gates` releases a held candidate is stated, and becomes Q9.
  - **M8, the invocation grant.** "What the verdict proves" is now "what it proves, and what it grants": EvalSuite write is unauthenticated invocation of the held candidate. Q6 is reframed, and gains (c), an admission policy that admits a suite write only from a principal who may also update Agents; (c) is now the recommendation. Design 07 A5.4's amendment is owed: the operator's ingress now carries eval tasks.
  - **The minors.** m1: an unavailable candidate pauses the run, `EvalPaused`, with no deadline. m2: no `Requeue: true`, the status patch drives the next pass, and a satisfied gate falls through to promotion. m3: every `GatesPassed` reason but the three `assessGates` can decide is set inside the rollout switch, and the table names the branch. m4: suites are read uncached, no informer is started, and a held candidate requeues every 5 minutes. m5: Q7's costs corrected for `helm upgrade`. m6: the collision-pair test's state is specified, and "exactly once" became "never re-sent after its outcome is recorded". m7: the eleven missing tests are added, each with its mutation. m8: the Status line quotes ADR-0030, and the approval must amend ADR-0024. m9: an echo-refusing CEL rule is added, and eval traffic is stated to be distinguishable.
  - The critique record is committed unmodified as `reviews/16-a3-critique.md`.
- **A5 (2026-09-14, answering `reviews/16-a4-critique.md`: REVISE, 0 BLOCKER, 6 MAJOR, 7 MINOR) — §1.1 revised again.** The re-critique found 13 of the first critique's 17 findings fully closed, no clobber between the two writers, and the four promotion conditions read consistently in one pass. Most of what it found is new, and comes from A4's second writer. Every finding is answered in §1.1, and each changed sentence is tagged there. Nothing is decided, and nothing in A5 is implemented.
  - **N1, the send was outside the lock.** Step 10 adds a protocol: read live, claim the case under the optimistic lock, send only after a claim that came back, and record with re-read-and-retry while the claim stands. `EvalRunning` no longer names k of N, so an eval patch that only records a case leaves the Agent reconciler's write a no-op. A claim found without an outcome becomes Q11, recommended at most once (`Interrupted`). A re-sent case is stated not to be assumed idempotent. A stale-reader unit test and a hit-count envtest are owed.
  - **N2, a stale Agent CRD.** Step 11: the eval controller reads back its run-start write and every claim, as design 03's `persistStatus` does, and sends nothing if a field did not come back. The Agent reconciler checks the installed Agent CRD's schema and names `EvalRecordNotKept`, because a pruned write cannot carry its own reason. The upgrade is owed to design 07 beside Q7, and so is a test.
  - **N3, reasons only the eval controller sees.** `status.eval.blocked {reason, message}` carries `EvalBindingUnsupported` and the run-start card mismatch to the Agent reconciler, which sets `GatesPassed` from it. It is added to design 02's owed list.
  - **N4, the evaluated Agent's own `Lock`.** The eval and the Agent's `-auth` transaction are serialised: no case is sent while `status.auth.transaction` is set, and the Agent reads `EvalWaitingForAuth`. The claim that the eval never delays a `Lock` is narrowed to other Agents. The J2 envtest now has a stub that answers each case after 1 s.

- **A6 (2026-09-14, answering `reviews/16-a5-critique.md`: REVISE, 0 BLOCKER, 2 MAJOR, 8 MINOR) — §1.1 revised a third time.** The third critique found N2, N3, N5, N6 and all seven of the re-critique's minors closed. Every finding is answered in §1.1, and each changed sentence is tagged there. Nothing is decided, and nothing in A6 is implemented.
  - **P1, the serialisation matched a refused `Adopt`.** The rule is now keyed on a `Create` or `Lock` recorded in `status.auth.transaction`, with each kind's stages named. A refused `Adopt` is a standing record that writes nothing, so it does not hold the eval: such an Agent is evaluated and promoted, on the unauthenticated route `Adopt` leaves. A transaction past its deadline still holds the eval, and whether it should is new question Q12, recommended yes. An envtest of the refused `Adopt` is owed, with the mutation `transaction != nil`, and so is one of the past-deadline wait.
  - **P2, graceful shutdown.** The send and the record run on `context.WithoutCancel`, bounded at 10 s + 5 s. That fits inside controller-runtime's default 30 s `GracefulShutdownTimeout` and the chart's 30 s `terminationGracePeriodSeconds`. So a rolling update lets the case in flight finish, and no new case is claimed once shutdown begins. Only a hard stop leaves an unrecorded claim, and Q11's "rare" is restated on that basis. A cancelled send is `Interrupted`, never `Unreachable`. A shutdown envtest and a chart test are owed.

    - m4: the conflict claim is narrowed to at most one outcome.

    - m6: the generation clause is the eval controller's alone.

- **A7 (2026-09-14, answering `reviews/16-a6-critique.md`: REVISE, 0 BLOCKER, 1 MAJOR, 4 MINOR) — §1.1 revised a fourth time.** The fourth critique found all ten of the third critique's findings closed. Every finding is answered in §1.1, and each changed sentence is tagged there. Nothing is decided, and nothing in A7 is implemented.
  - **R1, a cycle.** A `Lock` whose serving revision has no recorded card, and whose before-state was not observed, can finish only through a promotion. On a gated Agent that promotion needs a verdict, and A6's rule made the eval wait for the `Lock`. Of the critique's two fixes, A7 takes the first. The rule exempts exactly that state, read from status alone: a `Lock` at `ProbingAfter`, `beforeObserved` false, and no recorded card digest for `status.activeRevisionDigest`. Three reasons. A cycle that keeps a route open is worse than the contention. The contention cannot return, because the exemption starts only after the policy that closes the route is written and converged, and a pass lost at `ProbingAfter` in this state loses nothing that could finish the `Lock`. And the promotion's fourth condition guarantees the new serving revision has the card the `Lock` needs. No human decision was needed. The composition sentence and Q12's cost are corrected. An envtest of the missing-policy `Lock`, and one of the exemption's edges, are owed.

- **A15 (2026-09-16, answering `reviews/16-a13-critique.md`: FAIL, 0 BLOCKER, 2 MAJOR, 8 MINOR) — §1.1 revised.** The eleventh critique found both of the tenth critique's MAJORs closed — at-most-once, the in-flight claim and `failed[]` all survive the new clear — and every minor closed or half closed. The finality row asserts an empty `score` while the re-opened run reads `Running`, and kills "leave `score` standing at run start".

On the orchestrator's decision the eval controller gets a field for its requeue intervals, exactly as `AgentReconciler.AuthDeadline` exists beside `AuthTransactionDeadline` "for tests", with the zero value resolving to the three constants and a unit row for that resolution; "What the slice depends on" owes it, where nothing owed the seam before. The two rows that need only a stale card are re-mechanised onto the existing technique, back-dating `status.cards[]`'s `fetchedAt`, as `test/envtest/card_test.go` does. The two that need a short requeue name the field, and the read-back row's quantitative bounds are restated against it.

No decision among Q1 to Q13 moves, and the one rule that changes is clause 4's key, re-read by revision name to match the function the clause already cited.** Design 03 A77 makes the missing-policy `Lock` re-point its route at `status.activeRevision` at `ProbingAfter`, while `status.cards[]` records a digest for that revision (the human's decision of 2026-09-15, ADR-0034 Amendment 6), and adds a route gate to every `Lock`'s **`ProbingAfter`**: no answer is taken there until the assayd Gateway reports `Accepted` and `ResolvedRefs` on the route at its current generation. A78 corrects three sentences A77 left behind, among them the exits the operator's own message names. The wedge subsection's two opening paragraphs, its "The route", "The Agent's conditions", "The transient exit", "Not exits" and "Leaving it standing" rows, "The wedge is design 03's", and the promotion-during-a-`Lock` bullet under "How it composes with what exists" — where A77 found this design's "A missing-policy `Lock` does not move the route" — are rewritten on that boundary. "It never writes the route" and "So a promotion during that `Lock` leaves the route where it was" are false and are replaced; "`reconcileServed` … is not reached while a `Lock` is recorded" was checked against `authtxn.go` and is still true, so it stays.

The phrasing is taken from `missingPolicyRouteNote` (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`) rather than invented, because design 03 A78 records this message being wrong twice, once harmfully. **What the gate adds is stated as a cost**: on a gated Agent under this wedge the candidate cannot promote, since no case is claimed while the `Lock` is recorded, so the gate closes the one exit A77 opened — except by the two paths on which a verdict can still be written: one already `Passed` before the `Lock` began, and the outcome of the case in flight when it began, where that case is the run's last (step 7). Those two are also where A78's "a revert aborts the rollout" warning bites.

The route then sits on an uncarded R beside a carded C, unbounded, and three more revisions collect R with its Service — the garbage-collection outage this row exists for. **It asserts no exits there**, because a carded `status.activeRevision` fails the third clause of "When it names the exits"; the exits, R's name and the revert belong to the variant, where C is uncarded. The other four: clause 4 of the exemption reads `status.cards[]` "for `status.activeRevisionDigest`", which is `hasCardFor`'s key; `cardAttributes` and `cardedRevision` both match a card entry by revision **name**, and A77 requires the two readings never to be able to disagree about one revision. Keyed on the digest they can, because a revision name is 40 bits and two entries can carry one (A2), so the clause is re-keyed on the name. **That is a correction, not a decision**, and the difference from clause 1 is the reason A19 makes one and not the other: clause 4 cited `cardAttributes` as its own justification and then read a different key, and the two disagreements are not symmetric — a name-keyed read that finds a digest where the digest-keyed read does not fails the clause and holds the eval, which is safe, while the reverse exempts a `Lock` that `cardAttributes` would in fact have credited, and lets an eval patch race its status writes. Aligning on the key `cardAttributes` uses is the only reading that cannot do the second. Clause 1, by contrast, is a deliberate scope decision whose rationale expired, and moving it changes which Agents the eval runs beside. `runLock` now has **two** `recreateRoute` call sites, not one — A77's re-point hands off a route found gone — both still inside the re-creation branch clause 1 negates, so A13's rule is unchanged and only its count was wrong. The exemption's "What it buys" said a J2 or K2 `Lock` "credits its next `401`" after the promotion it earned; A77's route gate defers that credit past the promoting pass, because the write that moves the route bumps the generation the gate reads, so the bullet now says the credit comes on a later pass and what that costs. And "The fallback" put a route with no `backendRef` in the generic `EvalWaitingForAuth` text; a route with **two** now names no revision either, and it is the specific message rather than the fallback, so the bullet is split.

Widening it is a rule change to an approved section and needed the human, by the route Q13 (a) named; leaving it is fail-closed and costs liveness, not safety. **The human left it, on 2026-09-17**, so clause 1 reads today exactly as it read on 2026-09-14. §1.1 states all of that where the clause is defined, rather than in this log alone, and it put **three** answers to the human rather than two, as a table: leave it; widen to every missing-policy `Lock`; or widen only to one in the residue. Their spread is small, and stating its read cost took four rounds to get right — each round correcting the option the previous one had not touched. The exemption is a conjunction of five clauses and widening clause 1 suspends none of the rest, so **clause 4 binds every option**: none can exempt a `Lock` whose `status.activeRevision` is carded, and (b)'s whole excess over (c) is the single state where the **route's** revision is carded and the active one is not, which the `Lock` credits itself out of within a pass or two. **Both widenings turn clause 5 on** for a class that makes none of its reads today — **four** of them: one Gateway `get` and two paged policy lists, all uncached, and one cached `Get` of `<agent>-serving`, which is clause 5's own because `foreignTrafficPolicies` skips it only on an empty run-namespace list and at `ProbingAfter` that list holds `<agent>-auth`. Had the liveness been wanted, **(b) and (c) were both defensible**, and both stay in §1.1 with their costs. *(The third option is `reviews/16-a19-critique.md`'s, which recommends leaving it too.)* The Status line is unchanged: it names no amendment by design (A15's m6), and nothing in it went false.
  - **One finding against merged design 03 text, recorded and not acted on.** §8.1 case 18 (h)'s coverage sentence says "(c) pins the re-point against the foreign check, and nothing else reaches this ordering". Case 18 (c) holds its `Lock` at `ApplyingPolicies` and never enters the `ProbingAfter` arm, so the narrow ordering is invisible to it, and nothing in design 03's shipped suite pins it — which is why §1.1's own owed row claims to be its only pin. A19 does not edit design 03; this goes to design 03's process. *(Found while re-aiming that row's mutation, and confirmed independently by `reviews/16-a19-critique4.md`.)*
  - **What stays true, checked rather than assumed.** Q13 as the record of a decision taken on 2026-09-14; its routing-table row, "a missing-policy `Lock` … waits, and can wedge"; "The working exit" and its cost; Q13's "The administrator's exit works in every case today"; and Q13's "Its trigger is not always two events at once" — a revision that never recorded a card still wedges on a deleted policy alone when it is also the active revision. `reconcileServed`'s unreachability while a `Lock` is recorded, which three sentences rest on, was re-read in `authtxn.go`.
  - The table below gains one row per round. A19 answers an inventory rather than a critique of §1.1, so its first draft added none; each critique of a later draft is a critique of §1.1 as amended, and each is recorded there and committed unmodified as `reviews/16-a19-critique.md`, `reviews/16-a19-recritique.md`, `reviews/16-a19-critique3.md`, `reviews/16-a19-critique4.md` and `reviews/16-a19-critique5.md`, the last of which passes A19. A19 is revised in place rather than superseded by an A20, on design 03 A77's precedent: an amendment that has not merged answers its critiques within itself.
- **A20 (2026-10-19, the drafter's; decided by the human on 2026-10-21, every item as recommended, A20.23's decline included; answering #102, #105, #108, #111 and PR #112's notes) — §1.1 brought level with build steps 1 to 6, two decisions of the human recorded, and one question put to the human; revised in place after ten critiques, the tenth of which passed it.** Terms are defined in "Tag forms", below. Build steps 1 to 6 merged as PRs #103, #104, #109, #106, #110 and #112, the last at `875c1a3`. Building them made sentences of §1.1 false and made choices §1.1 does not state; because §1.1 is approved text, each was recorded on an issue or a PR thread rather than edited in (#102, #105, PR #112's reviews), and A20 carries them in. It also answers #101's two redirect sentences, which #102 asked to fold into the same amendment. **It was proposed on 2026-10-19 and decided by the human on 2026-10-21** ("Decided", below).
  - **Decided 2026-10-21 by the human, as recommended (A20.23 declined → #121; A20.38 (a) → #113, built under #123; A21.4 (b) → #122); folded into §1.1 by this change.** The human's words, on PR #117: "approved as recommended". Every kind (i) and kind (ii) item is decided as the decision table at the end of this entry recommends, including A20.23's decline: the approved `NoGatesDeclared` sentence stands, and `assessGates` is to name the serving revision (#121). A20.38 is answered (a): a fourth `blocked` reason for a candidate whose address cannot be used, owed as a short amendment and a code PR. #113 records that decision, and #123 tracks the build. The kind (iii) items A20.33 to A20.35 ended with build step 7, and A20.18's recorded order is superseded by A21.1 to A21.3; the fold removed their tags and wrote the as-built rule. The kind (iv) facts are written into §1.1 as facts. Recording A20 and A21 as approved parts in `humanApprovedParts` (`test/docs/approval_parts_test.go`), and in the approval lists of `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, is build step 11's, as the inventory's build step 11 lists it (`docs/research/design16-slice1-inventory-2026-10.md`), under ADR-0024's process, and is not done here. *(Superseded: PR #131 recorded A20 and A21 in `humanDecidedParts`, as decided and not approved, because this design's Status line names no approval of them; PR #136 recorded A22 and A23 the same way.)*
  - **Tags that outlive the fold.** The fold (#116) removed every proposed, recorded, measured, question and fact tag of A20 and A21 from §1.1 and the Status line, and the notices under the Status line. Thirteen tags remain, of two kinds. **Where the code at `0c5dcb0` departs from §1.1's text**, each removed by the change that ends the departure: *(A20.36, interim until build step 8: …)* three times — at "A pinned rollback, and a removed gate" and twice in Q5; *(A20.37, interim until build step 9: …)* twice — at "EvalSuite write is unauthenticated invocation" and in the decision table's Q6 row; and *(code departs until #121)* once, at the `GatesPassed` table's `NoGatesDeclared` row. **Where the human decided a change that neither §1.1 nor the code yet carries**, each removed by the amendment that writes the change: *(#113 decided (a): …; until #123 builds it, …)* twice — at the `blocked` row of the `status.eval` table and at the `EvalRunning` row of the `GatesPassed` table; and *(a follow-up amendment under #122 …; until then …)* five times — at the exempt state's order, in "What the gate branch derives in the exempt state" and in "The gate branch's order", and at the clause-5 reads on final-verdict and failed-pair passes, twice in "What wakes the eval controller while it waits" (the intervals' reasons, and a failed clause-5 read's error) and once in "What clause 5 costs". **Since A22**, decided by the human on 2026-10-22, the five A20.36 and A20.37 tags are gone: A22 removed them when build steps 8 and 9 had ended their departures (A22.28, taken), superseding this bullet's "removed by the change that ends the departure" for those five, and eight of the thirteen remain. A22's entry lists the tags that outlive its fold. Since #121, seven remain: the change that built #121 removed its tag. Since #122 (PR #141), two remain: the two `#113` tags. Since #123 (PR #142), none remain: PR #142 removed the two `#113` tags.
  - *The bullets below describe the text as proposed, before the fold.*
  - **Tag forms.** This bullet defines the terms and tag forms A20 uses; the notice under the Status line points here, and so does every other bullet of this entry. **"Build step N"** is a step of the build order `docs/research/design16-slice1-inventory-2026-10.md` defines; a bare "step N" is a step of §1.1's "How a run works". **Kinds of item.** Kind (i) is a rule A20 adds where §1.1 was silent. Kind (ii) is code, or a built test, that departs from approved text, put to the human. Kind (iii) is an interim departure that lasts until a named build step and is not proposed as text. Kind (iv) records a fact, measured by a test or read from the code, that makes approved text wrong or incomplete, with no decision. A question is put to the human and is neither proposed nor decided. **Deciding.** For each decidable item (every kind (i) or (ii) item but A20.18, which is recorded) the human takes it, declines it, or narrows it, with a note. A kind (ii) item's "If declined" line names what a decline implies: a code change, the fact that stands instead, or a change to approved text. Kind (iii), kind (iv) and recorded items carry no decision. **Forms.** A20 inserts text in §1.1 and the Status line and deletes none; the approved text stays and binds. Every insertion is a tag, inline or leading a paragraph or list item that it then covers whole. *(A20.n, proposed; replaces "X": Y)* names item n, which would put Y in place of the approved words X; *(A20.n, proposed; adds: Y)* names item n, which would add Y where the tag stands; and *(A20.n, proposed; adds)* leading a paragraph adds the paragraph. These quote which words an item touches, as a pointer for the reader; they are not instructions a script applies. *(A20.n, interim until build step N: …)* records how the code departs from the approved text beside it until build step N ends the departure; it is not proposed as text, and the approved text is what build step N must meet. *(A20.n, recorded, not proposed: …)* records a rule the code follows where §1.1 is silent, which the amendment accompanying build step 7 will propose; an inline recorded tag points from an approved sentence the code meets to the recorded paragraph; and *(A20.n, recorded, not proposed)* leading a paragraph records the paragraph. *(A20.n, fact: measured by a test: …)* and *(A20.n, fact: read from the code: …)* record a kind (iv) fact, and say which. *(A20.n, question put to the human: …)* points to an open question. *(A20: …)*, with no item number, records a fact about the code or the record and binds nothing either way; the notice under the Status line is a list item led by *(A20: notice)*, one such tag.
  - **How A20 is built, and why.** The second critique found the hand-built change list incomplete a second time, and the boundary between binding and proposed text unclear a second time, so A20 was restructured so that both hold by construction: it only inserts, every normative change is a numbered item below, only an item may carry a proposal, and each item has at least one tag at the text it concerns. **The fourth critique's simulation of taking every item literally broke the result at four sites and degraded it at five, so the tags are pointers for the reader, not instructions to apply.** **The fold's mechanics then drew findings in six critiques, from the third to the eighth, so they are owed to #116 and not stated here.** **The fifth critique found that tying A20.8's and A20.19's text to the answer to #113 failed a second time, so that form is removed: both are plain rules that hold under either answer, and the amendment that would add a fourth `blocked` reason under (a) revises them then.** **Nothing enforces this structure** (rule 7): the third and fourth critiques checked insert-only, tag-to-item resolution both ways and every quote by script, and showed that deleting an item, deleting its tag, or rewriting an approved sentence untagged all pass `go test ./test/docs/`. A `test/docs` rule with an independent golden fixture is owed, #115.
  - **The human's two decisions it records.** **#108, 2026-10-18, option (a)**: an interface that names a `tenant` is refused (A20.1); the human also let build step 3 merge ahead of this amendment. **#111, 2026-10-18**: `EvalFailed` comes before `EvalPairAlreadyFailed` (A20.14), and when the spec moves away from a failed revision the eval controller "clears the verdict and `cases[]` of the superseded run and keeps `failed[]`" (A20.15). **A20.15 also clears `score` and `at`, which the human's words do not name; that widening is the builder's**, made because both are written with the verdict and `score` is the `Eval` printer column (A15's MAJOR 1, for the run start). The human may take A20.15 as it stands or narrow it to #111's words, which is then a code change to `clearSuperseded`. A20.14 orders two reasons the human ordered, and is the one gate ordering A20 proposes; every other is A20.18's, recorded and not proposed.
  - **Every change, numbered.** The kinds and how each is decided are in "Tag forms". Each item gives: the approved text it concerns, quoted; the change; the code and the test it rests on, or that it is unpinned; the issue it answers; for a kind (ii) item, what declining it means (a kind (i) item is declined with a note that says what the human wants instead); the items it depends on; and, where there is a decision, a recommendation with its reason. The table at the end of this entry lists them in one place. Counts: kind (i), 17 (A20.1, A20.3 to A20.11, A20.13 to A20.16, A20.18 recorded and not proposed, A20.19, A20.20); kind (ii), 12 (A20.2, A20.12, A20.17, A20.21 to A20.25, A20.27, A20.28, A20.32, A20.39); kind (iii), 5 (A20.33 to A20.37); kind (iv), 4 (A20.26, A20.29 to A20.31); and one question, A20.38.
    - **A20.1** (i). Approved: "An entry whose URL carries a query or a fragment is refused, as is a URL that does not parse. So is a card with no such entry at all." Change: an entry that names a `tenant` is refused too. Code: `evalclient.EndpointPath`. Test: `TestATenantedInterfaceIsRefused`. #108, the human's decision; #108 keeps the two rejected alternatives, the tenant in the body with a new status field, and the tenant in the path. Recommended: take, because the human decided it (#108).
    - **A20.2** (ii). Approved: "The join: take only `url.Parse(entry.url).Path`, trim one trailing `/`, and append `/message:send`." Change: that join, read literally, takes the decoded path, so an agent serving `POST /a%2Fb/message:send` would be sent `/a/b/message:send` and its pair would land in `failed[]`; the code refuses any path outside an allowlist instead. Two attempts to compare the written path with the sent one each failed an independent review on PR #112, and under verify-change's two-failures rule the scope was cut to the allowlist. Cost: a card whose path carries an escape or a non-ASCII byte is not evaluated until served at an admitted path, including paths such as `/a%20b/` that would have gone on the wire as written; it buys that no `Failed` is recorded for the operator's re-encoding. Residue: an opaque URL is admitted as `/message:send`. Code: `EndpointPath`, `pathSendsAsWritten`. Test: `TestEveryByteIsSentAsWrittenOrRefused`, `TestSendPutsTheEndpointOnTheWireAsWritten`. #105 item 8. If declined: the code sends the decoded path again, which restores the defect this item documents, a `%2F` path recorded `Failed`; the decline that avoids it is #105 item 8's other design, sending `EscapedPath()` and requiring an absolute URL, a change to `EndpointPath`. Recommended: take, because the literal join records `Failed` against an honest candidate for the operator's own re-encoding.
    - **A20.3** (i). Approved: "The chosen path is recorded in `status.eval.endpointPath` and used for every case of that run". Change: at most 1024 characters, counted in runes, and no endpoint without a leading `/`. Code: `checkEndpointPath`. Test: `TestTheEndpointPathIsBounded`. #105 item 9. Depends on A20.2. Recommended: take, because a longer path cannot be recorded in the field, and one without a leading `/` is not the path sent.
    - **A20.4** (i). Approved: "From that card it takes the first `supportedInterfaces[]` entry whose `protocolBinding` is `HTTP+JSON` and whose `protocolVersion` is `1.0`." Change: a refused first entry is not passed over, and the refusals come in a stated order. Code: `EndpointPath`. Test: `TestATenantedEntryIsRefusedNotSkipped`, `TestEndpointRefusals`. #105. Depends on A20.1, A20.2 and A20.3. Recommended: take, because judging the first entry alone keeps the eval on the interface clients prefer, and a fixed order makes each refusal's message deterministic.
    - **A20.5** (i). Approved: "a fetch that did not succeed at all — a refused connection, a timeout, a body that does not parse — where no digest was compared". Change: a 5 s bound, a 1 MiB bound, and `json.Valid` before the digest. Code: `fetchCard`, `runStartCard`. Test: `TestABodyThatIsNotJSONIsAFailedFetch` pins the `json.Valid` half; the 5 s and 1 MiB bounds are unpinned, and stated as owed, #115. #102. Recommended: take, because without the bounds a slow or huge card holds a worker or its memory, and a non-JSON body is a failed fetch, not a mismatch.
    - **A20.6** (i). Approved: "Each of these refusals is written to `status.eval.blocked`, reason `EvalBindingUnsupported`." Change: a digest-matched card whose interfaces do not decode is `EvalBindingUnsupported` at `CardDriftInterval`. Code: `runStartCard`. Test: `TestARunStartRefusalIsWrittenToBlocked` pins the reason; the interval is unpinned and stated as owed, #115. #102. Recommended: take, because an undecodable interface is a stable property of the recorded card, so a 15 s retry would only repeat the refusal.
    - **A20.7** (i). Approved: "The failed-fetch message names the error class". Change: no run-start refusal carries an error's raw text or the card's URL; a failed fetch's message is the same while the failure is the same; a mismatch names the served digest. Code: `runStartCard`, `fetchCard`. Test: `TestAFailedFetchNamesItsOwnClass`. #102. Recommended: take, because a stable message keeps `blocked` from being rewritten every pass, and the card's URL is the agent's choice, not status to echo.
    - **A20.8** (i). Approved: "To the candidate revision's Service, by its ClusterIP and `spec.runtime.port`, as the card fetch does". Change: `evalclient.Check` is the one validator of that address (an IP address, a port from 1 to 65535, a request that builds), called before the run-start fetch and before every claim; when it refuses, or the candidate's Service cannot be read, the pass sends nothing, and claims and records no case. That holds under either answer to #113. The controller's branch for an error from `Send` cannot fire, because `Send` refuses exactly what `Check` refuses and `Check` admitted the case earlier in the pass; no test reaches it, which is rule 5's tension, stated. Code: `candidateAddress`, `Check`. Test: `TestAnUnusableAddressClaimsNothing`, `TestAnAddressThatBreaksMidRunClaimsNothing`, `TestCheckRefusesAnAddressThatIsNotAnIPAndAPort`. #105 items 11 and 12. If declined: the human's note gives the validation wanted instead, and a code change to `candidateAddress` and `Check` follows. Recommended: take, because an address fault is the operator's, and recording it would fail the candidate for good through `failed[]`.
    - **A20.9** (i). Approved: "the header `A2A-Version: 1.0`". Change: `Content-Type: application/a2a+json` is sent too, a SHOULD at `v1.0.1` §11.1 (`docs/specification.md` at tag `v1.0.1`: "application/a2a+json SHOULD be used for requests and responses"). Code: `Send`. Test: `TestTheRequest`. #105 item 10. Recommended: take, because §11.1 at `v1.0.1` says SHOULD.
    - **A20.10** (i). Approved: "`outcome` is `Sending` between a claim and its record (step 10)", and the outcome table's "pass" and "fail". Change: `Passed` and `Failed`, with the CRD enum `Sending;Passed;Failed`. Code: `EvalCaseOutcome`. Test: the CRD enum, generated. #105 item 3. Depends on A20.15. Recommended: take, because a CRD enum makes the stored spelling a contract the API server enforces.
    - **A20.11** (i). Approved: "a refused connection, or no answer within `caseTimeoutSeconds` | fail | `Unreachable`". Change: every failure before a status line, and a timeout during a `200`'s body, is `Unreachable`. Code: `Send`. Test: `TestOutcomeTable`, `TestABodyThatBreaksOffIsAProtocolError`. #105 item 2. Recommended: take, because none of those failures is the candidate's protocol fault, and reading them as `ProtocolError` would blame it.
    - **A20.12** (ii). Approved: "a body that is not a `SendMessageResponse` | fail | `ProtocolError`". Change: the code reads only the answer path, strictly, and scores some bodies protojson would refuse; full protojson validation against an embedded `a2a.proto` descriptor was measured working by a reviewer and not taken. Code: `score` in `internal/evalclient/response.go`. Test: `TestOutcomeTable`. #105 items 4 and 7. If declined: `score` validates the whole body with protojson against an embedded `a2a.proto` descriptor, a change to `internal/evalclient/response.go`. Recommended: take, because the answer path is what decides the outcome, and the stricter reading was measured working and can be taken later without a schema change.
    - **A20.13** (i). Approved: "The claim is bounded by 5 s, the send by `caseTimeoutSeconds`, and the record by 5 s including its retries." Change: a caller must not bound a send tighter, a constraint nothing enforces. Code: `Send`. Test: `TestACallerDeadlineIsUnreachable`. #105 item 5. Recommended: take, because it states the constraint the client documents, so a later caller does not change what `Unreachable` means.
    - **A20.14** (i). Approved: "A pair in `failed[]` never starts a run again, reason `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`." Change: `EvalFailed` comes first while the record names the pair. Code: `decideGate`. Test: `TestTheGateBranchsReasonsTakeTheirPrecedence`. #111, the human's decision. Recommended: take, because the human decided it (#111).
    - **A20.15** (i). Approved: "So reverting a failed revision B to A and re-applying B does not re-run B." Change: a superseded `Failed` record is cleared, with the trigger and the fields stated, `score` and `at` included as the builder's widening. A `Passed` record is not cleared, because the review of PR #112 measured that doing so makes a serving revision that passed read `ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated` and re-sends a passed, unpromoted candidate its cases. Code: `clearSuperseded`. Test: `TestAFailedPairIsFinalAndASuiteEditReopensIt`, `TestAServingRevisionKeepsItsPassedRecordAcrossAnAbandonedCandidate`, `TestAPassedCandidateIsNotSentItsCasesAgainAfterAnEditAndBack`. #111, the human's decision, widened. Recommended: take, keeping the `score` and `at` clearing, because a cleared verdict beside a stale score and timestamp would be loud and wrong (rule 8). Depends on A20.14.
    - **A20.16** (i). Approved: "If a field did not come back, it sends nothing, writes nothing more, and returns `RequeueAfter: CardDriftInterval`." Change: a `Running` record missing `endpointPath` or `cardDigest` starts the run again; without it a later pass would send to the path `""`. Code: `Reconcile`. Test: `TestARunStartRecordThatDoesNotComeBackSendsNothing`. #102. Recommended: take, because without it a pruned record is sent to the path `""`.
    - **A20.17** (ii). Approved: "Absent means `threshold: "1"`: every case must pass", and "with defaults applied, object keys sorted, and cases sorted by `id`". Change: the digest applies only the CRD's defaults, not §1.1's stated one, so an absent metric and `threshold: "1"` digest differently, a threshold's spelling counts, and the form is a compatibility contract. Code: `internal/evalsuite/digest.go`. Test: `TestSuiteDigestIsPinned`, `TestSuiteDigestPinsItsEscaping`. #102. If declined: `Digest` writes an absent metric as `threshold: "1"` before hashing, a change to `internal/evalsuite/digest.go` that re-opens, on upgrade, every recorded failed pair whose suite has no `metrics`; pairs whose suite writes a metric digest as before. Recommended: take, because the change a decline needs re-opens every recorded failed pair whose suite has no `metrics`.
    - **A20.18** (i, recorded, not proposed). Approved: "`EvalRecordNotKept` and `EvalRecordUnverifiable` come first." Change: none to the quoted sentence, which the code meets, so this item has no decision. It records the rest of the order as built, where §1.1 is silent; that order has no exempt state. Build step 7 adds the exempt state, as "What the gate branch derives in the exempt state" states it; the amendment that accompanies build step 7 proposes the final order. Code: `decideGate`, whose comment calls the order interim. Test: `TestTheGateBranchsReasonsTakeTheirPrecedence`, `TestAPassedVerdictOutranksWhatARunWouldWaitFor`. #102. A20.22 depends on it.
    - **A20.19** (i). Approved: "`False/EvalRunning`, naming the pair and N, in the gate branch." Change: the message says that `status.eval.cases[]` records the outcomes so far, and asserts nothing about cases being sent. Under #113 (a) the amendment that adds the fourth `blocked` reason revises this sentence then, as any later amendment would. Code: `decideGate`. Test: `TestRunningNamesNAndPausedNamesKOfN`. #113. Recommended: take, because the message must not claim a send it cannot see.
    - **A20.20** (i). Approved: the stale-reader row's mutation "claim nothing and trust the read". Change: the row also names dropping the optimistic lock, which build step 6's mutation table on #82 records killed there. Test: `TestAStaleReadNeverResendsARecordedCase`. #102. Recommended: take, because the row then names the mutation that kills it.
    - **A20.21** (ii). Approved: "The rules, each owed as CEL on the CRD, with a refusal that names the fix". Change: the count and length bounds are the schema's, because the API server estimates CEL cost from them: on envtest's API server 1.36.2, with `metrics` unbounded and the threshold at `maxLength: 16`, the threshold's rules exceeded the cost budget by factors of 6.7 and 3.1 and the CRD was refused. Code: `api/v1alpha1/evalsuite_types.go`. Test: `TestAdmissionRefusesEachMalformedSuiteAndASecondGate`. #102. If declined: CEL rules with messages that name the fix are added beside the schema bounds, which stay, because CEL in their place is what the API server refused; a change to `api/v1alpha1/evalsuite_types.go`. Recommended: take, because CEL in place of the bounds was refused by the API server, and a CEL copy beside them could never fire.
    - **A20.22** (ii). Approved: "| a run exists and the candidate is not available | `False/EvalPaused`, naming k of N, in the not-ready branches". Change: `EvalPaused` also before a run, and the not-ready branches report the gate's whole answer, so a new gated Agent carries `GatesPassed` before its revision is available. Code: `gateDecision.report`. Test: `TestPausedCountsOnlyTheCurrentPairsRun`. #102. If declined: the not-ready branches set `EvalPaused` only when a run exists and nothing otherwise, a change to their calls of `gateDecision.report`; a new gated Agent then carries no `GatesPassed` until its revision is available. Depends on A20.18. Recommended: take, because otherwise a new gated Agent carries no `GatesPassed` until its revision is available.
    - **A20.23** (ii). Approved: "it flips to `False/NoGatesDeclared`, naming the revision now serving". Change: names none except on a promoting pass, because `assessGates` runs before the pin and the switch. Code: `assessGates`. Test: `TestRemovingTheGatePromotesAFailedCandidateAndSaysSo`. #102. If declined: `assessGates` names `status.activeRevision` when it sets the condition, a small change to `assessGates` in `agent_controller.go`. Recommended: decline, because the approved message names the serving revision and so tells the reader more; the built message is true, and not a silent degraded path, only less informative, and declining costs one small change to `assessGates`.
    - **A20.24** (ii). Approved: "because the gate was added after it began serving". Change: also after a revert to a revision that passed; the message says what, not why. Code: `assessPromotedGate`. Test: `TestAGateAddedOverAServingRevisionSaysItWasNotEvaluated`. #102. If declined: the revert state comes from the approved run-start write itself (step 1), which replaces the whole of `status.eval`; meeting the row would need that write to keep a serving revision's `Passed` record, a change to approved step 1 and to `startRun`. Otherwise the row is kept and is false in that state. Recommended: take, because the state arises from the approved run-start write itself, so the honest message says what is recorded.
    - **A20.25** (ii). Approved: "it leaves `failed[]`, which is keyed by pair and carries finality (step 7), and `blocked` and `blockedFor`, which every pass computes for itself." Change: the run start writes `blocked` empty and `blockedFor` for the new pair; `failed[]` is still left. Code: `startRun`. Test: unpinned, owed, #115. #102. If declined: the run start leaves `blocked` and `blockedFor` as they were, a change to `startRun`; a stale refusal then stands beside a started run until the next pass rewrites it. Recommended: take, because a pass that starts a run has computed that nothing blocks it, so a stale refusal would stand beside a started run.
    - **A20.26** (iv, a fact read from the code). Approved: "implemented once and called by both". Change: none proposed. Read from the code, not measured, the I1 predicate is read one way wherever both controllers judge it: on every pass that reaches `authHoldsPromotion`, the pin is resolved exactly when `spec.release.targetRevisionDigest` is set, because `resolveReleasePin` returns `unresolvablePinError` for a set pin that does not resolve, and the Agent reconciler then reports `ReleasePinUnresolvable` and returns before the I1 hold. The readings differ only in that state, which the Agent reconciler never judges. There the eval controller takes the pin as set from `targetRevisionDigest` alone, so its I1 predicate turns the hold off (`i1Hold`, `sendpredicates.go`): a run can start on the recorded candidate, its verdict outlives the state, and a `Failed` verdict puts the pair in `failed[]` for good. That is the cost. Code: `Reconcile`, `i1Hold`, `resolveReleasePin`, `authHoldsPromotion`. Test: unpinned; owed on #115. #102. No decision: this records a fact; having the eval controller hold while a set pin does not resolve would be a later amendment.
    - **A20.27** (ii). When the candidate's Service cannot be read, or its ClusterIP is `None`, the eval controller sends nothing and claims and records no case (A20.8), and returns the error (A20.28); no `blocked` reason names the state, so the Agent reads `EvalRunning`, whose message is silent rather than wrong (A20.19), and only the log carries the cause. #113 frames this as mid-run only; read from `decideGate`, not measured, it holds at run start too, because `candidateAddress` runs before the run-start fetch, and `TestAnUnusableAddressClaimsNothing` measures only that such a pass fetches and writes nothing. Two exceptions make it worse: a refusal for the same pair still standing in `blocked` goes on being reported with its old cause, because `startRun` returns before `syncBlocked` (rule 8); and the message points at a `cases[]` that may hold another pair's record. How long it lasts, read from the code and not measured: `ensureService` recreates a missing Service, and replaces a headless one the operator recorded, on the Agent reconciler's next pass, which an Agent or Deployment event or its card re-read requeue brings, since it watches no Service; but a second replacement within `ServiceReplaceCooldown`, 10 minutes, is held and reported instead, a replacement the dry-run create is refused is not made, and a headless Service the operator has no record of is refused and reported (`internal/controller/service.go`). **(a)** A fourth `blocked` reason, for example `EvalCandidateUnaddressable`, first in `blocked`'s precedence. Cost: a reason and its `reasons.yaml` entry; a case in the gate branch's closed `blocked` switch, which would otherwise pass it through to `EvalRunning`, with a `GatesPassed` row and its `Ready` reason; a `blocked` write when the state begins and when it ends; moving `candidateAddress`'s failure into `syncBlocked` in `startRun` and `nextCase`, choosing what the pass returns, and keeping a transient Service read error from flapping `blocked`; and, under (a), a short amendment, owed, that adds the fourth reason and revises A20.19's sentence, plus a code PR with a row that fails if the state reads `EvalRunning`; #113 stays open until that is built. **(b)** A stated residue: `EvalRunning` keeps its message and the log carries the cause. Cost: a held candidate reads like a run in progress until the Service is repaired — one Agent reconciler pass in the common case, up to the 10-minute cooldown for a repeated replacement, and indefinitely for a refused replacement or a Service the operator does not own, though those two are reported on the Agent separately — and the two exceptions stand. Under (b), this residue is drafted for §1.1: "When the candidate's Service cannot be read, or `evalclient.Check` refuses its address, no `blocked` reason names that state, so the Agent reads `EvalRunning` until the Service is repaired, and only the eval controller's log carries the cause." **Recommended: (a)**: (b) is the silent degraded path NFR-8 and rule 8 forbid, and its two exceptions name a wrong cause. Until the human decides, PR #112 ships (b)'s message and the log line. Tag: the `blocked` row of the status-field table. Depends on A20.8 and A20.28.

- **A21 (2026-10-19, the drafter's; decided by the human on 2026-10-21, as recommended; accompanies build step 7, PR #118, `d2d1a17`) — the gate branch's final order proposed, §1.1 brought level with build step 7, and one question put to the human.** Build step 7, the J2 or K2 `Lock` exemption with its clause 5, clause 5's hold in `status.eval.blocked` and the missing-policy wedge message's exits, merged as PR #118 at `d2d1a17`, after four independent reviews on that PR, the fourth of which passed it. Building it made sentences of §1.1 false and made choices §1.1 does not state. The PR body's list "For the accompanying amendment (A21)" and the reviews' fix tables recorded them, and A21 carries them in. A20.18 said that this amendment would propose the gate branch's final order, and A21.1 to A21.3 do. **It was proposed on 2026-10-19, stacked on A20 so that the human decided both together, and decided by the human on 2026-10-21** ("Decided", below); where an A21 item rests on an A20 item, it names it.
  - **Decided 2026-10-21 by the human, as recommended (A20.23 declined → #121; A20.38 (a) → #113, built under #123; A21.4 (b) → #122); folded into §1.1 by this change.** The human's words, on PR #120: "approved as recommended". Every kind (i) and kind (ii) item is taken as the decision table at the end of this entry recommends. A21.4 is answered (b): a follow-up amendment and code PR move the failed rows to (4), ahead of every other exempt-state row, and remove the clause-5 reads on final-verdict and failed-pair passes (#122); until they merge, the order §1.1 states is the order as built, and §1.1 tags it. The kind (iv) facts are written into §1.1 as facts. A20's entry lists the tags that outlive the fold. The bullets below describe the text as proposed, before the fold.
  - **Tag forms.** A21 uses A20's terms, tag forms, kinds of item and deciding, defined once in the first bullet of A20's entry, "Tag forms", with `A21` in place of `A20`. Nothing here redefines them. *(A21: …)*, with no item number, records a fact and binds nothing either way; the notice under the Status line is a list item led by *(A21: notice)*.
