A20.34's answer to every recorded `Create` or `Lock` ahead of `blocked` ended when the gate branch gained the exempt state, whose order A21.2 states. A20.35's `blocked` without clause 5 ended when the eval controller began writing clause 5's hold (`TestAGatewayLevelPolicyHoldsTheExemptLockAcrossItsDeadline`, `TestAForeignTrafficPolicyHoldsTheExemptLockAcrossItsDeadline`). **A20.18's recorded paragraph, "The gate branch's order, as built", is superseded** by the paragraph A21.1 to A21.3 insert after it, "The gate branch's order", which proposes the final order. A20's notice under the Status line still counts five departures that build steps 7 to 9 end; three have ended, and A20.36 and A20.37 remain until build steps 8 and 9. A20's tags for A20.18 and A20.33 to A20.35 stay in §1.1, now describing departures that have ended. A20's build-step-4 fact tag in "What clause 5 costs", that the four bodies "are byte for byte what they were, and stay where design 03 cites them: `authabove.go:199`", is now false of `gatewayAuthPolicies`, and A21.14 supersedes it. What the fold (#116) does with each of these is #116's to specify.
  - **How A21 is built, and why.** As A20 is, for A20's reasons ("How A20 is built, and why"): it only inserts; every normative change is a numbered item below, and only an item carries a proposal; each item has at least one tag at the text it concerns; the tags are pointers for the reader, not instructions to apply; and the fold's mechanics are owed to #116, not stated here. Insert-only with A20's and A21's insertions both stripped, tag-to-item resolution both ways, and every quote of approved text were checked by script against `875c1a3`, A20's base, since which the approved text has not changed. Nothing enforces this structure (rule 7); the `test/docs` rule that would is owed, #115. **One choice is A21's own**: the order of a failed pair against clause 5's hold in the exempt state is put to the human as a question about a follow-up, A21.4, because the reviews of PR #118 left the code on the approved order and named the other order as the human's to choose. **A21's items and tags state the code as built, unconditionally**; the follow-up's specification lives inside A21.4 alone, with the list of items and tags it would rewrite, because threading a hypothetical answer through tags is the form A20 withdrew after its fifth critique, and A21's second critique found it failing here again. **A21.4 carries no argued costs**: under verify-change's two-failures rule, after a cost argued in it was found false in critiques 1, 3 and 4, it keeps only the question, the state as built, (b)'s specification and what it rewrites, and a list of facts, each measured, read from the code, or derived from (b)'s specification, with its source. Critique 5 then found a derived fact false, and it was replaced by that critique's measurement.
  - **Every change, numbered.** The kinds and how each is decided are in A20's "Tag forms". Each item gives the approved text it concerns, quoted; the change; the code and the test it rests on, or that it is unpinned; for a kind (ii) item, what declining it means; the items it depends on; and, where there is a decision, a recommendation with its reason. "Review n" is the nth independent review on PR #118. Counts: kind (i), 4 (A21.9, A21.10, A21.13, A21.16); kind (ii), 9 (A21.1 to A21.3, A21.5 to A21.7, A21.11, A21.14, A21.17); kind (iv), 3 (A21.8, A21.12, A21.15); and one question, A21.4.
    - **A21.1** (ii). Approved: "the gate branch applies `blocked` only while `blockedFor` matches on all three parts, as above, and otherwise takes the table's rows". Change: outside the exempt state, the whole order, first match winning: (1) `EvalRecordUnverifiable`, then `EvalRecordNotKept`; (2) `EvalSuiteNotFound`, then `EvalSuiteUnreadable`; (3) a `Passed` verdict for the pair, satisfied with its card recorded and `EvalAwaitingCard` without; (4) `EvalFailed`, then `EvalPairAlreadyFailed` (A20.14); (5) no card recorded for the candidate, `EvalAwaitingCard`; (6) a recorded `Create` or `Lock` that is not exempt, `EvalWaitingForAuth` (A21.16); (7) the candidate not available, `EvalPaused` (A21.3); (8) `blocked`, while `blockedFor` matches; (9) `EvalRunning`. It is A20.18's recorded order with that order's (8), `EvalPaused` and otherwise `EvalRunning`, split around `blocked`: the candidate's availability moves ahead of `blocked`, and `EvalRunning` stays after it. A21 reads the approved sentence as A21.3 does, as an order: `blocked` first, then the table's rows. On that reading the code departs from it at (4), (5), (6) and (7), which it answers before `blocked`; read as silent on the order, A20.18's reading, it would be a rule where §1.1 is silent. One reading serves both items, so this one is kind (ii). Why: (1) to (3) are the two orderings §1.1 fixes elsewhere; (4) comes before (5) and (8) because a failed pair holds whatever card is recorded and whatever `blocked` says; (5) comes before (8) because the eval controller computes no `blocked` on a pass whose candidate has no card recorded; (5) comes before (6) and (7) because a candidate with no card recorded cannot be evaluated whatever the transaction or its availability does; (6) comes before (7) and (8) because the eval controller computes nothing while that transaction is recorded (A21.7), so a `blocked` beside it was computed before it began; (7) before (8) is A21.3. Code: `decideGate` and its comment. Test: `TestTheGateBranchsReasonsTakeTheirPrecedence`, whose row "not available, beside a matched blocked" pins (7) before (8), and `TestAPassedVerdictOutranksWhatARunWouldWaitFor`. PR #118's list, item 1. If declined: a `blocked` whose `blockedFor` matches is answered right after (3), a change to `decideGate`; a stored run-start refusal, or a hold kept across a NACK excursion (A21.8), would then be shown over a failed pair, which neither can reopen, and over a recorded transaction or an unavailable candidate, beside which nothing recomputes it. Depends on A20.14 and A21.3. Recommended: take, because the reason shown should be the one that holds.
    - **A21.2** (ii). Approved: "If `blockedFor` does not match, it sets `GatesPassed=False/EvalWaitingForAuth`, whose message says the eval controller has not yet evaluated clause 5 for this `Lock`.", "If `blocked` is set, it sets `blocked`'s reason, with `blocked.message`." and "Otherwise it takes the table's rows, from the run". Change: in the exempt state, after A21.1's (1) to (3): (4) no card recorded for the candidate, `EvalAwaitingCard`; (5) the candidate not available, `EvalFailed`, then `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`, else `EvalPaused` (A21.3); (6) `blockedFor` does not match, the not-yet-evaluated `EvalWaitingForAuth`; (7) `blocked`, with its reason (A21.5); (8) `EvalFailed`, then `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`; (9) `EvalRunning`. It departs from the approved derivation, which begins at `blockedFor`, by putting the no-card row and the unavailable candidate ahead of it. The no-card row is ahead because the eval controller evaluates clause 5 only on a pass on which a card is recorded for the candidate, so `blockedFor` cannot come to match while none is; the unavailable candidate is A21.3. Rows (6) to (8) keep the approved derivation's order; A21.4 asks whether a follow-up should change it. Cost: beside an exempt `Lock`, a pair that has failed and has no card recorded reads `EvalAwaitingCard`, where outside the exempt state it reads `EvalFailed`; among the states with an unavailable candidate that review 3 checked, it found no other on which the two orders answer differently, apart from A21.4's. Code: `decideGate`. Test: `TestTheExemptStateTakesItsOwnPrecedence`, `TestBlockedAppliesInTheExemptStateAndNotBeyondIt`, `TestAGatewayLevelPolicyHoldsTheExemptLockAcrossItsDeadline`. PR #118's list, items 1 and 2. If declined: the derivation comes before the no-card row, a change to `decideGate`; an exempt `Lock` with no card recorded then reads the not-yet-evaluated `EvalWaitingForAuth` for as long as no card is recorded, naming a clause-5 evaluation that cannot happen when the cause is the card (rule 8). Declining the unavailable row is A21.3's decline. Depends on A21.3. Recommended: take, because the reason shown should be the one that holds.
    - **A21.3** (ii). Approved: "The rollout step's not-ready branches come before the gate.", and the two derivations A21.1 and A21.2 quote. Change: in both states a candidate that is not available is answered before `blockedFor` and `blocked`: outside the exempt state after the transaction row, and in it after the no-card row, with its `Failed` verdict and `failed[]` entry first. The approved text disagrees with itself about this state: step 8 puts the not-ready branches first, and the derivations put `blocked` first. The code takes step 8. Why: no pass on which the candidate is unavailable computes `blocked`, because the eval controller returns at the availability predicate, before clause 5 and before any run start; so a `blocked` that matches was computed before the candidate went away. Applied, a clause-5 hold would name a cause that may have gone for as long as the candidate stays away, with no bound; review 1 measured that, its MAJOR 1. The first clause-5 pass after the candidate comes back re-reads the hold, and clears it if it has gone. Code: `decideGate`, `Reconcile`. Test: `TestAHoldIsNotAppliedToAnUnavailableCandidate`, which kills both answering `blocked` first and skipping the clearing write; `TestTheGateBranchsReasonsTakeTheirPrecedence`; `TestTheExemptStateTakesItsOwnPrecedence`. Review 1, MAJOR 1; review 2, MINOR A. If declined: `blocked` is answered before `EvalPaused` in both states, a change to `decideGate`, which brings back review 1's MAJOR, a hold naming a removed policy for as long as the candidate is unavailable, unless the eval controller also writes `blocked` on a pass whose candidate is unavailable — review 1's other fix, a change to `Reconcile`, in one of two variants: writing `blocked` empty with `blockedFor`, which costs one write when the candidate goes away and no read, but then shows no hold that still stands when the candidate comes back until the next clause-5 pass; or re-reading clause 5, which adds its reads to every paused pass. Depends on A20.22. Recommended: take, because it is what step 8 says, and it costs no read.
    - **A21.4** (question), put to the human and not proposed: **should a later amendment and code change answer a pair that has failed ahead of clause 5's hold in the exempt state?** Approved: "Otherwise it takes the table's rows, from the run". Question: (b) is a follow-up that moves the failed rows ahead of the derivation in the exempt state; (a) is none. A21's items and tags state the order as built, whatever the answer. Tag: the exempt derivation's "Otherwise it takes the table's rows". Depends on A21.1 and A21.2.
      - **As built.** The exempt state answers the not-yet-evaluated row and `blocked` before `EvalFailed` and `EvalPairAlreadyFailed` (A21.2's (6) to (8)), as the approved derivation orders them; outside it a failed pair comes before `blocked` (A21.1's (4)). Beside an exempt `Lock` whose pair has failed, a standing hold therefore reads `GatesPassed=False/EvalWaitingForAuth`, and on each NACK excursion of the `Lock` `GatesPassed` flips between that and `EvalFailed` (review 2, MINOR C; reasoned, not measured). The messages are A21.5's; the clause-5 reads on final-verdict and failed-pair passes, and their interval, are A21.11's.
      - **(b), the specification.** The exempt order becomes (4) `EvalFailed`, then `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`; (5) no card recorded, `EvalAwaitingCard`; (6) the candidate not available, `EvalPaused`; (7) `blockedFor` does not match, the not-yet-evaluated `EvalWaitingForAuth`; (8) `blocked`; (9) `EvalRunning`. That is A21.1's order without the transaction row, and with the not-yet-evaluated row before `blocked`. The eval controller makes no clause-5 read on a pass whose pair has a final verdict, `Passed` or `Failed`, or is in `failed[]`; such a pass writes `blocked` empty with `blockedFor` for the pair only when that differs from what is stored, as the same pass does outside the exempt state, and returns `CardDriftInterval`. Code that goes dead, and is deleted (rule 5), as of `d2d1a17`: `holdFailedTail`; the not-yet-evaluated message's failed-pair branch; `failedBehind`; `pairHasFailed`; the `failedRows` calls in the unavailable case (`gate.go:299`) and after `blocked` (`:349`); and the `!exempt` guard on the first `failedRows` call (`:274`), which becomes unconditional.
      - **(b), what it rewrites.** A21.2, A21.3, A21.5, A21.6, A21.11 and A21.12; the A21 tags in "What wakes the eval controller while it waits", "What the gate branch derives in the exempt state" and "What clause 5 costs"; "The gate branch's order"; the tests `TestTheExemptStateTakesItsOwnPrecedence`, `TestAHoldAfterAFailedVerdictComesBeforeIt`, `TestAHoldBesideAFailedPairComesBeforeIt` and `TestAFailedPairIsNamedByWhatRecordsIt`, which tests only the wordings that go dead; and `reasons.yaml`'s `EvalWaitingForAuth` entry.
      - **Facts the human decides on.** Each was measured once by a probe in a critique of A21, read from the code, or follows from (b)'s specification, as stated. Design 03's `TestAJ2LockHoldsWhileAGatewayLevelAuthPolicyStands` pins `PolicyApplyIncomplete` and `Ready` at `GatewayAuthPolicy`, `GovernanceSkipped` at `AuthLockPending` and phase `Degraded` before the deadline, and `TestTheDeadlineOutcomeNamesTheGatewayLevelCause` (its `J2 Lock` case) pins `AuthLockUnverified` past it; `TestAGatewayLevelPolicyHoldsTheExemptLockAcrossItsDeadline` asserts only `PolicyApplyIncomplete`'s reason. Unpinned, and owed on #115: the `Degraded` condition's reason before the deadline, where the tests pin only the phase, and the phase and the `Degraded` reason past it, which no test pins; `GovernanceSkipped` past the deadline; the failed-list readings; `Ready=True/Available` beside a `Failed` verdict; and fact (iv), its handover shape included.
        - (i) Measured by a probe in the first critique: beside an exempt `Lock` and a `Failed` verdict with no hold, `Ready=True/Available`; under a Gateway-level hold before the `Lock`'s deadline, `Ready=False/GatewayAuthPolicy`. A probe in the fourth critique measured `Degraded` and `PolicyApplyIncomplete` at design 03's `GatewayAuthPolicy` there too.
        - (ii) Measured by a probe in the fourth critique: past the `Lock`'s deadline, 4 minutes, `runLock` sets `AuthLockUnverified` (`authtxn.go`). For a Gateway-level policy, `Ready`, `Degraded` and `PolicyApplyIncomplete` read `AuthLockUnverified` and `GovernanceSkipped` reads `AuthLockPending`, every message naming the policy; `TestAGatewayLevelPolicyHoldsTheExemptLockAcrossItsDeadline` already asserts `AuthLockUnverified` past the deadline. For a failed run-namespace policy list, `Ready` and `Degraded` read `RouteApplyFailed`, and `PolicyApplyIncomplete` stays `AuthLockUnverified` with `RouteApplyFailed` appended. A probe in the third critique measured, before the deadline, all three at `RouteApplyFailed` for that failed list.
        - (iii) From (b)'s specification: beside a final-verdict or failed-pair candidate, nothing that only the eval controller's reads would meet is recorded or shown.
        - (iv) Measured by a probe in the fifth critique, with one leader: a verdict is written only on a pass that found no hold, so `blocked` is empty when it lands, as built and under (b); a hold planted before the first pass sends no case and lands no verdict. The one reachable shape with one leader is a policy appearing while the last case is in flight: as built, the next pass reads clause 5 and writes the hold, then writes `blocked` empty when the policy goes, two writes; under (b), no read and no write. Read from the code and not measured, a leader handover gives a second shape: the new leader's pass finds a hold and writes it without recording `Interrupted`, so the claim stays `Sending`, and the old leader's record, retried after its `Conflict`, finds the claim standing and writes the verdict with the hold still stored (step 10's record). There (b) writes `blocked` empty on the next pass without reading clause 5, and the code as built writes it empty once the policy goes: one write either way. Switching to (b) also costs one write, on the first verdict or failed-pair pass that finds a stored hold.
        - Of the Agent's conditions, only `GatesPassed` differs between (a) and (b); `status.eval.blocked` differs too.
      - **Recommended: (b)**, on structural grounds only: both states order a failed pair alike, the `GatesPassed` flip on an excursion ends, and no clause-5 read is made on a verdict pass. The drafter's argued costs and benefits were struck after three of them were found false; the human decides on the facts listed above.
    - **A21.5** (ii). Approved: "If `blocked` is set, it sets `blocked`'s reason, with `blocked.message`." and "If `blockedFor` does not match, it sets `GatesPassed=False/EvalWaitingForAuth`, whose message says the eval controller has not yet evaluated clause 5 for this `Lock`." Change: two wordings for a pair that has already failed, both from the pass's own state. First, the not-yet-evaluated message, beside a failed pair, says that clause 5 has not yet been evaluated, that the pair has already failed so no case of it is claimed whatever clause 5 finds, and names what records the failure, which stands behind the condition. Second, for a pair that has already failed, the gate branch carries `blocked.message` with its closing sentence — "No case is claimed until it clears; a case already in flight is not stopped, and a Passed verdict already written still promotes" — replaced by one that says no case of the pair is claimed whether or not the hold clears, and names what records the failure, which is shown again once the hold clears: "its Failed verdict", or "its entry in status.eval.failed[]" once #111's clear has taken the verdict (A20.15). Why: the eval controller builds the hold from clause 5's reads alone, never from the verdict, so the hold's text is the same whether or not the pair has failed, and the gate branch words the tail from the pass's own state. Code: `decideGate`, `holdClaimsTail`, `holdFailedTail`, `failedBehind`. Test: `TestTheExemptStateTakesItsOwnPrecedence`, `TestAFailedPairIsNamedByWhatRecordsIt`, `TestAHoldAfterAFailedVerdictComesBeforeIt`, `TestAHoldBesideAFailedPairComesBeforeIt`. Review 2, MINOR C; review 3, MINOR 3. If declined: either the gate branch carries `blocked.message` unchanged, whose "until it clears" is false of a failed pair (rule 8), a change to `decideGate`; or the eval controller writes the failed-pair wording into `blocked` itself, a change to `clause5Hold` and `Reconcile`. A follow-up under A21.4 (b) would delete both wordings. Recommended: take.
    - **A21.6** (ii). Approved: "The eval controller computes it once per pass, from every reason, in this precedence, and writes it only when the computed value differs from the stored one" and "`blockedFor` is written in the same patch as `blocked`, and on every pass whose pair or `Lock` differs from the stored one." Change: `blocked` and `blockedFor` are written at most once per pass, and only on a pass on which every shared send predicate holds (A21.7). A pass under a hold writes the hold. A run-start pass writes the refusal, or the run-start patch, which sets `blocked` empty and `blockedFor` for the new pair (A20.25). A pass whose run start stops at an unusable candidate address — its Service cannot be read, or `evalclient.Check` refuses its address (A20.8) — clears a stored `EvalWaitingForAuth` hold, and writes nothing else. A pass of a running run writes `blocked` empty. A final-verdict or failed-pair pass writes the hold or empty. A pass whose clause-5 read fails writes the hold (A21.12). Review 4 tabled every exit of a pass and found none that writes twice. That keeps "once per pass" and "one per change of `blocked` or `blockedFor`" ("It does not bring back N4's contention"). The unusable-address path has two consequences, neither measured. After its clear, `blockedFor` matches and `blocked` is empty, so the exempt state reads `EvalRunning` on that pass and after it, though no run started and no card was fetched; review 4 reasoned this from `decideGate` and did not observe it. And where no hold was stored, `blockedFor` is left as it was, even when it names another pair or `Lock`, against the second sentence quoted: in the exempt state that reads as not yet evaluated though the pass evaluated clause 5, and a stored run-start refusal for the same pair goes on being reported, A20.38's first exception. Both are the state #113 asks about (A20.38): under its (a) the fourth `blocked` reason would be this path's one write and replace both readings, and under its (b) both join the stated residue. Code: `syncBlocked`, `startRun`. Test: `TestARunStartPassWritesBlockedOnce` (a refused fetch and an unusable address, one resourceVersion step each), `TestAHoldFoundGoneIsClearedBeforeARunStartThatStopsShort`, `TestARunStartRefusalAndClauseFiveShareOneSlot`, `TestAnUnusableAddressClaimsNothing`; the two readings are unpinned, owed on #115. Review 3, MINOR 1; review 4, its first divergence. If declined: the unusable-address path writes `blockedFor` whenever it differs, a change to `startRun`. That meets the second sentence quoted, but writes `blocked` empty beside the address fault, so the state reads `EvalRunning` there in more cases, not fewer; the remedy for both readings is #113 (a), not a decline. Depends on A20.8, A20.25, A20.38 and A21.7. Recommended: take, under either answer to #113: under its (a) the fourth reason replaces both readings, and under its (b) they join the stated residue.
    - **A21.7** (ii). Approved: "Any pass on which clauses 1 to 4 fail clears the hold, and that is the rule that does the work.", "and a standing one is cleared" and "Examples of that rule, not a closed list". Change: only a pass that reaches the computation clears the hold: one on which every shared send predicate holds and the suite is read, so one with no `Create` or `Lock` recorded, or with an exempt one. A pass that waits on a recorded `Create` or `Lock` that is not exempt reads and writes nothing, `blocked` included, so it clears nothing. A standing hold is masked meanwhile rather than cleared: the gate branch answers that transaction first (A21.1 (6)), and once the `Lock` ends `blockedFor`'s deadline no longer matches, so it is not applied. So a standing hold is rewritten by the first pass after it that reaches the computation — cleared if that pass finds no hold, written again if the exempt state has come back and clause 5 still holds (A21.8) — and not by the pass that finds the exempt state gone. Of the rule's five examples: the `Lock` advancing past `ProbingAfter` to `Served` removes the record, and so does a spec edit whose abandonment completes, in the Agent pass that observes the edit — a J2 `Lock` gives way to the recorded mode, and a K2 `Lock` to a refused `Adopt`, which does not count — so the next eval pass that reaches the computation rewrites the hold; a stage regression and a card recorded for the active revision leave the `Lock` recorded and not exempt, so the eval controller writes nothing beside it; and once the gate is removed it returns before any write. An abandonment that waits on a finalizer ends nothing: the `Lock` stays recorded at `ProbingAfter`, still meets clauses 1 to 4 on its record, and is claimed beside, as §1.1's `abandonWaiting` row says. The approved example "when a spec edit abandons the `Lock`" therefore holds only for an abandonment that completes. The same holds of "`blockedFor` is written … on every pass whose pair or `Lock` differs" (A21.6). Why: an eval write beside a transaction that is not exempt is the contention the transaction predicate exists to prevent ("Why", under "On the Agent being evaluated"). Code: `Reconcile`, which returns before any read or write when a recorded transaction is not exempt. Test: `TestALockThatIsNotExemptHoldsThePassBeforeAnyRead`, `TestAStaleHoldClearsWhenTheLockIsAbandoned`, which asserts that no transaction is recorded right after the abandoning edit, and `TestALockAbandonedBehindAFinalizerIsClaimedBeside`. PR #118's list, item 3. If declined: a pass that waits on a transaction writes `blocked` empty, a change to `Reconcile`, which puts an eval patch beside the status writes of the transaction the rule keeps the eval off. Recommended: take, because the approved rule's purpose is that wait.
    - **A21.8** (iv, a fact read from the code). Approved: "So a pass that finds the exempt state gone computes no `EvalWaitingForAuth`, and a standing one is cleared." Change: none proposed. Read from the code, and reasoned by review 1 (MINOR 2), not measured: a `Lock` that leaves `ProbingAfter` on a fresh NACK and comes back keeps its deadline, so a `blocked` and `blockedFor` written before the excursion match again, and the gate branch shows the old value until the next eval pass — a hold whose cause may have gone, or an empty slot that reads `EvalRunning` while clause 5 now holds. The window is one eval pass: the status write that brings the `Lock` back to `ProbingAfter` enqueues the Agent on the eval controller's `For(&Agent{})` watch, and that pass re-reads clause 5. Sends stay fail-closed throughout, because clause 5 is re-read before every claim. It is "Outside the exempt state"'s one-pass default, met on the way back in. No decision: closing the window would need the waiting pass to write, which A21.7 declines. Depends on A21.7.

Change: clause 5 is read last after every other send predicate too — the generation, a candidate pending, its availability, no I1 hold, a recorded card — and after the suite is read and digested; and a pass that clause 5 holds is not one that would start a run, so it fetches no card, and the computed value is the hold even where the card would refuse. Change: none proposed; the code meets the sentence. Read from the code: `gatewayAuthPolicies` never fails, so the error the pass returns for an unreadable Gateway or a failed Gateway-namespace list is built from `gatewayAuth`'s `unreadable` text. That text carries the API error's own text for a Gateway read that is Forbidden or of no recognised class, and for a failed list, and not for a Gateway the API server does not serve or that does not exist, which `gatewayAuthPolicies` words without it. It reaches the log and controller-runtime's backoff, never `blocked`, whose message names the error's class (A21.14).   - **Tag forms.** A22 uses A20's terms, tag forms, kinds of item and deciding, defined once in the first bullet of A20's entry, "Tag forms", with `A22` in place of `A20`. **It adds one form, removal, because it is the first amendment to remove a tag.** It also extends kind (iv)'s provenance: beyond A20's "measured by a test" and "read from the code", a fact may rest on a review's probe or a reviewer's mutation, and the item says which, by name (A22.10, A22.23, A22.30; A22.1, a kind (ii) item, cites a probe and a mutation the same way).     - at "A pinned rollback, and a removed gate": *(A20.36, interim until build step 8: read from the code and not measured, the pinned revision is the desired one, so the rollout switch records it as the candidate, and the eval controller, whose I1 predicate a set pin lifts, evaluates it like any candidate. A pinned rollback on a gated Agent is held until it earns a `Passed` verdict against the current suite, or reads `EvalPairAlreadyFailed` if that pair has failed. Before any verdict the gate branch answers the suite reasons, so a deleted or unreadable suite holds a pinned rollback until the suite is restored or `spec.gates` is removed.)*;
    - in Q5 (a): *(A20.36, interim until build step 8: no pin is refused or bypassed yet; a pinned rollback on a gated Agent is evaluated like a candidate)*;

    - at "EvalSuite write is unauthenticated invocation of the held candidate": *(A20.37, interim until build step 9: the EvalSuite CRD ships in the chart and Q6 (c)'s admission policy does not yet, so any principal who can write EvalSuites in a namespace holds this grant; the chart binds no role over EvalSuites to anyone but the operator, which holds `get`)*;
    - in the decision table's Q6 row: *(A20.37, interim until build step 9: not built; the CRD ships without it)*.

    - **A22.1** (ii). Approved: "a pin is refused when its digest is the current candidate's", read beside "The candidate is recorded in `status.candidateRevision` and `candidateRevisionDigest` while it is evaluated and while it is held." Change: the current candidate is the revision the current spec mints, its digest computed each pass with the content of its env sources, as the rollout step computes it, and not `status.candidateRevisionDigest`. A pin to it is refused whether or not a pass has recorded it. So a revision the spec is reverted to and pinned is refused in one write or in two; a pinned revision that the rollout records as the candidate while it comes up is not its own candidate, because the spec mints another; and one write that edits the spec and pins the candidate it leaves behind is admitted, since that revision is no longer the spec's (A22.8). Code: `pinIneligible` (`internal/controller/pinrule.go`), given `desiredDigest` by `Reconcile`; `specDigestOf` and `resolveEnvSourcesFrom`, which the Agent reconciler's `resolveEnvSources` now wraps, for the eval controller (A22.7). Test: `TestPinIneligibleFollowsQ5`, `TestAPinToTheRevisionTheSpecMintsIsRefusedInOneWriteOrTwo`, `TestAPinToACandidateTheSpecHasLeftIsAdmitted`, `TestAComingUpPinStaysAdmittedAcrossPassesThatDoNotJudgeIt`, `TestASourceContentEditLiftsACandidateRefusal`, and T28's "the current candidate". Review 3 of #119. **What taking it accepts**: two of A22.8's shapes, each measured by a test, serve the revision the gate was evaluating, unevaluated, while `spec.gates` stays declared: one write that edits the spec and pins the candidate it leaves behind is admitted (`TestAPinToACandidateTheSpecHasLeftIsAdmitted`), and a refused pin to the current candidate is admitted once an edit to a referenced ConfigMap or Secret mints another (`TestASourceContentEditLiftsACandidateRefusal`). Q5's decision chose (a) so as not to serve "the candidate that just failed, under a reason that calls it a rollback"; these two serve a candidate with no final verdict, under `PinnedRollback`. The spec reading was the coordinator's choice on review 2 of #119, as the first test's comment records. If declined: the rule reads `status.candidateRevisionDigest`. That is three changes, not one: `pinIneligible`'s caller; `pinnedRollbackCandidate`, which would otherwise hold the run of every candidate the Agent reconciler then refuses as the recorded candidate, since it holds whenever the pin names the recorded candidate and is not the spec's revision; and the refusal's message and `reasons.yaml`'s `PinnedRevisionNotEligible` entry, whose "does not stop the recorded candidate's run" would become false for those shapes. That reading refuses both shapes — shape 2 measured by review 3 of #119's mutation O1, "candidate clause ignores the spec check", which is that reading and which `TestAPinToACandidateTheSpecHasLeftIsAdmitted` killed; shape 3 read from the code — because the pin step reads status before the supersession and a refusal returns before it, so the recorded candidate stays the pinned one. Its costs: a pinned revision coming up is refused as its own candidate on the pass after the rollout records it, since a pin not yet serving is judged on every pass (A22.3), which O1 measured (`TestAComingUpPinStaysAdmittedAcrossPassesThatDoNotJudgeIt` killed it three times, and T28's served row); and whether a revert-and-pin is refused depends on whether a pass recorded the revert before the pin was read. Review 3 of #119 measured that second cost by a probe (PA) against the code then, which refused on the recorded candidate and the spec's revision together: one write that reverted the image to a retained revision and pinned it was admitted and served unevaluated, and the same edit in two writes was refused; the recorded-candidate reading alone gives the same answer for that shape, read from the code. Depends on A22.3. Recommended: take, because the alternative, whose timing dependence probe PA measured in its intersection form and which O1 measured refusing pins still coming up, makes Q5's own refusal of "a revision that is the current candidate again after serving earlier" depend on queue timing, and refuses every pinned rollback while it comes up; the two shapes it accepts each need an Agent editor's deliberate write, or a source edit, and A22.8 records them.
    - **A22.2** (ii). Approved: "a pin is refused when its digest ... appears in `status.eval.failed[]` paired with the **current** suite digest". Change: a pin that names the revision serving is never judged. The pin step calls `pinRule` only when the pinned digest is not `status.activeRevisionDigest`, and `pinIneligible` returns no refusal for it either. So a serving pinned revision whose pair fails the suite now in force serves on and is not refused. Q5's own "What (a) still refuses that served" is unchanged for a revision that is not serving. Why: a refusal returns before the rest of the pass, so refusing the revision that serves would roll nothing back and would stop the pass on every reconcile while it serves — route, `-auth` and drift convergence included — which was review 1 of #119's BLOCKER. Code: the pin step in `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/agent_controller.go`), `pinIneligible`'s first guard. Test: `TestAPinToTheServingRevisionIsNeverRefused`, `TestAnAdmittedPinIsNotRefusedWhileItServes` (the suite edited back to one it failed; the suite deleted, then restored). If declined: such a pin is refused, a change to the pin step, and the freeze review 1 found returns for as long as it serves; A22.17 asks the related question about every refusal. What a serving pinned revision reports is A22.6, and whether it should report more is A22.14. Recommended: take, because refusing a pin that already serves can change nothing but the freeze.
    - **A22.3** (i). Approved: "Any other pin promotes, not evaluated". Change: nothing about a pin is stored. The rule is derived on every pass from what the pass reads — the spec, status, the content of the spec's env sources, and the suite, read uncached (A22.4) — and reads no stored judgement and no condition. A pin admitted and not yet serving is judged again on each pass, so a suite edited to one it failed refuses it before it serves; once it serves, A22.2 applies. Code: `pinIneligible`, `pinRule`. Test: `TestAComingUpPinIsJudgedAgainstAChangedSuite`, `TestAComingUpPinStaysAdmittedAcrossPassesThatDoNotJudgeIt`, `TestAForgedGatesBypassedGrantsNothing`. Review 2 of #119, which found an admission record kept in a condition message droppable and forgeable. Recommended: take, because a stored judgement can be lost or forged between passes, and a derived one cannot.
    - **A22.4** (ii). Approved: "The refusal goes through the existing unresolvable-pin path, reason `PinnedRevisionNotEligible`: nothing is rolled back, and the current release is untouched." Change: the refusal departs from the existing path in two ways and adds one read. Under `gateEnforced` only, the pinned revision's retained material is verified first, then the rule is applied, both before the collision guard, the supersession and the rollout switch, so a pin whose material is gone reads `ReleasePinUnresolvable` and is never told to edit a suite; on an ungated Agent, or with the EvalSuite CRD absent, the pin is handled exactly as before Q5, its material verified after the supersession. The refusal sets `Degraded=True/PinnedRevisionNotEligible` and phase `Degraded`, leaves `Ready` as it was, and returns `RequeueAfter: CardDriftInterval`, where the unresolvable-pin path otherwise returns no requeue, because a suite edit can lift the refusal and nothing watches suites. And the refusal reads the suite uncached, as the gate branch does, on each pass that judges a pin (`pinRule`, through `r.reader()`). Code: `Reconcile`, `pinRule`, `reportUnresolvablePin` and `unresolvablePinError`'s `ineligible` field (`internal/controller/release.go`). Test: `TestAPinWhoseMaterialIsGoneIsRefusedForThatFirst`, `TestAnUngatedPinKeepsTheOrderItHadBeforeQ5`, and T28's failed-pair subtest, which asserts the requeue. First critique of A22, MINOR 8. If declined: the gated refusal returns no requeue and verifies material after the supersession, as the existing path does, a change to `Reconcile`; a refusal is then re-read only on an Agent event, so a suite edit that would lift it goes unseen, and a pin whose material is gone can be told to edit a suite. Recommended: take, because a refusal that names the wrong cause, or that is never re-read after the suite changes, is rule 8's shape.
    - **A22.5** (ii). Approved, in "Where each reason is set": "So it keeps only the reasons it can decide there: `GatesSkipped` with `NoGatesDeclared` or `EvalSuiteCRDAbsent`; `GatesPassed=False/GateDetectionUnwired`; and `GatesPassed=False/NoGatesDeclared` on an Agent whose gate was removed (A5)." Change: `GatesBypassed=True/PinnedRollback` is set in three places. `assessGates` sets its serving form, from status as the pass found it, so every exit of the pass keeps it. The pin step sets its pending form as soon as it admits a pin, so an exit after the pin step — the collision guard — keeps it. And the block after the rollout switch derives it again, either form, or unsets it. `GatesBypassed` is owned and not sticky (`ownedTypes`, `internal/controller/conditions.go`), is derived on each pass from what the pass reads — the spec, status, the spec's env sources' content and, for the pending form, the suite read uncached by `pinRule` — never from a stored judgement or condition, and is not asserted on a pass that does not enforce the gate. It is withdrawn on the first pass on which it no longer derives: the pin removed, or changed to a digest neither admitted nor serving without a `Passed` verdict; a `Passed` verdict recorded for the serving pinned revision; the pin refused; or a pending pin that stops resolving. The pending form is also withdrawn on an exit before the pin step — the run namespace unavailable, or a source of the current spec unresolved — and comes back on the next pass that reaches it. Code: `assessGates`, `Reconcile`, `servingPinBypass`, `pendingPinBypass`. Test: `TestAnAdmittedPinsBypassSurvivesAnEarlyExit`, `TestAPendingPinsBypassSurvivesAnExitAfterThePinStep`, `TestAPendingPinsBypassIsWithdrawnOnAnExitBeforeThePinStep`, `TestAForgedGatesBypassedOnAServingPinIsRecomputedAway`, `TestAServingPinsBypassIsDerivedAgainAfterTheCRDReturns`, `TestRePinningToTheServingRevisionWithdrawsTheBypass`, `TestAnAdmittedPinThatStopsResolvingWithdrawsItsBypass`. Review 4 of #119, MINOR 2; review 5's divergence 3. If declined: `GatesBypassed` is set inside the rollout step alone, a change to `assessGates` and the pin step; then any pass that writes status and returns early withdraws it, so a serving pinned revision's condition comes and goes with its early exits, which review 4 found for the pending form. Depends on A22.6. Recommended: take, because a condition that an unrelated early exit withdraws says nothing reliable.
    - **A22.6** (i). Approved: the table row "a pinned rollback that Q5 admits | unchanged; `GatesBypassed=True/PinnedRollback`", and "`GatesPassed` stays owned and sticky ... After a promotion it keeps describing the revision `status.eval` names". Change: `GatesBypassed` has two forms. The **pending** form, for a pin admitted on this pass and not yet serving, says the revision will be promoted once available without its eval gate, that the eval controller sends it no case, that it is judged again on each pass, and why it was admitted. The **serving** form, for a pin naming the revision serving while `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict for it, says it serves by pin, then what the record holds, chosen from a fixed set by `servingRecord` and by nothing else: no run; a run of another revision, which says whether this one "passed its gate is not recorded" when `failed[]` names the pinned digest, and "passed its gate or never ran is not recorded" only when it does not, since a `failed[]` pair records a run of it; a `Failed` verdict for it, naming the suite digest; a run of it with no verdict yet; or its pair with the verdict cleared (#111's clear); and, beside any of these, how many pairs `status.eval.failed[]` records for it and against which suite digests. It never says that the gate was bypassed or why the revision was not passed. On the one pass that admits a pin and promotes it, the serving form also carries the admission reason, "design 16 Q5 (a) admitted the pin: …", ahead of the record; every later pass, and `assessGates`, give one steady text. A serving pinned revision with a `Passed` verdict reads no `GatesBypassed`. While the serving form derives, the promotion branch does not assert `GatesPassed`, which keeps describing the revision `status.eval` names. Code: `servingPinBypass`, `servingRecord`, `pendingPinBypass`; the promotion branch's pinned case in `Reconcile`. Test: `TestServingRecordSaysOnlyWhatStatusEvalHolds` (exact text, every branch, the other-revision rows `another revision's pair, failed[] naming none of its own` and `… naming its own` included) and `TestServingPinBypassIsTheRecordAndNothingElse` (the whole message, steady state and admitting pass), `TestAServingPinThatPassedEarlierSaysOnlyWhatIsRecorded`, `TestAPinToTheServingRevisionIsNeverRefused`. Review 4 of #119, MAJOR 1, review 5, MAJOR A, and review 6's m1, which split the other-revision branch: serving messages were found false three times, the second saying "not recorded" while `status.eval` held the pinned revision's own `Failed` verdict, so the message is now the record and nothing else, and every branch is pinned by exact text. Depends on A22.2. Recommended: take, because what the serving form can say truly is what status records, and two looser wordings were each false in a reachable state.
    - **A22.7** (ii). Approved: "Both decide whether a case may be sent with the same predicates, implemented once, in `internal/controller/sendpredicates.go`, whose comment tables which controller evaluates which, and called by both:", followed by the list. Change: the list gains one predicate, that the candidate is not an admitted pinned rollback. The Agent reconciler evaluates it in the pin step, by promoting an admitted pin past the gate. The eval controller derives it itself (`pinnedRollbackCandidate`): the pin names `status.candidateRevisionDigest`, and that is not the revision the current spec mints (`specDigestOf`, over the shared `resolveEnvSourcesFrom`). It reads no condition, so a forged `GatesBypassed` pauses nothing, and a pin the Agent reconciler refuses as the current candidate leaves the recorded candidate's run going. When the spec's digest cannot be computed it holds, since the Agent reconciler judges no pin on such a pass: a source NotFound or Forbidden, or a digest error, holds the pass at `CardDriftInterval` (`waitHeld`); any other read error of a source is returned, and the pass retries under controller-runtime's backoff, sending nothing either way. It is read after the suite is read and digested, and before the threshold and clause 5. A pass it holds at `waitHeld` joins the passes "What wakes the eval controller while it waits" lists; the Agent reconciler's status write when it promotes the pin, or when the pin goes, brings the next pass. **Its cost**: on a pass whose pin names the recorded candidate, the eval controller reads every env source of the Agent's spec, ConfigMaps and Secrets, which the manager does not cache (`cmd/operator/main.go`), so each is an API-server `get`; "What clause 5 costs" does not bill them. Code: `pinnedRollbackCandidate`, `specDigestOf` (`internal/controller/sendpredicates.go`), `Reconcile` (`internal/controller/evalcontroller.go`). First critique of A22, MINOR 5, on the error branch. Test: `TestAPinnedRollbackIsSentNoCase`, `TestARefusedPinLeavesTheCandidatesRunGoing` (with a forged condition), `TestAPinNamingAnotherRevisionLeavesTheCandidatesRunGoing`, `TestTheEvalControllerHoldsAPinnedCandidateWhileTheSpecCannotBeDigested`; the interval and the reads are unpinned, owed on #115. Review 2 and review 4 of #119. If declined: the eval controller evaluates an admitted pinned rollback, which "promotes, not evaluated" forbids, and a `Failed` verdict would put a pair that was never meant to run into `failed[]` for good; the other way to learn admission, reading `GatesBypassed`, is the forgeable reading review 2 removed. Depends on A22.1 and A22.3. Recommended: take, because Q5 (a) says an admitted pin is not evaluated, and this is the only reading of it that a status write cannot forge.
    - **A22.8** (iv, read from the code; two shapes measured). Approved: "What (a) still admits that never served: a retained candidate that failed only an older suite, or whose failure was evicted from the 10-entry record." Change: none proposed; the list is incomplete. Q5 (a) as built also admits, unevaluated, three shapes that never served (tagged "two measured by a test, one read from the code"): a superseded candidate that was never evaluated, pinned in a write after the one that superseded it, since it is neither the spec's revision nor in `failed[]`; the same in one write that edits the spec and pins the candidate it leaves (`TestAPinToACandidateTheSpecHasLeftIsAdmitted`); and a refused pin to the current candidate after an edit to a referenced ConfigMap or Secret mints a new one with no write to the Agent, which makes the pinned revision a superseded candidate (`TestASourceContentEditLiftsACandidateRefusal`). The other way round, a pinned revision admitted and coming up is refused if the spec is then edited to mint that same revision, because it is judged again on each pass (A22.1, A22.3); read from the code. The first shape follows from Q5 (a)'s text whatever reading is taken, and is a fact. The second and third follow from A22.1 and fall with its decline; A22.1 weighs them. Depends on A22.1.
    - **A22.9** (iv, measured by a test). Approved: "`GatesPassed` stays owned and sticky ... After a promotion it keeps describing the revision `status.eval` names, until the next candidate resets it." Change: none proposed; recorded because a reader meets it after every pinned promotion. After a pinned rollback is promoted, `GatesPassed` keeps describing the candidate `status.eval` names — T28 measures `GatesPassed` unchanged after B is promoted by pin, and `TestAServingPinThatPassedEarlierSaysOnlyWhatIsRecorded` measures it reading C's `EvalFailed` beside a serving pin — and `GatesBypassed`'s serving form is what describes B (A22.6). A pin to a serving revision that passed its gate before a later run replaced the record reads the serving form's "another revision" branch, and the promotion branch no longer asserts `GatesPassed=True/EvalPassed` for it (`TestAServingPinThatPassedEarlierSaysOnlyWhatIsRecorded`; review 4 of #119's PR1, the shape of A22.16). No decision.
    - **A22.10** (iv; one shape measured by a probe, one read from the code). Approved: "nothing is rolled back, and the current release is untouched", and, in "The two controllers", "The readings differ only in that state, which the Agent reconciler never judges." Change: none proposed. **While a refusal stands, the recorded candidate can be run to a verdict.** One write that reverts the spec to a retained revision B and pins it, while a later candidate C is recorded, refuses B as the current candidate; the refusal returns before the supersession and stamps `observedGeneration`, so C stays recorded and every send predicate holds for it. Review 5 of #119 measured it by a probe (Q2): C was sent its cases and recorded `Passed`. Nothing promotes while the refusal stands, and clearing the pin supersedes C. The release is untouched; `status.eval` is not. The probe is not a test. `TestARefusedPinLeavesTheCandidatesRunGoing` pins a neighbouring shape, cases sent to a recorded candidate that is the spec's revision while a pin to it is refused; the revert-and-pin shape, whose recorded candidate is not the spec's revision, and its verdict are unpinned, owed on #115 (item 13), which asks whether the eval controller's predicate should read the refusal; A22.33 puts that question. **And the I1 readings now differ in a second state.** While a refusal stands the Agent reconciler returns before the I1 hold, and the eval controller's I1 predicate reads the pin as set from `targetRevisionDigest` alone and turns the hold off, as A20.26 records for a set pin that does not resolve; read from the code, and noted in `reasons.yaml`'s `PinnedRevisionNotEligible` entry. Unpinned, owed on #115. No decision here; A22.17 asks whether a refusal should freeze the pass at all, and A22.33 whether the eval controller should hold beside it. Tagged at "The readings differ only in that state".
    - **A22.11** (iv, read from the code). Approved: "a pin is refused when its digest ... appears in `status.eval.failed[]`". Change: none proposed. An Agent CRD too old to keep `status.eval` (`EvalRecordNotKept`) prunes `failed[]`, so on such an install a pin is never refused on it; the current-candidate refusal still applies. Unpinned. No decision. Tagged at Q5's "When there is no current suite digest", whose admission of a `failed[]` digest it extends.
    - **A22.12** (iv, read from the code). Approved: "Any other pin promotes". Change: none proposed. A pending pinned rollback is not promoted while a source of the current spec is unresolved, because the Agent reconciler returns at `EnvSourceUnresolved` before the pin step; the pending form of `GatesBypassed` is withdrawn meanwhile (`TestAPendingPinsBypassIsWithdrawnOnAnExitBeforeThePinStep`). It predates build step 8 and belongs to design 02's rollout step; the promotion side is unpinned, owed on #115. No decision. Tagged at Q5 (a)'s "Any other pin promotes".
    - **A22.13** (iv, read from the code, with one measurement cited; written into §1.1 at one place and tagged at the other). Approved: "As this design reads today's code, a pinned rollback holds with no exit whenever a gate is declared and the EvalSuite CRD is installed", and, in Q5's decision, "By this draft's reading, today's code holds a pinned rollback under a declared gate with no exit." Change: both sentences described the code when the slice was approved; they stopped being true at build step 6, and A20.36's tags said so. With the tags removed, A22 writes ahead of the first what build step 8 built and what the code did between build steps 6 and 8, and marks the two approved sentences there as describing the code when the slice was approved; at the second, question text that A20 does not rewrite, a fact tag says the same. It deletes neither. The written sentence's measurement is #82's step-8 comment: `TestAPinnedRollbackIsSentNoCase`, run against the code before build step 8, found the eval controller running and passing a pinned rollback. First critique of A22, MAJOR and MINOR 6. No decision.
    - **A22.14** (question), put to the human and not proposed: **what should a serving pinned revision report once it fails the suite in force?** As built: it is never refused (A22.2) and serves — fail-open, by design — and `GatesBypassed`'s serving form says what the record holds, its own `Failed` verdict and suite digest and its `failed[]` pairs included (A22.6); since review 5 of #119 that is true. It does not say whether the recorded suite digest is the suite in force, because the serving form reads status alone and no suite. **(a)** Leave it as built. **(b)** A follow-up that also names whether the suite in force fails it. Read from the code and not measured, it would add an uncached suite read to every pass of a serving pinned Agent, in `assessGates` and after the switch, which today read no suite for the serving form, and a second message form. The remedy either way is the owner's: clear the pin, or let the next candidate pass. **Recommended: (a)**, because the record is stated truly and names the suite digest, which its reader can compare with the suite.
    - **A22.15** (question): **should a transient suite-read error admit a pin that `failed[]` records?** As built: any failure to learn the suite digest — NotFound, any other read error, or a suite that cannot be digested — leaves no current suite digest, so only the current-candidate refusal applies and a `failed[]` pin is admitted, with a message saying the suite "could not be read" and `failed[]` "was not consulted". That is what Q5 (a)'s approved text commands for `EvalSuiteUnreadable`, "an API error". If the error spans the pass that promotes the pin, the admission is permanent, because a serving pin is never judged again (A22.2). **(a)** Leave it as built. **(b)** A follow-up that admits on NotFound only and holds a pin on any other read error, retrying under controller-runtime's backoff; a change to Q5 (a)'s approved text. Its cost: a suite that stays unreadable — Forbidden, say — then blocks every rollback, admitted or not, for as long as it stays unreadable, the incident A6 recorded the rule to avoid; and nothing at the moment of the read tells a transient error from a lasting one. **Recommended: (a)**, on A6's reasoning; the residue is that a rollback admitted during a read error serves.
    - **A22.16** (question): **what should a revision that bypassed its gate read once it is unpinned?** As built: `GatesBypassed` is withdrawn on the first pass without the pin. If the spec mints the serving revision, the promotion branch sets `GatesPassed=False/ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated`, which is true (read from the code, `assessPromotedGate`, and review 5 of #119's reading 3). Otherwise the spec's revision is the candidate, `GatesPassed` describes that candidate, and nothing names the serving revision as served without a `Passed` verdict — fail-open while it serves, and silent by omission (review 5 of #119, the human's reading 3). **(a)** Leave it as built. **(b)** A follow-up that keeps a serving form while the gate is enforced and `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict for the serving revision, pinned or not, derived from status alone as A22.6's is. Its cost: the condition then also fires for a revision that served before the gate was added, beside `ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated`, and `GatesBypassed` comes to mean "the serving revision has no `Passed` record" rather than "a pin bypassed the gate"; a new condition type would avoid that and add vocabulary. **Recommended: (b)**, because a gated Agent serving a revision its gate never passed is the state the gate exists to prevent, and leaving it unnamed is NFR-8's silent path; the shape, a widened `GatesBypassed` or a new type, is the follow-up's to propose.
    - **A22.17** (question): **should a refused pin keep reconciling the serving revision?** As built: a refusal, like every refusal on the unresolvable-pin path, returns early, so while it stands the serving revision's route, `-auth` and drift convergence stop (`reasons.yaml`'s `PinnedRevisionNotEligible` entry says so), and A22.10's stale run can go on. A pending pin can be refused by a suite edit, so a suite writer can start the freeze; since build step 9 every suite writer is an Agent editor, who could already freeze the pass with an unresolvable pin. **(a)** Leave it as built. **(b)** A follow-up in which a refusal reports `Degraded` and then completes the pass for the serving revision alone, skipping the supersession and the switch; a change to design 02's unresolvable-pin path too, if it is to cover `ReleasePinUnresolvable`. **Recommended: (a)** in this slice, because the path is design 02's, Q5 (a) chose it, and since Q6 (c) the freeze adds nothing an Agent editor lacks; (b) belongs with design 02's text (#99).
    - **A22.18** (i). Approved: Q6 (c), "An admission policy admits an EvalSuite create or update only when the requester may also `update` `agents` in that namespace. It is written as a ValidatingAdmissionPolicy using Kubernetes' CEL `authorizer` library." Change: the policy applies in every namespace, with no `namespaceSelector`, because a suite lives in its Agent's namespace and those carry no label of assayd's to select on, unlike the run namespaces that the run-namespace reservations select. Its one validation is exactly `authorizer.group('assayd.dev').resource('agents').namespace(request.namespace).check('update').allowed()`, with `failurePolicy: Fail` and a `Deny` binding, and its refusal names the namespace and the fix: grant `update` on `agents.assayd.dev` there, namespace-wide, or ask an Agent editor there to apply the suite. Code: `charts/assayd/templates/admission.yaml`. Test: `TestTheChartReservesEvalSuiteWritesToAgentEditors`, `TestTheChartRendersOneEvalSuiteWritersPolicyAndBinding`, `TestOnlyAnAgentEditorWritesAnEvalSuite` (T61), whose rows include an Agent editor in another namespace, denied. PR #125, choice 1. Recommended: take, because a selector would need a label nothing writes on Agent namespaces, and a refusal that names the fix is write-spec's rule.
    - **A22.19** (i). Approved: the same Q6 (c) text. Change: the policy exempts no one. Every principal that may `update` Agents namespace-wide passes it, the operator included, whose ClusterRole updates every Agent; RBAC alone, `get` and nothing else on `evalsuites`, keeps the operator from writing a suite. Code: `admission.yaml`, the operator's ClusterRole. Test: T61's operator row, admitted by the policy; `TestTheOperatorOnlyReadsEvalSuites`, which renders five value sets and pins the operator's RBAC at `get` on `evalsuites`; `TestTheEvalSuiteWritersTemplateIsNotConditional`. PR #125, choice 2. Whether to add an explicit clause refusing the operator is A22.26. Recommended: take, because Q6 (c)'s rule is about who may edit Agents, and an exemption would be a second rule to keep.
    - **A22.20** (i). Approved: "Deletes stay RBAC's, as they are for the policies design 07 already reserves." Change: every create and update is judged, whatever it changes — metadata-only edits by users and GitOps tools included — where the other four reservations admit an update that leaves what they reserve alone. The garbage collector, which removes a deletion finalizer by update, holds `update` on every resource and so passes the check where kube-controller-manager runs with `--use-service-account-credentials`; T61 measures that on envtest with bootstrap RBAC alone, and it is unverified on k3s and on managed control planes, where a failure stalls a foreground delete, failing closed. A principal granted `patch` on Agents and not `update` is refused (A22.25). Code: `admission.yaml`. Test: T61's garbage-collector and `patch`-only rows. PR #125, choice 3. Recommended: take, because the grant Q6 guards is the suite's content, and judging every update keeps the rule one expression.
    - **A22.21** (iv, read from the tests). Change: none proposed. The golden fixtures (`test/chart/testdata/evalsuite-writers-*.yaml`, four value profiles) were generated from the chart's render and then checked field by field against Q6 (c), so they are a change detector, not an independent specification. The specification is the chart's semantic assertions, the template pin and T61's enumerated rows. PR #125, choice 4. No decision.
    - **A22.22** (iv, read from the code). Change: none proposed. The operator does not fail closed when `assayd-evalsuite-writers` is absent: it neither detects nor reports the missing policy, where design 07 A5.9's reservations make it refuse with `LabelAuthorityAbsent`. Q6 (c) does not require it. With the policy gone, any principal RBAC lets write EvalSuites holds the grant "EvalSuite write is unauthenticated invocation of the held candidate" describes, and nothing reports it. It belongs on design 07's list, #100. PR #125, choice 5. No decision.
    - **A22.23** (iv, measured by a review). Change: none proposed. The subjects of the operator's ClusterRoleBinding are pinned by no test: a binding of the operator to a built-in role such as `cluster-admin`, templated under any value, passes every chart test, because `TestTheOperatorOnlyReadsEvalSuites` reads the chart's own role rules and not what its bindings bind. Review 5 of #125 measured that shape. A22.19's boundary, RBAC keeping the operator at `get`, rests on it. #127. No decision.
    - **A22.24** (iv, measured). Approved: in Q6's decision, "(c)'s minimum Kubernetes version is the chart's floor, 1.30, where ValidatingAdmissionPolicy is GA and carries the `authorizer` variable. It is unmeasured on 1.30 and on the e2e's k3s v1.33.6, and a test on the e2e's version is owed (below)", and T62's row, "Q6 (c) on the e2e's k3s v1.33.6". Change: none proposed. T62 was run twice on k3s v1.33.6, on a local k3d cluster, unmutated and with the binding dropped. It runs unskipped in both CI lanes (`gh pr checks 125`: e2e (k3d) and e2e (kind) passed). CI's e2e jobs on PR #125 ran on k3s v1.35.5-k3s1, under k3d, and on kind v1.35.0, so "the e2e's k3s v1.33.6" is not the version CI measures. T61 runs on envtest's API server. The 1.30 floor is measured nowhere. PR #125's gates. A22.27 asks what §1.1 should cite. No decision. Tagged at Q6's decision and at T62's row.
    - **A22.25** (question): **should an Agent editor who holds `patch` but not `update` stay refused?** As built: refused, because the check asks `update`, as Q6 (c)'s text says. Such a principal can already change the candidate's image through `patch`, so on Q6's own argument the grant adds nothing they lack. **(a)** Keep it refused. **(b)** Admit `update` or `patch`, a second `check` in the expression and a widening of Q6 (c)'s approved text. **Recommended: (a)**: it errs toward refusal, as the `resourceNames` case already does; the fix, granting `update`, is named in the refusal and in `docs/install.md`; and a widening of an approved rule should come from a user who meets it.
    - **A22.26** (question): **should the policy refuse the operator explicitly?** As built: no clause names the operator; RBAC alone keeps it at `get` (A22.19), pinned by `TestTheOperatorOnlyReadsEvalSuites` and exposed by #127's unpinned binding subjects (A22.23). **(a)** Leave it. **(b)** Add a CEL clause refusing the operator's ServiceAccount and `admission.extraOperators`, which puts the operator's identity in the expression under every value profile, with a mutation surface of its own. **Recommended: (a)**: an operator whose RBAC has been widened to write suites has been widened to more than suites, the boundary is pinned where it is drawn, and #127 is the gap to close.
    - **A22.27** (question): **which Kubernetes versions should §1.1 cite for Q6 (c)?** The facts are A22.24's. **(a)** Cite what CI measures, k3s v1.35.5-k3s1 and kind v1.35.0, say that v1.33.6 was measured locally, and keep "unmeasured on 1.30". **(b)** Pin the e2e harness's k3s image to v1.33.6, so the cited version is the one CI runs. **(c)** Add a CI leg on 1.30, so the floor is measured. **Recommended: (a)** in the fold that follows this decision, and (c) as a follow-up issue, because the floor is the claim that matters and (b) only moves the version cited.
    - **A22.28** (v, process). A20's text, in "Tags that outlive the fold": each tag where the code departs is "removed by the change that ends the departure"; and `AGENTS.md`: "Code that diverges from an approved design is drift, whatever it does. Amend the design first, as a numbered amendment, in the same change." Change: A22 removes A20.36's three tags and A20.37's two, quoted in full in "What A22 ends", in the removal form "Tag forms" defines, and so supersedes A20's sentence for these five. **What is put to the human**: removing the five tags now, in A22, and so superseding A20's sentence for them. **What is not**: that build steps 8 and 9, PRs #119 and #125, left §1.1's text, these tags included, to this separate amendment rather than amending in the same change. That departure from `AGENTS.md` is a fact of the record, which no answer here reverses, and A20 and A21 are its precedent: A20 carried build steps 1 to 6 the same way (its entry says each change was "recorded on an issue or a PR thread rather than edited in"), and A21 build step 7, both decided by the human on 2026-10-21. Each removed tag was false of the code once its step merged. Where a removed tag qualified a sentence describing the code, A22.13 marks that sentence. A22 states no rule for later interim tags. If declined: the fold restores the five tags as quoted, at the places `dd8e34c`'s text fixes, and A20's sentence stands; the decline restores only the tags. The tags then say the code departs where it no longer does, until a change the human accepts removes them; and at "A pinned rollback, and a removed gate" and in Q5's decision, the restored A20.36 tags would stand beside A22.13's marking, which a decline of A22.28 does not touch, because A22.13 is a fact and carries no decision, so §1.1 would state there both that a pinned rollback is evaluated like a candidate and that build step 8 ended that. First critique of A22, MINOR 7; second critique, MINOR 4. Recommended: take, because each tag is false of the code, the removal changes no rule and can be undone from the quotes, and leaving them would keep §1.1 asserting two ended departures until the fold.
    - **A22.29** (withdrawn). It recorded the supersession of A20's "removed by the change that ends the departure" as a fact, on a premise stated nowhere in the repository, that a code PR may not edit approved text. The second critique found that, and A22.28 now carries the supersession as a decidable item; the number is kept so that the first critique's record still resolves.
    - **A22.30** (iv, measured by a reviewer's probe; unpinned). Approved: "After a promotion it keeps describing the revision `status.eval` names, until the next candidate resets it." Change: none proposed. A pin that promotes a revision whose run was in flight leaves its `Running` pair in `status.eval` indefinitely, because the clear of a superseded record (step 7, `clearSuperseded`) clears only a `Failed` one; the serving form then says "a run of it … with no verdict yet" of a run nothing will finish. A reviewer's probe in reviews 6 and 7 of #119 measured it; no test pins it, owed on #115. No decision.
    - **A22.31** (iv, read from the code; not measured). Change: none proposed. On an Agent CRD that predates `verdict` and `suiteDigest` on `status.eval`'s pair, both are pruned, so the serving form's cleared-verdict sentence, "status.eval names it against suite digest … with its verdict cleared", renders with an empty digest. Such an install already reads `EvalRecordNotKept` on a gated candidate (step 11). Unpinned. No decision.
    - **A22.32** (iv, read from the code). Approved: "The refusal goes through the existing unresolvable-pin path". Change: none proposed. A pin refused on `failed[]` after the rollout recorded it as the candidate, while it came up, is still the recorded candidate, and the eval controller holds it (`pinnedRollbackCandidate`: the pin names the recorded candidate and is not the spec's revision), so nothing is evaluated, and that pair has failed already. `reasons.yaml`'s `PinnedRevisionNotEligible` entry states it. Unpinned. First critique of A22, NOTE 20. No decision.
    - **A22.33** (question): **should the eval controller hold the recorded candidate while a pin is refused as the current candidate?** #115 item 13 asks it for this amendment. As built (A22.10): one write that reverts the spec to a retained revision and pins it refuses the pin, the earlier candidate stays recorded, and the eval controller runs it to a verdict, with real requests, tool calls and spend; nothing promotes, and clearing the pin supersedes it. **(a)** Leave it as built. **(b)** A follow-up in which `pinnedRollbackCandidate` also holds when the pin equals the revision the current spec mints and does not name the recorded candidate, which the eval controller can derive with the reads it already makes for A22.7. Read from the code and not measured: (b) moves `specDigestOf` from passes whose pin names the recorded candidate to every pass with a pin set, so the eval controller reads the spec's env sources, uncached, on those passes too; and it adds no condition read, so it keeps A22.7's property that a status write cannot forge it. **Recommended: (b)**, because every case sent in that state goes to a revision the current spec does not mint, and the requests are real; a `Passed` verdict earned there is not cleared, so it could still promote that revision if the spec returned to it before another run started (read from the code: `clearSuperseded` clears only a `Failed` record).

- **A23 (2026-10-22, the drafter's; decided by the human on 2026-10-22, as recommended; accompanies build step 10, PR #130 → `29e508a`; stacked on A22) — §1.1 brought level with build step 10, `test/responder`'s two knobs and the e2e rows T68 and T69, and one question put to the human.** Build step 10 built the two knobs (`test/responder/main.go`) and T68 and T69 (`test/e2e/gate_test.go`), with the Gateway-level policy's setup moved into a helper shared with the older hold test (`test/e2e/gatewayhold_test.go`). It is cited here at `2597bdf`, the head of `d16-step10-e2e`, after one independent review on PR #130 that passed it with 1 MINOR and 8 NOTEs. Building it made choices §1.1 does not state and measured facts that make two of its rows incomplete. PR #130's sections "For the accompanying amendment" and "Review record", and the review posted verbatim on it, recorded them, and A23 carries them in. **It was proposed on 2026-10-22 and decided by the human on 2026-10-22, with A22** ("Decided", below).
