The kind (i) and kind (ii) items A23.1, A23.3 to A23.6, A23.8 and A23.9 are taken as the decision table at the end of this entry recommends. A23.17 is answered (a): no new e2e row; clause 5 stays pinned by envtest, and §1.1's T69 row says so. The kind (iv) facts are written into §1.1 as facts where §1.1 has a sentence they concern: A23.7, A23.10, A23.11 and A23.18 at the T68 and T69 rows, A23.2 at the knobs' line, and A23.13 at the T68 and T69 rows; A23.12 confirms a sentence §1.1 already carries, and A23.14 to A23.16, a cleanup on a timed-out run, the inventory's wording and the rows' timings, have no sentence in §1.1 to land on. A22's entry lists the tags that outlive the fold; A23 leaves none.
  - *The bullets below describe the text as proposed, before the fold.*
  - **Tag forms.** A23 uses A20's terms, tag forms, kinds of item and deciding, with A22's additions — the removal form, kind (v), and kind (iv) facts resting on a review's probe or a reviewer's mutation, named as such — all as A22's "Tag forms" states them, with `A23` in place of `A22`. It removes no tag and adds no kind. Every kind (i) and kind (ii) item has a tag at the text it concerns, and a kind (iv) fact is tagged where it makes an approved sentence false or incomplete at its place: A23.1 at the knobs' line of "What the slice depends on"; A23.3 to A23.7 in T68's row of "Tests the slice owes"; A23.18 in T68's mutation cell; and A23.8 to A23.11 in T69's row. That is eleven tags: seven proposed and four facts. A23.2 and A23.12 to A23.16 are not tagged, because no approved sentence is false or incomplete at a place they concern: §1.1 claims no gate for the responder's unit tests, says itself that eval requests do not cross the gateway, and says nothing of who writes a row's suite, of cleanups, of the inventory's wording or of timings.
  - **What A23 ends, and what it supersedes.** A23 edits no text A20, A21 or A22 inserted. Three sentences that A22 wrote into the Status line and §1.1's opening as facts, that build step 10 is not built, are no longer true, and A23 rewrites them directly, as A22 rewrote A21's: the Status line's "build step 10, `test/responder`'s two knobs and the e2e rows T68 and T69, is not"; the opening's "build step 10 is not"; and its "Not built: `test/responder`'s two knobs and the e2e rows T68 and T69 (build step 10)". The opening's sentence on tags, which A22 wrote naming A22 alone, now names A23 beside it. A22's entry, which records those sentences as A22 wrote them, stays as it is. A22's items and tags are untouched. A22's "What A22 ends" says that §1.1 carries twenty-seven tags; with A23's eleven it carries thirty-eight, and that count is superseded here, not edited there.

    **Facts A23 writes into §1.1 directly**, because they record what is built and bind nothing: the Status line's and the opening's build counts, with PR #130 at `29e508a`; the opening's sentence on what build step 10 built; its sentence on tags; and "What the slice depends on"'s "Not built: build step 10", for the knobs, now "Built in build step 10, PR #130".
  - **How A23 is built, and why.** As A20, A21 and A22 are, for their reasons. Every normative change is a numbered item; each gives the approved text it concerns, quoted, the change, the code and test it rests on, or that it is unpinned, for a kind (ii) item what declining it means, and, where there is a decision, a recommendation. "The review" is the one independent review on PR #130, posted verbatim there; "m1" to "m9" are the author's mutations, "mA" and "mB" the review's, and "c5" both theirs, as its mutation table names them. Line numbers cite `test/e2e/gate_test.go` at `2597bdf`. **When A23 merges**: after PR #130, which merged as `29e508a` on 2026-10-22; A23 was drafted and critiqued against the branch with a placeholder where this entry now reads `29e508a`, five times, each filled from the squash commit before A23's PR was opened; and with A22 or after it. Nothing enforces this structure (rule 7); #115.
  - **Every change, numbered.** Counts: kind (i), 6 (A23.1, A23.3, A23.4, A23.6, A23.8, A23.9); kind (ii), 1 (A23.5); kind (iii), (v), none; kind (iv), 10 (A23.2, A23.7, A23.10 to A23.16, A23.18); and one question, A23.17.
    - **A23.1** (i). Approved: "two env knobs on `test/responder`: one that changes its answer's prefix, and one echoed into the task's metadata, so the e2e can tell a failing revision and two passing ones apart." Change: the knobs are `ANSWER_PREFIX` and `ANSWER_MARKER`. `ANSWER_PREFIX` replaces the answer's `echo: ` prefix and is read verbatim with `os.LookupEnv`; only an unset variable keeps the default, so an empty value is an empty prefix. The responder's `env()` helper (`main.go:509`–`:514` at `2597bdf`) trims a set value and returns the default for an unset or blank one: read through it, the default would be unchanged, but T68's `held: ` would become `held:` and an empty prefix would fall back to `echo: `. `ANSWER_MARKER` is reported in the task's metadata as `marker` only when non-empty (`marker != ""`), and never changes the answer text. With neither set the responder answers exactly as before. Code: `test/responder/main.go`. Test: `TestTheAnswerPrefixAndTheMarkerAreKnobs`, and `TestItCompletesASendMessageTask`, which now also asserts no marker by default; the review killed six unit mutations of the knobs (ignoring either knob, reading the prefix through `env()`, an empty prefix as the default, a marker always set, a marker in the answer). The review measured the trimming read as a mutation: "read the prefix through the trimming `env()`: fails at `:205` (`held:hello`) and `:211`". T68 passes under either reading: `held:hello` fails the suite's `contains: echo: hello` as `held: hello` does, and the hold window checks revision 1's answers, not revision 2's. What the verbatim read protects is the fixture's documented contract — a knob passes its value through unchanged, and an empty prefix is distinct from an unset one — which `TestTheAnswerPrefixAndTheMarkerAreKnobs` pins. PR #130's inventory row and the comment in `main.go` (`:119`–`:121`) still give the earlier reason, that `env()` would trim the default's trailing space, which is false of `env()`, since it returns the default untouched; they are PR #130's text and are recorded here, not edited. Recommended: take, because a test knob that alters what it is given makes every row that sets it harder to read.
    - **A23.2** (iv, read from the build). Change: none proposed. `test/responder`'s unit tests, `TestTheAnswerPrefixAndTheMarkerAreKnobs` among them, run in neither `make test` nor CI; that was already true of the whole package, and of `test/mcpserver`, before build step 10. The review and the author ran it by hand. #129. No decision.
    - **A23.3** (i). Approved, T68's row: "An edit to the responder's answer prefix mints a revision that fails and holds … An edit that restores the prefix and changes the metadata marker passes". Change: the row uses revision 1 with marker `first`; revision 2 with `ANSWER_PREFIX=held: `; and revision 3 with the prefix restored and marker `third`. Its suite has one case, `hello`, expecting `echo: hello`, and the requests through the gateway send `probe`, answered `echo: probe`. Code and test: `TestARevisionIsEvaluatedBeforeItTakesTraffic`. Recommended: take.
    - **A23.4** (i). Approved: "for 60 s every answer through the gateway is the first revision's". Change: the 60 s window is 31 requests, each followed by a 2 s sleep, from one probe Pod, each with `--max-time 10`, inside the probe's 3-minute ceiling; every one must be HTTP 200, a completed task, `echo: probe` and marker `first`. Because the requests take time on top of the sleeps, the window is at least 60 s. At a sustained latency above about 3.8 s a request the probe misses its ceiling and the row fails, closed: it can flake, not pass falsely (the review's NOTE 6, read from the code). Test: `gate_test.go:100`. The review's mB, the route following the candidate before its verdict, was killed inside the window (`:102`), so the window is load-bearing. Recommended: take.
    - **A23.5** (ii). Approved: "once the route names it, 20 consecutive answers through the gateway carry the new marker". Change: the row first waits for the route's single `backendRef` to name revision 3 (`waitForRouteNames`), then for the first answer that carries `third`, within 3 minutes, one request per probe Pod, a new Pod on each attempt about 5 s apart (`waitForMarker`, `:119`), and only then sends the 20 consecutive answers, back to back, from another (`:120`), so the first `third` answer is not one of the 20; each must be HTTP 200, a completed task, `echo: probe` and marker `third` (`:118`–`:125`). So answers between the route naming revision 3 and the first that carries `third` are not counted, which allows for the proxy's lag behind the route object. Test: `TestARevisionIsEvaluatedBeforeItTakesTraffic`. The review's NOTE 2. If declined: the 20 are counted from the moment the route names revision 3, a change to the row, which then also fails whenever the proxy lags the route object, not only when traffic splits; neither has been measured. Recommended: take, because the route object is checked first and strictly, and what the 20 answers measure is that the data path follows it.
    - **A23.6** (i). Approved: T68's row, which names its outcomes and no other assertion. Change: the row also asserts that `status.eval` records `Passed` for revisions 1 and 3, and `Failed` for revision 2 with its case `hello` recorded `Failed`; `GatesPassed=True/EvalPassed` on revisions 1 and 3 once active, and `False/EvalFailed` on revision 2; phase `Held`, `status.activeRevision` still revision 1 and the candidate revision 2 while it holds; the route served under `apikey` and naming revision 1 once published (`:71`, `:72`), and naming revision 1 again after the hold window (`:106`); and an answer through the gateway carrying `first` after revision 1 is published. Test: `gate_test.go:66`–`:125`. The author's m1, the gate promoting with no verdict, was killed at `:66`; m4 and the review's mA, the matcher always passing and the eval's cases sent to the active revision's Service, at `:85`; m9, the responder ignoring `ANSWER_MARKER`, at `:77`. Recommended: take.
    - **A23.7** (iv, read from the code; the review's MINOR). Approved: T68's row, "passes and is published". Change: none proposed. The row waits for `Passed`, then the promotion, then a published route, in turn; it never samples the route while revision 1's eval runs. That the route stayed unpublished until the promotion is structural: every `ensureServingRoute` call takes `status.activeRevision` (`internal/controller/authtxn.go`, in `runCreate` and the passes after it), and a new Agent enters no `Create` until it has an active revision. So no mutation can separate "published before `Passed`" from m1, and it is not measured. For later revisions, mB measures the related property. Tagged at T68's row. No decision.
    - **A23.8** (i). Approved: T69's row, "a Gateway-level auth policy standing while a gated Agent's candidate passes: the candidate is promoted, and `Create` holds, unpublished, reason `GatewayAuthPolicy`". Change: the row is `TestAGatewayLevelPolicyHoldsTheCreateAfterTheGateHasPassed`. Its Gateway-level policy is hand-authored from `compiler.AuthPolicy`'s output — API-key authentication and a CEL authorization rule, the rule being what makes W1 count it (A23.10), since W1 does not count a Gateway-level policy that sets authentication alone — as an `AgentgatewayPolicy` targeting the assayd Gateway's serving listener, applied by `applyGatewayLevelAuthPolicy` (`test/e2e/gatewayhold_test.go`), which is shared with `TestAGatewayLevelAuthPolicyHoldsANewAgent` and deletes the policy on cleanup; the review found the moved setup identical line for line (NOTE 5). Recommended: take.
    - **A23.9** (i). Approved: the same row. Change: the row also asserts that the `Create` is held at `ProbingAfter` on a `401`, with `PolicyApplyIncomplete=GatewayAuthPolicy` naming the policy; that the promotion stands and the route has no `backendRefs`; and, once the policy is removed, that the route is published on the promoted revision, an anonymous request gets `401` (`:189`) and a keyed one `200`, a completed task carrying the agent's marker `gwgated` (`:191`) (`gate_test.go:161`–`:191`). Test: `TestAGatewayLevelPolicyHoldsTheCreateAfterTheGateHasPassed`. The author's mutation "promote only once the route is served" was killed, by PR #130's account alone, which names no failing line; the review did not re-run it. Recommended: take, because the publication after the policy goes is what shows the hold was the policy's and not the gate's.
    - **A23.10** (iv, measured by mutation, the author's m7 re-run by the review). Approved: T69's mutation "credit `Create`'s `401` under the Gateway-level policy", and its assertion "reason `GatewayAuthPolicy`". Change: none proposed. The mutation is `got401 && !held` → `got401` in `internal/controller/authtxn.go`. Under it the `Create` credits its `401` and reaches `Served`, and W1 (design 03 A75) then raises the same `PolicyApplyIncomplete=GatewayAuthPolicy` on the served Agent, so the row's reason wait (`:161`) still passes. The row is killed by the transaction check (`:171`, not held at `ProbingAfter`) and the route check (`:177`, published). Those two assertions carry the row, and the reason alone would not (the review's NOTE 3). Tagged at T69's row. No decision.
    - **A23.11** (iv, measured by mutation, by the author and the review). Change: none proposed. T69 does not exercise clause 5 of the exemption: a `Create` is never exempt, and a new gated Agent records no transaction while its first revision's eval runs, since a `Create` is entered only once a revision serves. The mutation c5, clause 5's hold removed (`if exempt {` → `if false && exempt {`, `internal/controller/evalcontroller.go`), survives both T68 and T69. No §1.1 row reaches clause 5 on a real gateway, and no e2e row pins it; envtest does. The second critique of A23 ran c5 against the exemption's envtest rows, and eight fail: T47's two (`TestAGatewayLevelPolicyHoldsTheExemptLockAcrossItsDeadline`, `TestAnUnreadableGatewayHoldsTheExemptLock`), T48's two (`TestAForeignTrafficPolicyHoldsTheExemptLockAcrossItsDeadline`, `TestAFailedForeignListHoldsTheExemptLock`), T58 (`TestAStaleHoldClearsWhenTheLockIsAbandoned`), and three rows not in the inventory, `TestAHoldIsNotAppliedToAnUnavailableCandidate`, `TestAHoldAfterAFailedVerdictComesBeforeIt` and `TestAHoldBesideAFailedPairComesBeforeIt`. A fact about the rows §1.1 specifies, not a defect of them. Tagged at T69's row because that row's subject, a Gateway-level auth policy standing beside a gated Agent's candidate, reads as if it covered clause 5's Gateway-level hold, and its mutation column names no clause-5 mutation; the tag makes that row's coverage complete. A23.17 asks whether to add one. No decision.
    - **A23.12** (iv, confirming §1.1). Approved, in "What the verdict proves, and what it grants": "Eval requests do not cross the gateway, the route's `-auth`, or any policy on the route." Change: none proposed. Both rows keep it true: the operator sends the cases to the candidate's revision Service, and only the callers' requests in the rows cross the gateway. Nothing observes the address directly; the review's mA, cases sent to the active revision's Service, is caught by T68 at `:85`, but only because revision 2's prefix differs from revision 1's. No decision.
    - **A23.13** (iv, read from the tests). Change: none proposed. Both rows write their suites as the cluster administrator, so neither exercises Q6 (c)'s policy; T62 does. No decision.
    - **A23.14** (iv, read from the tests; the review's NOTE 4). Change: none proposed. `applyGatewayLevelAuthPolicy` deletes its policy through `t.Cleanup`, which a failure runs and a `go test` timeout does not. On a reused cluster a timed-out run leaves `assayd-e2e-gated-gateway-auth` standing, and it holds every API-key `Create` or `Lock` of the next run that comes before T69, whose own pre-delete removes it. An `auth: none` route's transaction is not held, since it has no `401` to credit, but, reasoned from design 03 A75 and not measured, the leaked policy would refuse anonymous callers of that published route, and would put every served API-key Agent beside it under W1, `Degraded` with `PolicyApplyIncomplete=GatewayAuthPolicy`. `TestAGatewayLevelAuthPolicyHoldsANewAgent` had the same pattern before build step 10; CI's fresh cluster is unaffected. Unpinned. No decision.
    - **A23.15** (iv, read from the inventory). Change: none proposed. On k3s v1.33.6+k3s1 with agentgateway 1.5.0 on local k3d clusters, T68 took 96 s in the author's full `make e2e` and 135 s and 123 s in the review's two unmutated runs, and T69 35 s, 39 s and 39 s. CI's k3d lane, on k3s v1.35.5-k3s1, was green on PR #130 at `d7663f9`, with T68 at 127 s and T69 at 34 s, and on `2597bdf`, with T68 at 125 s and T69 at 64 s.     - **A23.18** (iv, measured by the author's mutation, re-run by the review). Approved: T68's mutation cell, "make the gate branch always pass, which lets the failing revision's prefix through the gateway". Change: none proposed. The mutation, m1 (`gate.satisfied = true` after `gateFor`), is caught at the first verdict wait (`gate_test.go:66`): revision 1 is promoted with no verdict, `waitForVerdict` fails, and the row stops before revision 2 exists, so no failing prefix ever reaches the gateway in that run. The cell names the right mutation and the wrong mechanism of its kill. Added after the second critique, at the end, so that no tag is renumbered. Tagged at T68's mutation cell. No decision.
    - **A23.17** (question), put to the human and not proposed: **should §1.1 gain an e2e row that reaches clause 5 on a real gateway?** As built: no §1.1 row does (A23.11); envtest pins clause 5, eight of its tests failing under the c5 mutant, which survives T68 and T69. Facts, each read from the code or from §1.1: clause 5 makes four reads — the Gateway and two `AgentgatewayPolicy` lists through the uncached reader, and a cached `Get` of `<agent>-serving` for its labels, as §1.1's "What clause 5 costs" counts them — and its hold depends on no gateway data path, so envtest's API server serves every input it reads; reaching it needs a J2 or K2 `Lock` at `ProbingAfter` whose serving revision has no recorded card and whose before-state was not observed (clauses 1 to 4), beside a gated candidate, which on a real cluster means a card that fails to validate while the revision serves. Nothing here measures how long such a row would take or how reliably it builds that state. **(a)** No new row; clause 5 stays pinned by envtest. **(b)** A follow-up row on k3d, building that state with a real Gateway-level policy and asserting `EvalWaitingForAuth` and no case sent until the policy goes. **Recommended: (a)**, because every input clause 5 reads is an API object that envtest's API server holds, so a real gateway adds no input to it; and of what envtest does not exercise, the operator's RBAC is exercised on k3d by the Agent reconciler's identical reads, under the one service account both controllers share, through the same `gatewayAuthPolicies` and `foreignTrafficPolicies` that T69 and `TestAGatewayLevelAuthPolicyHoldsANewAgent` run; that the eval controller is given the same Gateway configuration and uncached reader is read from `cmd/operator/main.go:205`, and its own path to clause 5 runs in no k3d row (A23.11); (b) would measure design 03's `Lock` on a real gateway more than it would clause 5.

- **A24 (2026-10-26, the drafter's; decided by the human on 2026-10-29, as recommended; answering #122 and #123; revised in place after eight critiques, the eighth a PASS whose minors are closed in place, and narrowed by the human on 2026-10-27 and 2026-10-28) — the text of two changes already decided, and two questions put to the human.** Two answers of the human left §1.1 owing "a short amendment plus a code PR": A21.4 (b), on PRs #117 and #120 (#122); and A20.38 (a), which #113 recorded (#123). §1.1 carries seven tags saying what was decided and what holds until it is built: five *(a follow-up amendment under #122 …)* and two *(#113 decided (a): …)*.     - **A24.1** (ii, #122). The exempt order.
      - Approved, in "What the gate branch derives in the exempt state": "After the head of the order both states share, the gate branch answers no card recorded for the candidate, `EvalAwaitingCard`, and then a candidate that is not available: its `Failed` verdict, then its pair in `failed[]`, else `EvalPaused`." and "Otherwise it takes the table's rows, from the run: … and `EvalFailed`, `EvalPairAlreadyFailed` or the promotion branch once a verdict is written."; and in "The gate branch's order", the exempt state's (4) to (9), "Build step 7 (PR #118) gave `decideGate` (`internal/controller/gate.go`) this order, in both states." and the "(A21.1 to A21.3)" that closes its reasons.
      - Change: A21.4 (b)'s order. In the exempt state, after (1) to (3): (4) a `Failed` verdict for the pair, `EvalFailed`, then the pair in `failed[]`, `EvalPairAlreadyFailed`; (5) no card recorded, `EvalAwaitingCard`; (6) the candidate not available, `EvalPaused`; (7) `blockedFor` does not match, the not-yet-evaluated `EvalWaitingForAuth`; (8) `blocked`, with its reason; (9) `EvalRunning`. Both states then answer a failed pair at (4), and differ only in the exempt state's not-yet-evaluated row and the other's transaction row; beside a failed pair, `GatesPassed` no longer flips on a NACK excursion (A21.4, "As built"). The exempt state's reasons become: (4) comes first for the reason it does outside it; (5) and (6) come before the derivation because `blockedFor` cannot come to match while no card is recorded or the candidate is away. A21.4 (b) gave the whole order, so no choice is left open.

    - **A24.2** (ii, #122). No clause-5 read on a verdict pass.
      - Approved, in "What wakes the eval controller while it waits": "Beside an exempt `Lock`, a pass whose pair has a final verdict, or is in `failed[]`, still evaluates clause 5 and writes its hold or empty, …" "It does so on a pass whose pair has a final verdict too, because the error is returned before the verdict branches." and "beside an exempt `Lock` too, hold or none"; in "What clause 5 costs": "A pass whose pair has a final verdict, or is in `failed[]`, comes round at least once per `CardDriftInterval` instead, … (A21.11)."; in A19's costing of clause 1's options, "so clause 5 goes on reading every 5 minutes with no bound"; and in "`blocked`: one slot, one computation", item 2, "otherwise `EvalWaitingForAuth`, when clauses 1 to 4 hold and clause 5 finds a hold;", and the paragraph after the list, "cleared if that pass finds no hold, and written again if the exempt state has come back and clause 5 still holds".
      - Change: as A21.4 (b) specifies. Beside an exempt `Lock`, a pass whose pair has a final verdict, `Passed` or `Failed`, or is in `failed[]`, makes no clause-5 read, writes `blocked` empty with `blockedFor` only when that differs from what is stored, returns `CardDriftInterval`, and never returns a clause-5 read error. A19's costing of an option not taken is history, and is tagged rather than rewritten. A matching `blockedFor` then no longer proves, in the exempt state, that clause 5 was evaluated; that is harmless, because rows (3) and (4) of the order answer every such pair first. "What wakes the eval controller while it waits"'s "beside an exempt `Lock` too, hold or none" becomes "beside an exempt `Lock` too".

Mutations: read clause 5 before the verdict cases (the unit row kills it); write the hold on a verdict pass (the envtest rows kill it); drop the verdict cases' `syncBlocked` call (the two clear rows kill it, which the eighth critique measured no other row doing).

Recommended: take.

The code only they use is deleted (rule 5): `holdFailedTail` (`internal/controller/evalclause5.go:115`–`:125`); the failed-pair branch of the not-yet-evaluated message (`gate.go:313`–`:318`) and of the `blocked` message (`:335`–`:340`); `pairHasFailed` (`:397`–`:403`); `failedBehind` (`:405`–`:414`). `holdClaimsTail` (`evalclause5.go:110`–`:113`) stays, since `clause5Hold` uses it (`:107`). `reasons.yaml`'s `EvalWaitingForAuth` entry (`internal/refgen/reasons.yaml:785`) loses the sentences from "A hold comes ahead of a `Failed` verdict" to "the verdict or the `failed[]` entry", and "for a pair that has already failed the gate branch replaces that message's closing sentence, … with one saying none of the pair is" (`:821`–`:823`); it gains that a failed pair is answered before it in both states, and an unavailable candidate before it in the exempt state, since outside it a recorded transaction that is not exempt comes first (`gate.go:288`, before `:294`).

    - **A24.4** (ii, #123). The fourth `blocked` reason, and where it is written.
      - Approved: the `blocked` row of the `status.eval` table, "among three that only the eval controller observes. They are …"; "`blocked`: one slot, one computation", "and three reasons share it" and "a pass whose run start stops at an unusable candidate address (step 3) writes only to clear a stored `EvalWaitingForAuth`"; "`blockedFor` says what was evaluated", "A pass whose run start stops at an unusable candidate address rewrites it only when it clears a stored hold, … (A21.6)."; A21.8's window, "a hold whose cause may have gone, or an empty slot that reads `EvalRunning` while clause 5 now holds"; and "One state comes close, … would be that pass's one write."
      - Change: a fourth reason, named by A24.7, is written to `blocked`, with `blockedFor` for the pair and the `Lock`, when a pass that would start a run or claim a case cannot derive the candidate's address (A24.5, subject to A24.19). Its place in the precedence is A24.17. What the pass returns is A24.20. **The write is still at most one per pass** (A21.6):

        | Pass | Its one `blocked` write |
        |---|---|
        | run start, address not derived | the fourth reason, in place of today's clear of a stored `EvalWaitingForAuth` (`startRun`, `evalcontroller.go:518`–`:530`), whose work it does by overwriting what is stored |
        | run start, address derived | the run-start patch, or a run-start refusal, as today; a refusal returns there (`evalcontroller.go:535`–`:541`), and after a run-start patch the pass goes on into `nextCase` (`:353`), whose `candidateAddress` re-reads the Service (`:590`) and re-runs `Check` with `status.eval.endpointPath` before the claim, as step 3 requires; a read failure or a refusal there writes nothing on that pass, which returns the error, and the next pass, a mid-run one, writes the fourth reason. Once the host and port have passed, that refusal is close to unreachable, because the request's URL is built with `url.URL`, which escapes the path (read from the code, not measured) |
        | mid-run, a case to claim | after finding the next case and deriving the address: the fourth reason, or empty |
        | mid-run, records `Interrupted` or finds every case recorded | empty, as today, deriving no address; a stored fourth reason beside an orphaned claim is cleared once and written again by the next pass |
        | held by clause 5 | the hold, as today; no Service is read |

      - Where the fourth reason is written, A21.6's two residues end — no `blockedFor` naming another pair or `Lock`, no stored run-start refusal still reported — and so does the near-miss, `EvalRunning` after a run start that stopped at an unusable address. A21.8's window covers it as every `blocked`: written before a NACK excursion, it is shown after the `Lock` comes back until the next eval pass. #113's record lists "A21.8 and A21.19" among the texts this amendment revises, most likely meaning A20.8 and A20.19, since #123 cites "the pre-fetch clear (A20.8, A21.6)"; A20.8's step 3 is tagged under A24.5, A20.19 is A24.6's, and A21.8 gains the clause above.
      - **How long it can outlive its cause**: the write of the fourth reason is an Agent status change, so the next pass is immediate, brought by the eval controller's `For(&Agent{})` watch (and the Agent reconciler's `GatesPassed` write may bring one more); it fails the same way and writes nothing, and from then on each pass returns the error and waits on the per-item backoff, which controller-runtime v0.24.1's default limiter, `NewTypedItemExponentialFailureRateLimiter(5ms, 1000s)` (`pkg/controller/controller.go:252`–`:255` in the module source), grows from 5 ms to a cap of 1000 s; a repeated failure writes the same value and raises no Agent event, and neither controller watches Services. The operator's own repair wakes it sooner: `ensureService`, on re-creating the Service, calls `persistServiceRecord` (`internal/controller/service.go:141`–`:146`), whose `Status().Update` (`:401`) is an Agent event. So the fourth reason stands for up to about 1000 s, about 17 minutes, only when a pass reads the cache before the re-created Service reaches it, or when a repair writes no Agent status (read from the code, not measured). Sends stay fail-closed meanwhile, because the address is derived before every claim.
      - Code: `candidateAddress` (`internal/controller/evalcontroller.go:587`–`:603`) returns a fault the pass can write, built from the Service's name and the fault (A24.7), never an error's text (A20.7); `startRun` (`:517`–`:532`); `Reconcile`'s `default` case (`:348`–`:352`) and `nextCase` (`:781`–`:784`) derive the address before the one write, and `nextCase` runs `Check` before every claim (step 3), and writes `blocked` only on a pass that has not already written it; the comment citing #113 (`:586`) and the `(#113)` in `TestRunningNamesNAndPausedNamesKOfN`'s comment (`evalcontroller_test.go:591`) go.
      - Test: a unit row, `TestAnUnaddressableCandidateIsNamed`, at run start and mid-run, with the Service missing, headless, and failing to read, each asserting the fourth reason and its `blockedFor`, and failing if the Agent reads `EvalRunning`; `TestARunStartPassWritesBlockedOnce` (`internal/controller/exemption_test.go:655`) extended to the mid-run pass **with a non-empty `blocked` stored first**, without which a write of empty before the fourth reason stores no change; `TestAHoldFoundGoneIsClearedBeforeARunStartThatStopsShort` (`:621`) rewritten; `TestAnUnusableAddressClaimsNothing` (`evalcontroller_test.go:341`) rewritten, since it asserts `Status.Eval == nil` (`:355`–`:358`) and the fourth reason creates `status.eval`: it keeps its no-fetch, no-claim and no-send assertions, and asserts no case or verdict recorded and `blocked` the fourth reason with `blockedFor` for the pair, as the eighth critique's build measured it failing; `TestAnAddressThatBreaksMidRunClaimsNothing` (`:364`) kept. Mutations: skip the write; write empty before deriving the address mid-run.
      - Cost: one `blocked` write when the state begins and one when it ends; no new read, since the Service is read today on those passes (`:590`), through the cache; the staleness bound above. If declined: #113 (a) is not built, and #123 stays open. Depends on A24.5, A24.7, A24.17 and A24.19. Tags: six — the `blocked` row, "`blocked`: one slot, one computation", "`blockedFor` says what was evaluated", A21.8's window, the near-miss sentence, and step 3 (with A24.5). Recommended: take.
    - **A24.5** (ii, #123). Which address errors write it.
      - Approved: step 3, "when it refuses, or the candidate's Service cannot be read, the pass sends nothing, and claims and records no case"; and "writes only to clear a stored `EvalWaitingForAuth`". The human's decision, as #113 and #123 record it: the eval controller writes the fourth reason "when `evalclient.Check` refuses the derived address or the candidate's Service cannot be read".
      - Change: those words. A failure of the Service read (`r.Get`, `evalcontroller.go:590`) and a `Check` refusal (`:596`) write the fourth reason, except on a run-start pass, whose one write was the run-start patch and which writes nothing more (A24.4); whether a first read failure that is not NotFound writes at once is A24.19. The message names a read failure by its class from `readErrorClass` (`evalclause5.go:129`), and a `Check` refusal by the part refused (A24.7), so a fault that persists writes once.
      - Code: `candidateAddress`; and `evalclient.Check` (`internal/evalclient/client.go:213`–`:228`), which today returns `fmt.Errorf` values only, returns a typed refusal naming the part refused — the host, the port, or the request for the path — so that the message names it without reading error text (A20.7), and `Check` stays the one validator (step 3). Test: `TestAnUnaddressableCandidateIsNamed`'s failing-read row; a unit row in `internal/evalclient` asserting each refusal's part. Mutation: write nothing on a read error that is not NotFound (under A24.19 (a)). If declined: the human's note names the errors that write it; for every other error, today's clear of a stored `EvalWaitingForAuth` at run start must be kept, or rule 8's exception #113 named returns. Tags: three — step 3, "What wakes the eval controller while it waits", and "`blocked`: one slot" (with A24.4). Recommended: take.

      - Change: four reasons. The gate branch's closed `blocked` switch (`gate.go:325`–`:332`) gains the fourth, `Ready` reason `AwaitingGates`. The `GatesPassed` table gains a row after `EvalBindingUnsupported`'s: "`status.eval.blocked` says the candidate's address cannot be derived | `False/` the fourth reason, from `blocked` | `Held` | `True/AwaitingGates` | `True/Available` / `False/AwaitingGates`". It is `blocked` in "The gate branch's order", (8) in both states. `EvalPaused`'s message names a matched fourth reason, as A21.9 has it name a matched hold or refusal, because otherwise "one starts once it is available again" is a promise the standing reason denies. A20.19's sentence is kept: `EvalRunning` still "asserts nothing about cases being sent", because the row can be shown between a Service going and the next eval pass. `reasons.yaml` gains the entry; `EvalPaused`'s operator text (`reasons.yaml:655`–`:662`), which names a matched clause-5 hold and run-start refusal, gains the fourth reason; and `EvalRunning`'s note, "A reason of its own needs a design 16 amendment (#113)" (`reasons.yaml:729`–`:735`), is rewritten to name it.

Mutation: drop the case from either switch. If declined: the gate branch shows `EvalRunning` for it, the state #113 (a) ends. Tags: three — "Three reasons are observed", the `EvalRunning` row, the `EvalPaused` row. Recommended: take.
    - **A24.7** (question, #123): **what is the fourth reason called, and what does its message say?**
      - **(a)** `EvalCandidateUnaddressable`, the name A20.38 and #113 offered. **(b)** `EvalCandidateServiceUnusable`. **(c)** `EvalAddressUnusable`.
      - The message, under any name: "the eval controller cannot derive an address to send a case to: <fault>. Nothing is fetched, claimed or sent, and no outcome is recorded, because the fault is not the candidate's. The Agent reconciler re-creates a missing Service, and replaces a headless one it recorded, on a pass that reaches its Service step; its own conditions say when it does not." <fault> is "the candidate's Service <namespace>/<name> was not found", "… could not be read (<class>)" — near-unreachable, since a cached read blocks rather than fails (A24.19), leaving a cancelled context, since no reconciliation timeout is set — or, for a `Check` refusal, "the candidate's Service <namespace>/<name>: " followed by the part refused (`internal/evalclient/client.go:213`–`:228`): "its ClusterIP <ip> is not an IP address" (`None` and empty included; empty for an `ExternalName` Service), "spec.runtime.port <n> is not from 1 to 65535" — the port is the Agent's (`evalcontroller.go:595`) — or "no request can be built for the path <path>". `Check`'s `net.SplitHostPort` refusal has no part of its own and is unreachable on `JoinHostPort`'s output; the code PR maps it to the host. The repair sentence is hedged because the Agent reconciler does not reach its Service step while a pin refusal stands or a source is unresolved.
      - **Recommended: (a)**: the name the human read when deciding, and it matches `ActiveRevisionServiceUnaddressable` (`reasons.yaml:48`), so one idea keeps one word.

    - **A24.14** (v, process). Who records the decision, and who folds.
      - Change: **on the day the human decides A24, a docs-only change records it**: A24's head and decision table say what was decided; each A24 tag says "decided" or "declined" in place of "proposed", and **keeps only the branch of the answer taken**, the others pruned; the opening's *(A24: …)* tag is reworded to say A24 is decided; A24 enters `humanDecidedParts` (`test/docs/approval_parts_test.go:74`), whose comment (`:57`–`:73`), which says each entry was "folded into §1.1 by the fold that records them here", gains a sentence saying A24 was recorded before its folds, which its code PRs make — and whose "decided as recommended" is edited if the human departs from any recommendation; and the README row, `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` say so, as `TestADecidedAmendmentIsRecordedAsDecided` (`:1041`; it reads those, `:1090`–`:1094`) requires; `docs/architecture.md:244` and its mirror `docs/architecture.html:882` say so too, which no test checks. **Each code PR then folds its own items**: it writes the decided text into §1.1, removes, in A22's removal form, the decided tags it ends and A24's tags of its items, and drops its issue from §1.1 opening's list "(#122, #123, #133, #135)" and from the same list at `docs/architecture.md:244` and `docs/architecture.html:882`; the #123 fold also updates `docs/research/design16-slice1-inventory-2026-10.md:9`, which says an unusable address "still reads `EvalRunning`", and `:100`, which names `TestAnUnusableAddressClaimsNothing` as that path's pin; the first fold also removes `docs/architecture.html:882`'s "#121's departure and", drift on `main` that `docs/architecture.md:244` no longer carries; the last removes the *(A24: …)* tag.
      - Cost: each fold is reviewed inside a code review, not by a critique whose only question is faithfulness; both earlier folds needed such critiques, and #136's second returned REVISE with a MAJOR. So each code PR's review must include a faithfulness check of its folded text. If declined: a separate fold after the code, under its own critique. Tags: one, the *(A24: …)* tag. Recommended: take, because a fold that lands with its code cannot describe code not yet merged.

Why: each change has its own rows and mutations; #122 and #123 touch the same functions, `decideGate` and `Reconcile`, which serial PRs keep apart, and #122 first because #123's `blocked` writes sit in the branches #122 leaves; and each PR folds one issue's tags (A24.14). Cost: two review loops, and #123 waits on #122; one PR would be one loop for a diff twice the size. If declined: the human's note says how many. Tags: none. Recommended: take.

    - **A24.17** (ii, #123). Where the fourth reason stands in `blocked`'s precedence.
      - Approved: "`blocked`: one slot, one computation", whose first item, the run-start refusal, is computed "only once the hold has cleared"; and A20.38 (a), "first in `blocked`'s precedence".
      - Change: first in the numbered list, in the list's own sense: "1. the fourth reason, when this pass would start a run or claim a case and cannot derive the candidate's address (A24.5), except on a run-start pass, whose one write was the run-start patch and which writes nothing more (A24.4). A pass that clause 5 holds would do neither, so it reads no Service, and the fourth reason is computed only once the hold has cleared;". It outranks the run-start refusal because the run-start fetch goes to that address; clause 5's hold outranks both (A21.10).
      - If declined: an absolute first, outranking the hold, which needs a cached Service read on every held pass. Both readings show a true reason. Tags: one, at the list's first item. Recommended: take, because it is what A21.10 does for the run-start refusal.
    - **A24.18** — moved to A25 (#133), by the human's decision of 2026-10-27.
    - **A24.19** (question, #123): **is a Service read failure guarded against flapping `blocked`?** A20.38 (a), which the human took, listed among its costs "keeping a transient Service read error from flapping `blocked`". Facts, read from the code: `candidateAddress` reads through the manager's cache (`r.Get`; Services are not in `DisableFor`, `cmd/operator/main.go:269`). In controller-runtime v0.24.1 (`go.mod:15`), a cached get blocks until that type's informer has synced (`pkg/cache/internal/informers.go:329`–`:331` in the module source: `shouldBlock && started && !i.Informer.HasSynced()` calls `cache.WaitForCacheSync`), and a Forbidden list leaves the informer retrying, so the get blocks rather than fails. A synced cached get returns the object or NotFound; the only other failures left are a sync wait ended by a cancelled context or a timeout. *(The second draft said a run's start after `workloadAvailableIn` proved the sync; that reads a Deployment, not a Service, and proved nothing.)* NotFound is the realistic failure, and it is a true state, not a transient: a Service deleted and re-created writes the fourth reason once and clears it once. `syncBlocked` writes only when the value changes, and the message carries a class, not text, so a failure that persists writes once.
      - #123's record lists as owed "flapping on transient Service read errors guarded (A20.38's cost list)". (a) answers it by the facts above, not by a guard; (b) by a guard; (c) by writing no fourth reason for the failures a guard would catch.
      - **(a)** No separate guard: every read failure writes the fourth reason. A20.38 (a)'s cost is met by the two facts above — one write in and one out for each change of state, and none while it persists. Under (a) the tags at step 3, "What wakes the eval controller while it waits", "`blocked`: one slot" and the near-miss read as written.
      - **(b)** A guard in memory: a read failure that is not NotFound writes the fourth reason only when the previous pass for the same Agent, in this process, failed with the same class. A first such failure does not write the fourth reason: at run start it keeps today's clear of a stored `EvalWaitingForAuth`, and mid-run it writes `blocked` empty, as today's `default` case does (`evalcontroller.go:348`–`:352`), so no stale hold or refusal is left standing; the near-miss sentence then stays, for that first pass. Cost: per-Agent state that decides a status write, lost on restart or leader change; a unit row and a mutation.
      - **(c)** Narrow the reason to NotFound and `Check` refusals; other read failures never write it, keep today's clear at run start, and write `blocked` empty mid-run, as today. So, mid-run, such a failure leaves the Agent reading `EvalRunning` while nothing is sent, the state #113 (a) exists to end; and at run start, keeping today's clear, it leaves the near-miss and A21.6's two residues standing for those failures. It narrows the human's "cannot be read".
      - **Recommended: (a)**: the failure (b) guards against barely occurs on a cached read, (b)'s state costs more than the writes it saves, and (a) builds the human's words.
    - **A24.20** (ii, #123). What an address-fault pass returns. A20.38 (a)'s cost list, which the human took, includes "choosing what the pass returns", so it is put here.
      - Approved: "A pass whose candidate's Service cannot be read, or whose address `evalclient.Check` refuses, returns the error, so controller-runtime's per-item backoff governs it and no requeue interval applies" (A20.28, in "What wakes the eval controller while it waits").
      - Change: none to that sentence; the pass that writes the fourth reason, and every pass after it on which the fault stands, still returns the error. Reason: the fault is the operator's to repair, the error reaches the log, and the repair's own Agent status write wakes the next pass (A24.4).
      - Cost: where that wake-up does not come, the fourth reason can stand up to about 1000 s after the repair (A24.4). If declined: the pass writes the reason and returns `RequeueAfter: CardRetryInterval`, 15 s, as §1.1 already gives a failed run-start fetch because "Nothing in Agent status changes" to wake it; that bounds the staleness at 15 s, at the price of a pass every 15 s for as long as the fault stands. Tags: none, since it changes no sentence. Recommended: take.

- **A25 (2026-10-30, the drafter's; proposed, and not decided by the human; answering #133; revised in place after three critiques, and narrowed after the second) — the text of A22.16 (b): four items that build it, one process item, and six questions put to the human.** A22.16 asked what a revision that bypassed its gate reads once it is unpinned. The human answered on PR #128, "as recommended", which took A22.16's option (b): "A follow-up that keeps a serving form while the gate is enforced and `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict for the serving revision, pinned or not, derived from status alone as A22.6's is." Its cost, accepted with it: "the condition then also fires for a revision that served before the gate was added". A22's decision record words the answer "a follow-up amendment and code PR keep a serving form of `GatesBypassed` while the gate is enforced and `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict for the serving revision, pinned or not (#133)", and §1.1's *(A22.16 decided (b): …)* tag says "a serving form of `GatesBypassed`" too. A22.16's recommendation, which the human took with it, left "the shape, a widened `GatesBypassed` or a new type" to this follow-up. A24 first carried #133, as A24.8 to A24.11, A24.16 and A24.18, and the human split it out on 2026-10-27 (A24, "#133 is split out").
  - **Departures from decided or approved text, all of them.** "Pinned or not", "derived from status alone", and the firing for a revision active before the gate are built as decided (A25.1, A25.8), and no option narrows them. **One departs, and only if the human takes it**: A25.2 (b), a new type, departs from the record's wording "a serving form of `GatesBypassed`", though not from the recommendation the human took. **One reverses a record, and only if the human takes it**: A25.5 (i) reverses A22.9's recorded behaviour, which A22.9 marks "No decision". **One leaves part of the decided state unnamed under every answer offered**: a revision that passed an earlier suite and never ran the suite in force reads as passed once unpinned (A25.11), because matching the suite in force would need a read beyond status. A6's approved rule that the eval controller's read-back is its "only guard against a stale CRD", and "does not also check the schema", stands under every answer.
  - **Decided.** *Not decided. On the day the human decides, a docs-only change fills this bullet with the answers and the human's words (A25.10).*
  - **Tag forms.** A25 uses A20's terms, tag forms, kinds of item and deciding, with A22's and A24's additions, with `A25` in place of `A20`. A tag whose text depends on an answer says so; a tag that names no answer holds under every answer. A25 removes no tag, and adds ten proposed tags in §1.1 and one *(A25: …)* tag at §1.1's opening. Code citations are to `main` at `2cbfc2b`, each opened there. "Measured by critique N of A24" marks what that critique, posted on PR #140, built in a scratch copy of `1b21915` and ran on envtest; the code it built on reads the same at `2cbfc2b` (read from the code). "Measured by A25's critique N" marks what that critique built at `2cbfc2b`, under A25.2 (b), A25.4 (b2) and A25.5 (i), and ran on envtest; its report and spike are kept with the drafting session's record and are posted on A25's pull request when it opens. A25's first critique measured that the whole envtest suite passed, that A25.4's two-controller row killed both its mutations, and that the Agent reconciler made no status write while `status.eval` read `Running`. Everything else is read from the code, not measured.
  - **The state #133 names.** `GatesBypassed`'s serving form needs a pin that names the active revision (`servingPinBypass`, `internal/controller/pinrule.go:132`–`:148`), so it is withdrawn on the first pass after the pin is removed. If the spec mints the active revision, `GatesPassed=False/ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated` names it (`assessPromotedGate`, `internal/controller/agent_controller.go:2163`–`:2183`); otherwise nothing names the active revision as unpassed. **One fact shapes every option**: `status.eval` records one pair, and a run start replaces it, keeping only `failed[]` (`startRun`'s patch, `internal/controller/evalcontroller.go:557`–`:566`). Nothing else replaces a `Passed` record (`clearSuperseded`, `:418`–`:422`, clears only a `Failed` one). So from the next candidate's run start, `status.eval` no longer records the `Passed` verdict that promoted the active revision.
  - **Every change, numbered.** Kind (ii), 4 (A25.1, A25.6, A25.8, A25.9); kind (v), 1 (A25.10); six questions (A25.2 to A25.5, A25.7, A25.11). A25.11 sits after A25.4, whose rule it settles, and is numbered last so that earlier references hold. All answer #133. The decision table's last column says which answers each item depends on.
    - **A25.1** (ii, #133). The unpassed statement.
      - Approved: in "Where each reason is set", "It is withdrawn on the first pass on which it no longer derives: the pin removed, …"; and in the row "a pinned rollback that Q5 admits", the serving form "for a pin naming the revision serving while `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict for it".
      - Change: while the gate is enforced, `status.activeRevisionDigest` is set, and no `Passed` verdict is recorded for it, pinned or not, a condition says so. This entry calls it **the unpassed statement**. Its type is A25.2, what "recorded" reads A25.4, its reason A25.7, and its message A25.9. The promotion branch sets `status.activeRevision` and its digest together (`agent_controller.go:1073`), so an Agent with a name and no digest is not named; that is reachable only on status written before digests were recorded (read from the code, not measured).
      - **It is defined by derivation.** It is a function of the spec and status alone, derived in `assessGates`'s `gateEnforced` case, from status as the pass found it, so a pass that exits early still carries it (`agent_controller.go:2113`–`:2121`), and again after the rollout switch, from the status the pass leaves, where `GatesBypassed` is derived (`:1100`–`:1116`). It reads no stored condition. It is owned and not sticky: set on every pass on which it derives, and withdrawn on the first on which it does not. So a `Passed` verdict recorded for the active revision, the promotion of a revision that has one, and a gate removed or not enforced each withdraw it; a pin removed, refused or changed does not.
      - **It moves nothing.** No `Ready`, `Progressing`, `Degraded` or phase change, no hold, and no requeue. The gate never reads it, nor `passed[]`: promotion reads `status.eval`'s pair alone (`internal/controller/gate.go:264`), so a revision `passed[]` records is evaluated again when it is the candidate.
      - **It adds no status write.** Its value changes only when the active revision, the gate, what is recorded for the active revision, or whether `spec.release.targetRevisionDigest` is set (A25.9) changes. Under A25.4 (b2), a run start, a claim and a case record change none of them; under (b1) the same holds except once per Agent in its upgrade residue, when the first run start after the upgrade removes the only record of the active revision's pass. Under (b2), the run start moves the active revision's pair from `status.eval` into `passed[]` in one patch, and never evicts it (A25.4). So the Agent reconciler's status write stays a no-op while cases record (A5; T31, T33). Measured by critique 2 of A24 and by A25's first critique: T33 (`TestEveryCaseIsSentOnceBesideTheAgentReconciler`) and the whole envtest suite passed. Under A25.4 (a), the statement flips at every run start.
      - Code: a function beside `servingPinBypass`, called from `assessGates` and the block after the switch; under A25.2 (b), the type in `api/v1alpha1`, in `designConditions()` (`api/v1alpha1/agent_types.go:1165`) and in `ownedTypes` (`internal/controller/conditions.go:67`); a `reasons.yaml` entry.
      - Test, in envtest: (1) an ungated revision A is active and a gate is added with the spec minting A: the statement names A; B passes and is promoted: it is withdrawn. (2) A pinned revision B is active with no `Passed` verdict; the pin is removed and the spec mints C: the statement names B throughout C's run. (3) A25.4's two-controller row. (4) The condition written by hand on an Agent whose active revision passed is withdrawn on the next pass. (5) The gate removed: withdrawn. And a unit row that runs one promoting pass and asserts that what it writes carries no statement for the revision it replaced. Mutations: require a pin (rows 1 and 2); drop the derivation after the switch (the unit row); drop the type from `ownedTypes` (row 4); assert on a pass that does not enforce the gate (row 5).
      - Cost: one condition on every gated Agent whose active revision has no recorded `Passed` verdict, until a revision that passes is promoted, including every Agent that gains a gate over an active revision (A25.8). **Downgrade**: an operator rolled back to a release whose `ownedTypes` lacks the new type leaves it standing for good, because `merge` keeps a type it does not own as another controller's (`conditions.go:159`–`:160`); and an older operator's whole-status `Status().Update` (`agent_controller.go:2488`) erases `passed[]`. Under A25.2 (a), only the second applies. If declined: A22.16 (b) is not built, and #133 stays open. Tags: one, beside the *(A22.16 decided (b): …)* tag. Recommended: take.
    - **A25.2** (question, #133): **which condition type carries it?** `kubectl get` and most dashboards show a condition's type and status, not its reason.
      - **(a)** Widen `GatesBypassed`, the shape the record's wording names. It then means "the active revision has no recorded `Passed` verdict", not "a pin bypassed the gate". Beside an admitted pin to another revision not yet active, the pending form is carried, because it announces the promotion Q5 (a) exists to announce, and gains one sentence (A25.9). Costs: `GatesBypassed=True` on every Agent that gains a gate over an active revision, a type claiming a bypass where no pin did anything; `reasons.yaml:1107`, "A refused pin never names the revision serving, so no `GatesBypassed` is derived for it", becomes false; and, measured by A25's first critique, eight envtest functions fail, all because each expects no `GatesBypassed` beside a refused pin or once the pin stops resolving. T33 passes. No design 02 edit.
      - **(b)** A new type, **`ActiveRevisionPassUnrecorded`**, `True` while the statement derives, absent otherwise, owned and not sticky, with one reason (A25.7). It departs from the record's wording, as A24.16 (b) said of the same option; if taken, A25.10 marks A22's record. The name says what the record shows, not that the revision failed or never ran, because in A25.4's residues a revision that passed has no recorded pass: measured by A25's second critique, a revision promoted on `EvalPassed` whose next candidate failed before `passed[]` existed reads the statement. "Active", not "serving", because #138's review measured "is serving" false beside `Ready=False/WorkloadUnavailable`. Costs: a constant, an `ownedTypes` entry, a `reasons.yaml` entry and the regenerated reference; a pinned active revision with no recorded pass carries both this and `GatesBypassed`'s serving form (measured by A25's first critique), one fact in two types; and **an edit to design 02 §3.1**, because `TestConditionVocabularyIsClosed` (`api/v1alpha1/schema_contract_test.go:193`) reads §3.1's `conditions:` list (`docs/designs/02-agent-crd-operator.md:97`) and fails on a constant the list lacks, measured by critique 2 of A24 (A25.3). Measured by A25's first critique: under (b), the whole envtest suite passed unchanged.
      - Considered, not offered: widening `GatesBypassed` for pins only (A24.16 (c)), which would leave a revision active before the gate unnamed once the spec mints another, narrowing the decision.
      - **Recommended: (b)**: no two forms of one condition compete, `GatesBypassed`'s forms and every existing test are unchanged under A25.5 (ii) (measured under (i) as well), and the type claims only what the record shows.
    - **A25.3** (question, #133, under A25.2 (b)): **who edits design 02 §3.1, and under what review?** A25 cannot amend design 02. The human's decision of 2026-10-13 on #99 covers "the changes [the slice] requires in design 02", each going "through design 02's own process … this issue does not approve it"; A25 would be a decided amendment, not an approved part (`humanDecidedParts`'s comment, `test/docs/approval_parts_test.go:57`–`:75`). No pull request has added a type to §3.1; before pull requests began, two commits did, beside their code, `be050bf` (`Progressing`) and `86b81ea` (`RevisionHashCollision`, design 02 A37), and docs-only design 02 amendments added most of the list, among them A15, written from design 03's A1, A2 and A6 (`53c30b7`: `PolicyCompileFailed`, `PolicyApplyIncomplete`, `GatewayIncompatible`), `8f5e702` (`ModelDrifted`, `GovernanceSkipped`), A20 (`b5e7339`: `EnvSourceUnresolved`) and A26 (`ae09073`: `EnvSourceProtectionUnavailable`, `RevisionMaterialChanged`) (A25's third critique). Design 02 is not approved, so a design 02 amendment needs no approval, only the human's decision where a design 16 decision rests on it. So nothing authorises the edit until the human does, and without it A25.2 (b) cannot pass `make unit`.
      - **(a)** The answer here authorises it: the #133 code PR carries a numbered design 02 amendment that adds the type to §3.1's list, with one sentence saying design 16 §1.1 defines it, and nothing else; the PR's independent review checks it against A25 as decided. Design 02 stays unapproved, and the type joins #99's list.
      - **(b)** Design 02's own process first: a docs-only PR proposes the amendment, an independent critique runs, the human decides, and the #133 code PR waits. Cost: one more loop and one more decision for one word.
      - **Recommended: (a)**: the word and its meaning are decided here, on this text; (b) if the human wants every change to design 02's vocabulary to pass through design 02's own process.
    - **A25.4** (question, #133): **what does "no `Passed` verdict recorded for the active revision" read?**

      | Option | Reads | Written by | Residue |
      |---|---|---|---|
      | **(a)** | `status.eval` alone, as A22.6's form reads it | nothing new | **measured wrong on every gated rollout**: by critique 2 of A24, B, which passed and was promoted, read unpassed throughout C's run; by critique 1's trace, for good if C fails |
      | **(b1)** | `status.eval` and a new `status.eval.passed[]` | the eval controller, in the verdict patch that writes `Passed` (step 7; `withOutcome`, `evalcontroller.go:953`–`:979`); the run-start patch must then keep `passed[]`, since it keeps only `failed[]` today (`:563`), and built without that, (b1) behaves as (a) (measured by A25's first critique) | a revision that passed before the code merges and is still active has no entry: from the first run start after the upgrade it reads unpassed, and for good if that candidate fails; and A25.11's residue |
      | **(b2)** | as (b1) | the run-start patch (`:557`–`:566`): when the record it replaces holds a `Passed` verdict, it appends that pair | a gated Agent whose `status.eval` no longer holds its active revision's `Passed` record when the code merges — a candidate running or failed then, a revert after a failed candidate, or a candidate superseded mid-run — reads unpassed until a revision that passes is promoted (measured by A25's second critique); and A25.11's residue |

      - Neither adds a write: each fills `passed[]` in a patch the eval controller already makes. (b2) catches every replacement, because only the run-start patch replaces a `Passed` record (critique 3 of A24 and A25's second critique read the same).
      - Considered, not offered: a record of how the active revision was promoted, written by the Agent reconciler (A24.9 (c)). It names no field or reading for revisions promoted before it existed, and reading those "as passed" would narrow the decision.
      - **`passed[]`'s rules.** Each entry is an `EvalPair`, `{revisionDigest, suiteDigest}`, `failed[]`'s type (`api/v1alpha1/agent_types.go:903`), **keyed by revision digest**, which is what every reader matches on: an append replaces the revision's existing entry and moves it to the newest place, so at most one entry names any revision. `failed[]`'s `appendFailed` (`evalcontroller.go:985`–`:991`) does not de-duplicate, because a failed pair never runs again. The cap is 10, as a CRD `maxItems` and a code constant, like `failed[]`'s (`agent_types.go:838`, `evalcontroller.go:983`). When full, the oldest entry whose revision digest is not `status.activeRevisionDigest` is evicted; at most one entry names the active revision, so one can always be evicted, the active revision's entry never is, and a run start never makes the statement appear. The patch reads the digest under the same optimistic lock. *(A pair key, as first drafted, let ten entries name the active revision, and the CRD refuses an eleventh: measured by A25's third critique, "status.eval.passed: Too many: 11: must have at most 10 items", which would hold the next candidate for good.)* Which suite a reader matches against is A25.11. A pin to an old revision whose entry was evicted reads unpassed.
      - Tests: **a two-controller envtest row**, in one manager: A passes and is promoted; B's run starts; `passed[]` holds A, no statement names A, and the Agent reconciler makes no status write while B's cases record; B fails, and the statement is still absent. Mutations: drop the append; read `status.eval` alone. Measured by critique 2 of A24 under (b2), and by A25's first critique: the row killed both, and no other row did. A unit row for the key, the cap and the active entry kept (mutations: append a second entry for a revision; evict nothing; evict the active entry). `TestStatusEvalCarriesTheSlicesRecord` (`api/v1alpha1/evalsuite_schema_test.go:123`) extended with `passed` and its `maxItems`. The upgrade residue is pinned by a new row that writes `status.eval` with no `passed[]` and asserts the statement names the active revision; `TestAServingPinThatPassedEarlierSaysOnlyWhatIsRecorded` (`test/envtest/pin_test.go:999`) asserts only `GatesBypassed` and `GatesPassed`, so it cannot. `passed[]` joins #99's list of `EvalStatus` fields design 02 owes.
      - **Recommended: (b2)**: (a) is measured wrong on every gated rollout; (b2) reads what the eval controller holds at the moment it replaces it, and leaves the smaller residue. Tags: three, at the `status.eval` table's count, at step 1's run-start write, and, under (b1), at step 7's verdict patch.
    - **A25.11** (question, #133, under A25.4 (b1) or (b2)): **against which verdicts does `passed[]` match a revision?** A25's third critique measured, on the recommended set with the rule as first drafted, two states in which a revision that served by pin is named by no condition once the pin is removed, while under A25.4 (a) the statement names it in both:
      - **a later failure**: B passes S1; C passes S1 and is promoted, and C's run start moves (B, S1) into `passed[]`; the suite is edited to S2, the spec reverted to B, and B fails S2, so `failed[]` holds (B, S2); the spec goes back to C, the suite is edited to S3, and B is pinned, which Q5 (a) admits; then the pin is removed. Pinned, `GatesBypassed`'s serving form names (B, S2); unpinned, nothing names B, and nothing does while C then fails against S3;
      - **the suite in force never run**: B passes S1; C passes S1 and is promoted; the suite is edited to S2; B is pinned, then unpinned. B never ran S2, and nothing names it.
      - **(a)** Any verdict, as first drafted: an entry stands until it is evicted. Both states above are silent once the pin is removed, the state #133 exists for.
      - **(b)** A `Failed` verdict removes the revision's entry from `passed[]`, in the verdict patch that appends to `failed[]` (step 7), so `passed[]` holds the revisions whose last verdict was `Passed`. No new write, no Agent status change while cases record (the candidate is never the active revision), and still derived from status alone. Measured by A25's third critique: with it, B is named once unpinned, and while C fails. Under A25.4 (b1), the same patch appends on `Passed` and removes on `Failed`. **Residue**: a revision that passed an earlier suite and never ran the suite in force, or whose later run was superseded before a verdict, reads as passed once unpinned.
      - Not offered: matching the suite in force. Status does not hold the `EvalSuite`'s current digest, so the statement would read the suite, departing from "derived from status alone".
      - Tests: one envtest row per state above. Mutations: drop the removal, which the later-failure row kills; read `status.eval` alone, which both kill.
      - **Recommended: (b)**: it names the later failure the decision exists to name, at no cost in writes, and leaves only the residue no status-only reading can close. Tags: one, at step 7's `failed[]` append.
    - **A25.5** (question, #133, under A25.4 (b1) or (b2)): **do the conditions that already say whether the active revision passed read `passed[]` too?** Three readers read `status.eval`'s pair alone today: `assessPromotedGate`, which sets `GatesPassed=True/EvalPassed` or `False/ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated` when the spec mints the active revision; the promotion branch's skip of `GatesPassed` for a pinned revision (`agent_controller.go:1084`); and `servingPinBypass`'s test (`pinrule.go:137`, through `evalPassedFor`, `:116`–`:118`).
      - **(i)** All three read it, and `EvalPassed` names the newest pair for the revision. Then a revert to a revision `passed[]` records reads `GatesPassed=True/EvalPassed` with "the score is not kept" (measured by A25's first critique), where today it reads `ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated`; and a pin to such a revision reads no serving form and `GatesPassed=True/EvalPassed`, **reversing A22.9's record**, "reads the serving form's 'another revision' branch, and the promotion branch no longer asserts `GatesPassed=True/EvalPassed` for it". **It hides a later failure**: because `passed[]` matches against any suite and a pin is refused only on a `failed[]` pair for the current suite digest, a revision that passed suite S1, then failed S2, and is pinned after an edit to S3 reads `GatesPassed=True/EvalPassed` against S1, and no condition names the (B, S2) failure `failed[]` records; today the serving form names it (measured by A25's second critique, `TestA25SpikeAPinToARevisionThatFailedALaterSuite`). Text made stale and rewritten: `reasons.yaml`'s `EvalPassed` (`:674`), `ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated` (`:32`) and `PinnedRollback` (`:1136`) entries; the comments at `agent_controller.go:1085`–`:1087`, `:1100`–`:1107` and `:2158`–`:2172`, and at `pinrule.go:114`–`:131`; and the `targetRevisionDigest` field's doc, "reads GatesBypassed only while status.eval records no Passed verdict for it" (`api/v1alpha1/agent_types.go:439`–`:441`), which `kubectl explain` shows and which is regenerated into both CRD files and `docs/reference/crd-agent.md` (A25's second critique). Rows owed: the revert, the pin, and the later-failure state; mutation: either reader reads `status.eval` alone.
      - **(ii)** Only the unpassed statement reads it. `GatesPassed` and `GatesBypassed` keep today's readings, A22.9's record stands, and a later failure is named by the serving form **while the pin stands**; once it is removed, it is named by the statement only under A25.11 (b). Two sentences that say what "status.eval" records become false of `status.eval` as a whole, since `passed[]` sits inside it, and say "`status.eval`'s pair" instead: `servingRecord`'s "so whether this revision passed its gate … is not recorded" (`pinrule.go:170`–`:175`) and `assessPromotedGate`'s "not a Passed verdict for this one" (`:2175`–`:2176`); and, for the same reason, §1.1's "a pinned rollback" row's "while `status.eval` records no `Passed` verdict for it", the `targetRevisionDigest` field's doc (`api/v1alpha1/agent_types.go:439`–`:441`, regenerated into `config/crd/assayd.dev_agents.yaml`, `charts/assayd/crds/assayd.dev_agents.yaml` and `docs/reference/crd-agent.md`), `reasons.yaml`'s `PinnedRollback` entry (`:1141`–`:1148`, generated into `docs/reference/conditions.md`), the comments at `agent_controller.go:1100`–`:1104` and `pinrule.go:121`–`:124`, and `TestAServingPinThatPassedEarlierSaysOnlyWhatIsRecorded`'s comment (`test/envtest/pin_test.go:994`–`:998`). The statement must not reuse `evalPassedFor` (`pinrule.go:116`–`:118`), which the serving form and the pinned skip share: extending it builds (i). Row owed: the pinned later-failure state, asserting the serving form names (B, S2); mutation: the serving form reads `passed[]`, which A25's third critique measured surviving all 95 existing pin, gate, bypass and serving-revision rows and killed only by that row. Cost: after a revert to a revision `passed[]` records, `GatesPassed=False/ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated` stands with no statement beside it, a reason whose name claims the revision was not evaluated, as it does today; and a pin to such a revision reads `GatesBypassed`'s serving form for a revision that passed an earlier suite, as A22.9 records.
      - Existing rows, read from the code, not measured: `TestAGateAddedOverAServingRevisionSaysItWasNotEvaluated` (`test/envtest/gate_test.go:493`) and `TestAServingRevisionKeepsItsPassedRecordAcrossAnAbandonedCandidate` (`test/envtest/evalcontroller_test.go:1109`) are unchanged under either answer.
      - **Recommended: (ii)** (changed from (i) after A25's second critique): it changes the least recorded behaviour, keeps the serving form's report of a later failure while the pin stands, which (i) hides, and leaves `GatesPassed`'s meaning as A22.9 records it; what (ii) leaves, `ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated` beside a pass `passed[]` records, is today's reading, not a new one. Tags: four, at the `GatesPassed` table's "passed", "gate declared over a serving revision" and "pinned rollback" rows, and at "keeps describing the revision `status.eval` names".
    - **A25.6** (ii, #133, under A25.4 (b1) or (b2)). A stale Agent CRD holds the gate. *(A question until the second critique; its options that added a schema check to the eval controller, which reverses A6, and that derived the statement from a CRD read, which departs from "derived from status alone", are withdrawn.)*
      - Approved: step 11's schema list, "each of `suiteDigest`, … and `failed`", and A6's "This read-back is the eval controller's only guard against a stale CRD. It does not also check the schema".
      - Change: `passed` joins `evalRecordFields` (`gate.go:591`–`:595`) and step 11's schema list, so a CRD that does not keep it holds every gated candidate with `GatesPassed=False/EvalRecordNotKept`, as a CRD missing any other field of the record does today. The eval controller does not read `passed[]` back and checks no schema, so A6's rule stands; the Agent reconciler's schema check is the guard for this field.
      - **Residue, stated plainly**: the hold stops promotion, not evaluation. On a CRD that drops only `passed`, the eval controller still starts runs and records verdicts, and the pair a run start moves (under (b2)), or a verdict patch appends (under (b1)), is pruned and lost. From the first run start on such a CRD (under (b1), the first after a lost append), the active revision reads the statement, a revision that passed, beside the candidate's `EvalRecordNotKept`; it stands until a revision that passes is promoted, which is at once when the CRD is applied if the held candidate's run passed meanwhile. Measured by A25's third critique under (b2), with a cold schema cache, the state an operator upgraded without its CRDs starts in. With a warm cache, a CRD that goes stale within its 30 s TTL lets a candidate through until the cache next reads it, step 11's existing behaviour for every field (measured by the same critique).
      - `EvalRecordNotKept`'s message (`gate.go:239`–`:245`) says the CRD "prunes the eval record from every write … and no verdict could be kept", which is false when only `passed` is missing: it then says the CRD does not keep `status.eval.passed`, so a `Passed` record a later run replaces is lost, that the candidate holds until this release's CRDs are applied, and that the statement beside it may name a revision that passed. `reasons.yaml`'s entry (`:716`–`:724`) gains `passed` in its list and that case.
      - Test: `evalRecordMissingFrom`'s unit rows (`internal/controller/gate_test.go:338`–`:371`) gain `passed`, and their count of 8 missing fields becomes 9; an envtest row on a CRD that drops only `passed` asserts the hold and its message. Mutation: drop `passed` from the set.
      - Cost: an install that upgrades the operator without applying this release's `charts/assayd/crds/` holds every gated candidate, for a field that decides no promotion. If declined: the human's note says whether a stale CRD holds; not holding leaves the statement naming revisions that passed with nothing saying why. Tags: one, at step 11's schema list. Recommended: take.
    - **A25.7** (question, #133): **what is the statement's reason?** Options (a) and (b) apply only under A25.2 (b), and (c) to (e) only under A25.2 (a).
      - Under A25.2 (b): **(a)** `NoPassedVerdictRecorded`, which says what the record shows and claims neither a bypass nor a cause; **(b)** `ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated`, reused: one word fewer, but false of a pinned revision with a run of it recorded or its verdict cleared (`servingRecord`'s cases).
      - Under A25.2 (a), A24.10's options: **(c)** `PinnedRollback` for every form, false once the pin is gone; **(d)** `PinnedRollback` while a pin names the active revision and `NoPassedVerdictRecorded` otherwise, still false for a pin to a revision active before the gate, which `pinIneligible` admits unjudged (`pinrule.go:62`–`:64`); **(e)** `NoPassedVerdictRecorded` for every serving form and `PinnedRollback` for the pending form only, which changes the pinned serving form's reason that T28 and the pin rows assert.
      - **Recommended: (a)** under A25.2 (b), **(e)** under A25.2 (a): each is the only option whose reason is true in every state.
    - **A25.8** (ii, #133). It fires for a revision that was active before the gate was added, as decided ("the condition then also fires for a revision that served before the gate was added, beside `ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated`").
      - Change: none beyond A25.1; the record cannot tell such a revision from a pinned one. While the spec mints it, the statement stands beside `GatesPassed=False/ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated` (T63, `TestAGateAddedOverAServingRevisionSaysItWasNotEvaluated`); once the spec mints another, it alone names it. Measured by A25's first critique: A0, active before the gate, is named, and no longer once B passes and is promoted. No option makes it not fire, since that would narrow the decision. Tags: none; A25.1's carries it. Recommended: take.
    - **A25.9** (ii, #133). The message.
      - Change: "revision R (digest d) is the active revision, and no Passed verdict is recorded for it {where}. A gate evaluates each new revision before it takes traffic, not the revision already active. This condition is withdrawn when a revision with a recorded Passed verdict is promoted." {where} is "in status.eval" under A25.4 (a), and "in status.eval or status.eval.passed[]" under (b1) or (b2). One closing sentence follows. With `spec.release.targetRevisionDigest` unset: "The next revision this spec mints is held for its gate before it takes traffic." Set: "While spec.release.targetRevisionDigest is set, no revision this spec mints is promoted; GatesBypassed or Degraded says what the pin does." Under A25.2 (a), the pending form's message gains "Revision R, active now, has no Passed verdict recorded either."
      - Why: "is the active revision", not "is serving" (A25.2). It names no `status.eval` pair, so a run start, a claim and a case record leave it unchanged (A25.1). It says nothing of why — a gate added late, a pin, a revert — because the record does not say. The pin sentence is true in every pin state: a pin to the active revision, admitted and pending, admitted and promoted on the pass, refused as the current candidate, refused on `failed[]`, and unresolvable (A25's second critique, read from `pinIneligible` and the block after the switch); A25's first critique measured the first draft's "is the one promoted next" false beside a refused pin and a pin to the active revision. It avoids "policy surface", which `ActiveRevisionNotEvaluated`'s message uses.

Mutation: name `status.eval`'s pair, which T33 kills (critique 1 of A24). If declined: the human's note gives the wording. Tags: none. Recommended: take.

Under A25.3 (b), it waits on that amendment.
      - Why one PR: A25.1 to A25.9 are one change in the same functions. Cost: the fold is reviewed inside the code review, which includes a faithfulness check of the folded text (A24.14). If declined: the human's note says how. Tags: one, the *(A25: …)* tag. Recommended: take.
  - **What A25 does not change.** No decision among Q1 to Q13 moves; clause 1 and the Status line are unchanged. No revision is promoted or evaluated differently: the gate reads `status.eval`'s pair alone, and `passed[]` never lets a candidate skip its run. One hold is added: a CRD that does not keep `passed` holds gated candidates (A25.6). Nothing is withdrawn from traffic. No text of A20 to A24 is edited; A25.10's decision-day marker is the one addition, under A25.2 (b). A25 edits no text of designs 02, 03 or 07; design 02's §3.1 edit goes by A25.3's answer. §1.1's *(A22.33 decided (b): …)* tag and everything else of #135 are A26's. **No ADR amendment is proposed**, on A20's reasoning.

The third returned REVISE, 0 BLOCKER, 1 MAJOR, 7 MINOR, 5 NOTE, and A25 was revised in place: the matching rule put as A25.11, `passed[]` keyed by revision digest, A25.5 (ii)'s claim narrowed to while the pin stands, and its list and owed row completed.
